# PART VI: SUNDAY OF THE BLIND MAN, LEAVE-TAKING OF PASCHA, FEAST OF THE ASCENSION, AND SUNDAY OF THE HOLY FATHERS OF NICAEA

## SUNDAY OF THE BLIND MAN AND FEAST OF THE ASCENSION[^1147]

*[Woodcut Plate: Christ restoring sight to the man born blind (John 9:1–7), surrounded by His disciples.]*[^1148]

## SIXTH SUNDAY AFTER PASCHA, OF THE BLIND MAN

*On Saturday evening, at Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," we set 4 verses, and we sing 4 Resurrection stichera.*[^1149]

*Tone 5:*  
By Thy precious Cross, O Christ...  
Giving Resurrection unto the human race...  
A great wonder, the Creator of invisible things...  
Evening worship do we offer unto Thee...  

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 5:*  
O Lord, passing by on the way...  
*See all these at Great Vespers.*[^1150]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion: the present Dogmatikon.*  
*Tone 5:*  
Let us honor the God-befitting and all-pure Maiden, more honorable than the Cherubim: for the Creator of all, desiring to become man, took up His dwelling in her ineffably. O dread things and most glorious mysteries! Who would not marvel, hearing this: that God becomes man, and there is no alteration in Him; and He passes through the gates of virginity, and no diminution was left in Him; even as the Prophet saith: Man shall never pass through them, but only the Lord God of Israel, Who hath great mercy![^1151]

*At the Aposticha, Resurrection sticheron, Tone 5:*  
Thee, Christ the incarnate Savior, Who didst not part from heaven, we magnify with voices of song: for Thou didst accept the Cross and death for our race, as the Lord Lover of mankind; and having overthrown the gates of hades, Thou didst rise on the third day, saving our souls.[^1152]

*Then the appointed prosomoia stichera of the Most Holy Theotokos.*  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡмъ):*  
*Verse: I will remember thy name in every generation and generation.*[^1153]  
Rejoice, Seal of the Prophets, proclamation of the divinely-speaking Apostles: for truly didst thou bear God existing in truth, incarnate for us beyond mind and word; whereby having regained our ancient nobility and attained paradise's sweetness, we honor thee with songs as the cause of such brightness: and being enriched by thee as an acceptable intercessor, O All-holy One, we partake of the ever-existing life of thy Son, Who richly grants great mercy.[^1154]

*Verse: Hear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear.*[^1155]  
Rejoice, spiritual Heaven, in which God dwelt, having become incarnate; Jar of divine manna, Candlestick of the divine Sun, overshadowing Mountain, bridal chamber receptive of God, life-giving Table, golden Lampstand, light-bearing Paradise, unburnt Bush, Ark of holiness, heavenly Ladder, living Cloud, Rod from the root of Jesse blossomed forth, O Virgin: entreat Christ that great mercy may be given unto our souls![^1156]

*Verse: The rich among the people shall entreat thy face.*[^1157]  
Rejoice, O All-immaculate One, who didst ineffably conceive the Son of God and didst bear Him, Who truly received flesh like unto ours from thy blood, having a rational and volitional soul, and Who wrapped Himself without change in Adam, for the sake of His mercy and ineffable goodness; whence Christ is proclaimed unto us in two natures, showing forth in Himself the operation of both: Whom entreat to grant great mercy unto them that hymn thee![^1158]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 8:*  
O Christ God, spiritual Sun of Righteousness, Who by Thy most pure touch didst enlighten both eyes of him who from the womb was deprived of light, and didst illumine our spiritual eyes: show us forth as sons of the day, that with faith we may cry unto Thee: Glory to Thee, O Lover of mankind, for Thy great and ineffable tender compassion toward us![^1159]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 5:*  
Thou art the Temple and the Door, the Palace and the Throne of the King, O all-honored Virgin: through whom my Redeemer, Christ the Lord, appeared unto those sleeping in darkness, being the Sun of Righteousness, desiring to enlighten those whom He fashioned according to His own image with His hand: therefore, O All-hymned One, having acquired maternal boldness toward Him, unceasingly pray that our souls may be saved.[^1160]

*And the rest as usual.*

## AT GREAT VESPERS

*We set 10 verses, and we sing 3 Resurrection stichera, 4 Eastern stichera, and 2 idiomela of the Blind Man, repeating the first.*[^1161]

*Resurrection stichera, Tone 5:*  
By Thy precious Cross, O Christ, Thou hast put the devil to shame, and by Thy Resurrection Thou hast blunted the sting of sin, and saved us from the gates of death: we glorify Thee, O Only-begotten One.[^1162]

Giving Resurrection unto the human race, He was led as a sheep to the slaughter; the princes of hades were terrified of Him, and the gates of grief were lifted up: for Christ the King of glory entered, saying unto those in bonds: Come forth! and unto those in darkness: Show yourselves![^1163]

A great wonder! The Creator of invisible things, having suffered in the flesh for love of mankind, arose immortal: come, families of the nations, let us worship Him; for having been delivered from deceit by His tender compassion, we have learned to sing of one God in three Hypostases.[^1164]

*Other Eastern stichera, the same Tone:*  
Evening worship do we offer unto Thee, the Unwaning Light, Who at the end of the ages didst shine forth in the flesh unto the world as in a mirror, and didst descend even unto hades, and didst dispel the darkness there, and didst show the light of the Resurrection unto the nations: O Lord and Giver of light, glory to Thee![^1165]

We glorify Christ, the Author of our salvation: for when He arose from the dead, the world was saved from deceit; the angelic choir rejoices, the deceit of demons flees, fallen Adam hath arisen, the devil is abolished.[^1166]

Those of the guard were instructed by the lawless ones: Conceal the Rising of Christ, and take pieces of silver, and say: While we were asleep, the dead man was stolen from the tomb. Who hath seen, who hath heard that a dead man was ever stolen, especially one anointed and naked, leaving his burial garments in the tomb? Be not deceived, O Jews: learn from the sayings of the Prophets, and understand that He is truly the Redeemer of the world and the Almighty![^1167]

O Lord Who didst lead hades captive and trample down death, our Savior Who didst enlighten the world by Thy precious Cross, have mercy on us.[^1168]

*Other stichera, idiomela of the Blind Man.*  
*Tone 2:*  
He that was born blind said unto his own thoughts: Was I born without eyes for the sins of my parents? Was I born as a reproof for the unbelief of the nations? I have no way to ask when it is night, when it is day; my feet cannot endure stumbling upon stones: for I have not seen the sun shining, nor Him in image Who fashioned me; but I pray Thee, O Christ God: Look upon me and have mercy on me!  
*Twice.*[^1169]

Passing by from the sanctuary, Jesus found a man blind from birth, and having compassion, He placed clay upon his eyes, and said unto him: Go, wash in Siloam; and having washed, he received his sight, sending up glory unto God. And his neighbors said unto him: Who hath opened thine eyes, which no one among them that see was able to heal? And he, crying aloud, said: A man called Jesus, He said unto me: Wash in Siloam, and I received my sight. He is truly the One of Whom Moses spoke in the Law, Christ the Messiah: He is the Savior of our souls![^1170]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 5:*  
O Lord, passing by on the way, Thou didst find a man blind from birth: and the disciples being astonished, questioned Thee, saying: Teacher, who did sin, this man or his parents, that he was born blind? And Thou, my Savior, didst cry aloud unto them: Neither hath this man sinned, nor his parents, but that the works of God should be made manifest in him; I must work the works of Him that sent Me, which no man can work. And having said these things, spitting on the ground and making clay, Thou didst anoint his eyes, saying unto him: Go, wash in the pool of Siloam. And he having washed was made whole, and cried unto Thee: I believe, Lord, and he worshipped Thee: therefore do we also cry: Have mercy on us![^1171]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
In the Red Sea of old an image of the Unwedded Bride was inscribed: there Moses was the parter of the water, and here Gabriel the minister of the wonder; then Israel walked dry-shod through the deep, and now the Virgin hath borne Christ without seed; the sea remained impassable after the passage of Israel, and the Immaculate One remained incorrupt after the birth of Emmanuel: O God Who art and Who wast before, Who hast appeared as man, have mercy on us![^1172]

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day.*  
*At the Litya, sticheron of the temple.*[^1173]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 4:*  
Considering his whole life to be night, the blind man cried unto Thee: O Lord, open mine eyes, O Son of David our Savior, that with all men I also may hymn Thy power![^1174]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion, the same Tone:*  
Look upon the supplications of Thy servants, O All-immaculate One, assuaging the fierce uprisings against us, releasing us from every affliction: for thee alone do we possess as a firm and sure foundation, and we have acquired thy patronage, that we who call upon thee may not be put to shame, O Lady. Hasten to the supplication of them that cry unto thee in faith: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, help of all, joy and protection, and salvation of our souls![^1175]

*At the Aposticha, Resurrection sticheron, Tone 5:*  
Thee, Christ the incarnate Savior, Who didst not part from heaven, we magnify with voices of song: for Thou didst accept the Cross and death for our race, as the Lord Lover of mankind; and having overthrown the gates of hades, Thou didst rise on the third day, saving our souls.[^1176]

*Then the Paschal Stichera, with their refrains: seek in the Sunday of Pascha at Matins (see fol. 15).*[^1177]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 8:*  
O Christ God, spiritual Sun of Righteousness, Who by Thy most pure touch didst enlighten both eyes of him who from the womb was deprived of light, and didst illumine our spiritual eyes: show us forth as sons of the day,[^1178] that with faith we may cry unto Thee: Glory to Thee, O Lover of mankind, for Thy great and ineffable tender compassion toward us![^1179]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*The day of Resurrection...*  
*And the rest, as prescribed in the past weeks.*[^1180]

*Then the Dismissal Troparion, Tone 5:*  
Let us, the faithful, praise and worship the Word, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, born of the Virgin for our salvation: for He was pleased of His own will to ascend the Cross in the flesh, and to endure death, and to raise up the dead by His glorious Resurrection.[^1181]

*Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, impassable Gate of the Lord; rejoice, Rampart and Shelter of those who flee unto thee; rejoice, tempest-free Haven, and Unwedded One, who didst bear in the flesh thy Creator and God: cease not to pray for those that hymn and worship thy Birth-giving.[^1182]

*On the Sunday of the Blind Man, at the Midnight Office:*  
*We sing the Trinity Canon of the Tone from the Octoechos.*  
*Then: It is truly meet... and the rest as usual, and dismissal.*[^1183]

## ON THE SIXTH SUNDAY AFTER PASCHA, OF THE BLIND MAN, AT MATINS

*After the First Stichology, Resurrection Sessional Hymns.*  
*Tone 5:*  
Let us praise the Cross of the Lord, let us honor His holy Burial with songs, and let us exceedingly glorify His Resurrection: for as God He raised the dead together with Himself from the tombs, having led captive the dominion of death and the strength of the devil: and light shone forth upon those in hades.[^1184]

*Verse: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up.*[^1185]  
O Lord, Thou didst call Thyself dead, Who didst put death to death; Thou wast laid in a tomb, Who didst empty the tombs: above, soldiers guarded the tomb; below, Thou didst raise up the dead from the ages: O Almighty and Incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee![^1186]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, holy Mountain and God-trodden; rejoice, living and unburnt Bush; rejoice, only Bridge to God for the world, leading mortals unto eternal life; rejoice, incorruptible Maiden, who without knowing man didst bear the Salvation of our souls.[^1187]

*After the Second Stichology, Sessional Hymns:*  
*Tone 5:*  
O Lord, the lawless ones nailed Thee in the midst of the condemned, and pierced Thy side with a spear, O Merciful One! And Thou didst accept burial, having shattered the gates of hades, and didst rise on the third day: the women ran to see Thee, and announced the Rising unto the Apostles: O exceedingly exalted Savior, Whom the Angels hymn, O blessed Lord, glory to Thee![^1188]

*Verse: I will confess Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart, I will tell of all Thy wonders.*[^1189]  
Thy marvelous mystery, O my Savior, became salvation unto the world: for having risen from the tomb in a manner befitting God, Thou didst raise up the corrupted together with Thyself as God, O Life of all, Lord, glory to Thee![^1190]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
O unwedded Mother, Pure Parent of God, who didst gladden Eve's sorrow with joy: we, the faithful, hymn and worship thee, for thou hast raised us up from the ancient curse; and now pray unceasingly, O all-hymned and Most Holy One, that we may be saved.[^1191]

*And the reading in the commentary on the Gospel from John, Homilies 56, 57, and 58.*[^1192]

*After the Amomos, the troparion: The angelic host... and the rest as usual.*[^1193]

*And straightway the Hypakoe, Tone 5:*  
The Myrrh-bearing women, perplexed in mind by the angelic vision, yet enlightened in soul by the divine Rising, brought the glad tidings unto the Apostles: Proclaim the Resurrection among the nations, the Lord working together with wonders, granting great mercy unto us.[^1194]

*And straightway the Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], Tone 5.*  
*Antiphon 1:*  
When I am afflicted, like David I sing unto Thee, O my Savior: Deliver my soul from a deceitful tongue.[^1195]  
Blessed is the life of the dwellers in the wilderness, who are borne on wings by divine love.  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
By the Holy Spirit all visible and invisible things are held together: for being sovereign, He is truly One of the Trinity.[^1196]  
*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*  

*Antiphon 2:*  
Let us lift ourselves up unto the mountains, O my soul; go thither, whence help comes.[^1197]  
May Thy right hand, O Christ, touching me also, preserve me from all deceit.  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
Theologizing unto the Holy Spirit, let us say: Thou art God, Life, Love, Light, Mind: Thou art Goodness, Thou reignest unto the ages.[^1198]  
*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*  

*Antiphon 3:*  
Concerning them that said unto me: We will go into the courts of the Lord; being filled with great joy, I send up prayers.[^1199]

In the house of David dread things are accomplished: for there fire burns up every shameful mind.[^1200]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
Unto the Holy Spirit belongs life-originating dignity, from Whom every living creature is animated, even as in the Father, and together with the Word.[^1201]  
*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*  

*Prokeimenon, Tone 5:*  
Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up, for Thou reignest unto the ages.[^1202]  
*Verse: I will confess Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart.*  

*Gospel from John, the Eighth Matins Gospel: Mary stood at the tomb...*[^1203]  
*Then: Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ... thrice, and Psalm 50.*[^1204]

*Canon of Pascha, with the Heirmoi and with the Theotokia, to 8; and of the Blind Man to 6.*[^1205]

*Canon of the Blind Man, Tone 5.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
The land whereon the sun never shone nor saw at any time, the deep which the bare breadth of heaven never saw, Israel passed dry-shod, O Lord; and Thou didst bring him into the mountain of Thy holiness, as he praised and sang a song of victory.[^1206]

*Troparion:*  
Having accepted voluntary crucifixion in the flesh, O Savior, Thou didst pour forth blessing and life unto the world, O Master, only All-blessed One and Creator of all: therefore do we bless Thee, singing and glorifying, chanting and singing a song of victory.[^1207]

*Troparion:*  
In the lowermost pit, when Thou wast become dead, O Christ, noble Joseph placed Thee, and rolled a stone unto the doors of the tomb, O Long-suffering One: but Thou didst rise, O Glorious One, and didst raise the world together with Thyself, as it sings and chants a song of victory.[^1208]

*Troparion:*  
Why bring ye myrrh with tears? the Angel who appeared cried unto the honored women: Christ is risen! Run, say unto the God-beholding disciples who mourn and weep, that they may leap and keep festival radiantly![^1209]

*Troparion:*  
Working marvelous wonders, the Redeemer healed also him that was blind from birth, having anointed him with clay, and said: Go and wash in Siloam, that thou mayest know Me as God walking upon earth, bearing flesh for the mercy of compassions.[^1210]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadikon:*  
Honoring the one tri-hypostatic Essence, let us, the faithful, glorify the Father, and the Son, and the right Spirit, the Creator and Lord and Redeemer of all, the one uncreated God, crying with the bodiless hosts: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O King![^1211]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
The Lord took up His dwelling in thy unwedded womb, O Pure One, for the mercy of compassions, desiring to save man corrupted by the wiles of the enemy: Him therefore entreat to save this city from all captivity and invasion of enemies.[^1212]

*Katavasia:*  
Unto God the Savior, Who guided the people through the sea with dry feet, and drowned Pharaoh with all his army: unto Him alone let us sing, for He hath been glorified.[^1213]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
Establish my heart, O Lord, tossed by the waves of this life, guiding it unto a calm haven, as God.[^1214]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst establish wavering hearts, O Long-suffering One, having shaken all the earth by Thy precious Crucifixion, which Thou didst endure in the flesh.[^1215]

*Troparion:*  
Noble Joseph placed Thee in a new tomb, O Compassionate One; but Thou didst rise from the dead on the third day, having made us new.[^1216]

*Troparion:*  
Why seek ye the Lord as dead? He is risen, as He said! the Angel cried unto the women, shining with divine countenance.[^1217]

*Troparion:*  
The man who was of old blind from birth, when he drew near unto Thee, Thou didst heal, O All-compassionate One, as he glorified Thy dispensation and Thy wonders.[^1218]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadikon:*  
We worship God the Father, Who is before all beginning, and the Son, and the divine Spirit, the thrice-uncreated Essence, tri-hypostatic, the one God of all.[^1219]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
From a virginal womb thou didst bring forth God incarnate: Him therefore entreat, O all-holy Mistress, to have compassion on us.[^1220]

*Katavasia:*  
By the power of Thy Cross, O Christ, establish my mind, that I may sing and glorify Thy saving Ascension.[^1221]

*Kontakion and Ikos of Pascha (see fol. 402).*[^1222]  
*Then the Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости):*  
Passing by, the Master and Creator of the ages found sitting upon the way a blind man, who wept and said: In my life I never saw the sun shining, nor the light of the moon giving radiance; therefore do I cry unto Thee: O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin and dost enlighten all things, enlighten me, as Thou art compassionate, that falling down before Thee, I may cry unto Thee, O Master, Christ God: Grant remission of transgressions unto us, for the multitude of Thy mercy, O Lover of mankind![^1223]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*[^1224]  
*And a reading.*[^1225]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
I heard Thy report, O Lord, and was afraid; I understood Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, O only Lover of mankind.[^1226]

*Troparion:*  
When I was put to death by the tree, having been sacrificed, O my Savior, Who art Life, Thou didst make me alive again, for Thy great mercy: therefore, O Word, I glorify Thee.[^1227]

*Troparion:*  
Abiding marvelously with the disciples, O Lord, Thou didst say unto them: Go ye, preach everywhere My Resurrection.[^1228]

*Troparion:*  
Assuring those that love Thee of Thy Rising from the tomb, O Christ, Thou didst abide with them for many days, creating joy for them.[^1229]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst refashion the man blind from the womb, O Lord, saying: Go, wash and receive sight, glorifying My Godhead.[^1230]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadikon:*  
O Trinity of equal honor, without beginning, indivisible in essence, divided in Hypostases, save all who faithfully glorify Thee with fear![^1231]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Thy supernatural childbearing we glorify, running faithfully unto thee, O all-undefiled one, as the Mother of the God of all.[^1232]

*Katavasia:*  
I heard the report of the power of the Cross, that paradise was opened thereby, and I cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^1233]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Forestalling me, have compassion upon my wretched soul that whispers with the darkness of passions, and shine forth, O Noetic Sun, day-giving rays within me, to divide the night from the light.[^1234]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst ascend upon the Tree, O Compassionate One, and didst lift up all men together with Thyself; and Thou didst slay the contending serpent, and didst make alive the creation of Thy hands, as the only God of all.[^1235]

*Troparion:*  
Thou wast placed in the tomb of Thine own will, O immortal King, and didst empty all the realms of hades, raising the dead by Thy Resurrection.[^1236]

*Troparion:*  
As Thou wast working great wonders on earth, O Word, lawless people slew Thee; but Thou Thyself, O Lord, being alone almighty, as Thou didst foretell, didst rise from the dead, O Christ.[^1237]

*Troparion:*  
Having opened the eyes of him who had not seen sensible light, Thou didst enlighten the pupils of his soul, and didst cause him to glorify Thee, having recognized Thee as the Creator Who didst appear as man for compassion's sake.[^1238]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadikon:*  
The Trinity in Unity, and unto the Trinity Unity, let us all, the faithful, praise: the Father, and the Son, and the right Spirit, the one God, truly the Creator of all.[^1239]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
How didst thou give birth, O woman full of the grace of God, pure Virgin-Mother, having known no trial of man? How dost thou nourish Him Who nourishes all creation? As He alone knows Himself, the Creator and God of all.[^1240]

*Katavasia:*  
Rising early at dawn, we cry unto Thee, O Lord, save us: for Thou art our God; besides Thee we know none other.[^1241]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Even as Thou didst deliver the Prophet from the beast, O Lord, so also lead me up from the depth of uncontainable passions, I pray Thee, that I may look again unto Thy holy temple.[^1242]

*Troparion:*  
Thou Who wast crucified with robbers, O Master, hast delivered from the wicked robbers of soul-destroying passions, O Lord Lover of mankind, all who sing in harmony of Thy Crucifixion and Rising.[^1243]

*Troparion:*  
They placed Thee breathless, as one dead, in the tomb, O Christ, Who breathest life into all the dead; yet Thou didst rise, O Lord, emptying all the tombs, O Word, by Thy divine power.[^1244]

*Troparion:*  
After Thy Rising, O Christ, Thou saidst unto Thy friends: Tarry ye now in Jerusalem, until ye be endued with invincible power from on high and sure help.[^1245]

*Troparion:*  
Having made clay, Thou didst anoint the eyes of him that was blind from birth, and didst grant him to receive sight, as he sang of Thine ineffable power, O Word, whereby Thou hast saved the world.[^1246]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadikon:*  
O tri-hypostatic Unity, Father unbegotten, Son begotten, and Spirit proceeding, O thrice-holy Lord, one Essence and Power, save all Thy people![^1247]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Who shall declare thy greatness, O Pure One? For in supernatural manner thou didst bear God in the flesh, Who through thee delivers the world from every sin, O all-undefiled Virgin.[^1248]

*Katavasia:*  
The deep encompassed me, the sea-monster was a tomb to me; but I cried unto Thee, Lover of mankind, and Thy right hand saved me, O Lord.[^1249]

*Kontakion.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*  
Blinded in the eyes of my soul, I come unto Thee, O Christ, as the man blind from birth, crying unto Thee in repentance: Thou art the most radiant Light of those in darkness![^1250]

*Ikos:*  
Grant me a stream of ineffable wisdom and knowledge from on high, O Christ, Thou Light of those in darkness and Guide of the errant, that I, wretched as I am, may be able to recount Thy wonders which the divine book of the Gospel of peace hath taught: that is, the miracle of the blind man; for he that was blind from birth receives both sensible and spiritual eyes, crying out in faith: Thou art the most radiant Light of those in darkness![^1251]

*Synaxarion of the Menaion. Then the present Synaxarion:*[^1252]

*Verses:*  
*Giver of light, being Light of Light,*  
*Thou dost restore the man blind from birth, O Word.*[^1253]

On this same day, the Sixth Sunday after Pascha, we celebrate the miracle of our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ wrought upon the man blind from birth, which indeed was also performed through water, even as the miracle of the Samaritan woman and that of the paralytic.[^1254] It came to pass thus: while Christ was conversing with the Jews, and showing Himself equal to the Father, and saying: "Before Abraham was, I am," they cast stones at Him (Jn 8:58–59). But He, departing thence, found a blind man stumbling—he had been born thus, having only the appearance of eyes and sunken sockets.[^1255] The Savior, finding him thus, was asked by His disciples, who had heard what was said concerning the paralytic: "Behold, thou art made whole; sin no more" (Jn 5:14), and that the sin of parents is visited upon children: "Master, who did sin, this man, or his parents, that he was born blind?" (Jn 9:2).[^1256] Otherwise also, holding a certain Epicurean notion that souls pre-exist, and having sinned immaterially, are joined into bodies—all of which Christ refuted, saying: "Neither hath this man sinned, nor his parents: but that the works of God should be made manifest," that is, Mine (Jn 9:3). Nor is the word spoken concerning the Father, and the "that" expresses the outcome and not the cause.[^1257] And having said this, Christ spat on the ground, made clay, and anointed the place of his eyes, and commanded him to go to the Pool of Siloam and wash, to show that He Himself in the beginning took dust from the earth and created man (Gen 2:7). And since the eye is the most divine member of all in the body, He creates that which did not exist, showing that He is the One Who gives motion also to the power of the soul. Nor did He anoint with water, but with spittle, that it might be known that all grace was from the spittle of His mouth, and that He intended to send him to Siloam. And He commanded him to wash, lest anyone should attribute the healing to the earth and clay of that place.[^1258] And He sends him to Siloam, that he might have many witnesses of the healing, for many met him as he walked with his eyes anointed with clay. And some say that when he washed, the clay from the spittle was not washed away, but that that clay, by the addition of that which was better, was transformed into the structure of eyes. Now "Siloam" is interpreted "Sent" (Jn 9:7); for it was a stronghold of the city of Jerusalem. In the days of Hezekiah, when enemies besieged the city and cut off Siloam, so that water was dug everywhere, they dug out the inner wells and streams within, for the reception of water. And if anyone was sent thither by the command of the Prophet Isaiah, the water again gushed forth and was drawn; but if any of the enemies wished to draw, the springs of water were withheld, and so it happened in their case. That Christ might therefore show that He also is of God, He sends the blind man thither, and light immediately follows. Some indeed thought that Siloam was interpreted "Sent" also on account of the present blind man sent by Christ.[^1259] The blind man then returned, having washed, by an ineffable power, the mystery being unknown even to him who had suffered it. And his neighbors and those who otherwise knew him, seeing him seeing well, were in doubt. But he confessed that he had formerly been blind, and being questioned concerning the cause of his receiving sight, proclaimed Christ, the healer of the affliction.[^1260] When the Pharisees heard of the marvelous sign, the Savior was again blasphemed, as not keeping the Sabbath; for on the Sabbath, as was His custom, that which was done unto the blind man was also performed. A division therefore arose among them: some saying that Jesus was of God on account of the miracles that were done, but others that He was not of God, because He kept not the Sabbath. Those who held a good opinion of Him questioned the blind man: "What sayest thou of Him?" And he proclaimed Him to be a Prophet, which was more honorable than their view. But they again did not believe that, when he was blind, Christ granted him healing; therefore they called his parents also, not believing his neighbors, wishing rather to conceal the matter; but they made it all the more evident, since the parents confessed everything, though fearing to be put out of the synagogue, referring the deed to his own age.[^1261] But again they say unto the blind man: "Give God the praise," for thou wast healed by Him and not by Christ; for He is a sinner, because He breaks the Sabbath. But he, wishing to show by deeds that He is God, said: "I know not; one thing I know, that, whereas I was blind, now I see." Again therefore they said unto him: "How opened He thine eyes?" But he, being vexed, did not speak in detail, but affirmed that if He were not of God, He could do no such sign. And at first he was reviled by them, because he had confessed, and was His disciple, and because he said: "Since the world began was it not heard that any man opened the eyes of one that was born blind" (Jn 9:32); for others also enlightened the blind, but one born blind none at all. Having mocked him therefore, they cast him far out of the synagogue.[^1262] After this Jesus found him, and said unto him: "Dost thou believe on the Son of God?" And he, having learned Who He was that was speaking with him and was seen by him—for he knew Him not before, being blind—worshiped Him, and became His disciple, proclaiming Thanksgiving.[^1263] This was said also allegorically: For the blind man is the people from among the Gentiles, whom Christ, passing by the calumny of living upon earth and not in heaven, found—even though He came again for the sake of the Hebrew people, yet passing by, He came also unto the Gentiles. And having spat upon the ground and made clay, He anointed him: which calumny He taught before, because He came down upon earth as a drop, and was incarnate of the Holy Virgin. Then He also delivered divine Baptism, which is Siloam. Thereafter, the Christian people from the Gentiles dare all things for Christ, and are persecuted, and bear witness, and afterward are proclaimed and glorified by Him.[^1264]  
By Thine unsearchable mercy, O Christ our God, Giver of light, have mercy on us and save us. Amen.[^1265]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The prayer of the children was an extinguisher of the fire; the dewy furnace, a herald of a miracle, neither burning nor consuming the singers of the God of our Fathers.[^1266]

*Troparion:*  
When Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree, O Savior, the sun was extinguished, the earth was shaken, creation trembled, and the dead arose from the tombs.[^1267]

*Troparion:*  
When Thou didst arise from the dead, O King, the souls that were sleeping there arose together with Thee, glorifying Thy power, whereby the bonds of death were dissolved.[^1268]

*Troparion:*  
Early in the morning the choir of women came to anoint Thee with myrrh; but having learned that Thou wast risen, O Lord, they rejoiced with the sacred disciples: through them grant us cleansing from evils.[^1269]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst anoint the eyes of the blind man with clay, and didst command him to go to Siloam; and having washed, immediately he saw, chanting unto Thee, O Christ, the King of all.[^1270]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadikon:*  
The Father unoriginate, the Son co-unoriginate, and the most holy Spirit we praise, singing: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God, King of all![^1271]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
After childbearing thou didst appear a Virgin, O Pure One, for thou didst bear God, Who renewed nature by His own power, O all-undefiled one: Him entreat unceasingly, that we all may be saved.[^1272]

*Katavasia:*  
Thou Who didst save the children that praised Thee in the fiery furnace: Blessed is the God of our Fathers![^1273]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
The host of Angels, the assembly of men, the King and Creator of all: ye priests, praise; ye Levites, bless; ye peoples, supremely exalt unto all ages![^1274]

*Troparion:*  
The angelic ranks, seeing Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, King of all, and all creation altering in fear, were dismayed, singing of Thy love for mankind.[^1275]

*Troparion:*  
Hades below, seeing Thee, groaned, and swiftly surrendered the dead kept there from the ages, O Christ, who sang of Thy love for mankind.[^1276]

*Troparion:*  
Working extraordinary wonders, O Christ, Thou wast of Thine own will lifted up upon the Cross, and didst join Thyself unto the dead, having slain hades, and by Thy fortitude Thou didst loose all the prisoners.[^1277]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst refashion the blind man who approached Thee, O Christ, commanding him to go unto the pool of Siloam, to wash and receive sight, and to proclaim Thee as God Who didst appear in the flesh for the salvation of the world.[^1278]

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*[^1279]  
*Triadikon:*  
O indivisible Trinity, unconfused Unity, God of all things and Creator of all, save from all temptations those who praise and faithfully worship Thy dominion![^1280]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
O all-undefiled Virgin, full of the grace of God, pray ever unto thy Son, that He put me not to shame in the day of judgment, but number me among the chosen sheep.[^1281]

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, singing and supremely exalting Him unto all ages.*[^1282]  
*Katavasia:*  
The Son and God, begotten of the Father before the ages, and in latter times incarnate of the Virgin Mother: ye priests, praise; ye peoples, supremely exalt unto all ages![^1283]

*We do not sing the More Honorable.*[^1284]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
For the Mighty One hath done great things unto thee, preserving thee a pure Virgin after childbearing, as having borne without seed thine own Creator: therefore, O Theotokos, we magnify thee.[^1285]

*Troparion:*  
Thou wast nailed to the tree of the Cross, O Christ God, and didst vanquish all the opposing powers of the enemy, and didst destroy the primal curse, O Savior: therefore do we worthily magnify Thee.[^1286]

*Troparion:*  
When hades below saw Thee with Thy soul, O Word, it groaned, and in fear released all the dead, who recognized the dominion of Thy power: therefore do we worthily magnify Thee.[^1287]

*Troparion:*  
Beholding Him working signs and dread wonders, the assembly of the Hebrews through envy slew Him Who despoiled hades by His Resurrection, and Who, as Mighty, raises all.[^1288]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst rise from the dead, as Thou hadst said, O Giver of life, and didst appear unto the holy disciples after Thy Rising, having wrought signs and refashioned the blind man: with them we magnify Thee unto the ages.[^1289]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadikon:*  
The Light of the Father I revere, the Light of the Son I glorify, the Light of the right Spirit I hymn: one Light, indivisible, understood in three Persons, God and King of all creation.[^1290]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Having appeared more spacious than the heavens, O pure Virgin, thou didst contain in the flesh the uncontainable God, and didst bear the Redemption of all who hymn thee with unwavering faith.[^1291]

*Katavasia:*  
Thee, the Mother of God beyond mind and word, who didst ineffably give birth in time unto the Timeless One, we, the faithful, magnify with one accord.[^1292]

*Exapostilarion of Pascha, once:*  
Having fallen asleep in the flesh as a mortal, O King and Lord, on the third day Thou didst rise again, raising Adam from corruption and abolishing death: O Pascha of incorruption, salvation of the world![^1293]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Of the Blind Man. Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Подо́бенъ):*  
Enlighten my spiritual eyes, O Lord, blinded by darksome sin, implanting humility, O Compassionate One, and wash them with tears of repentance.[^1294]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жєны̀ оу҆слы́шите):*  
Passing by, our Savior found a man blind, lacking eyes; and having spat upon the ground and made clay, He anointed him, and sent him to Siloam to go and wash; and having washed, he came seeing Thy light, O my Christ.[^1295]

*At the Praises, we set 8 verses. And we sing the Resurrection stichera.*[^1296]  
*Tone 5:*  
O Lord, while the tomb was sealed by the lawless, Thou camest forth from the sepulcher, even as Thou wast born of the Theotokos: Thine incorporeal Angels understood not how Thou didst take flesh; the soldiers guarding Thee perceived not...[^1297]

...when Thou didst rise: for both things were sealed unto the inquirers, but wonders were made manifest unto those who worship the mystery in faith; grant joy and great mercy unto us who sing thereof.[^1298]

O Lord, Who didst shatter the eternal bars and burst asunder the bonds, Thou didst rise from the tomb, leaving behind Thy grave-clothes, for a testimony of the true three-day Burial of Thine; and Thou didst go before into Galilee, Thou Who wast guarded in the cave: great is Thy mercy, O ungraspable Savior, have mercy on us and save us.[^1299]

O Lord, the women ran unto the tomb to see Thee, the Christ Who didst suffer for our sake; and having come, they found an Angel sitting upon the stone, seized with fear, and he cried unto them, saying: The Lord is risen! Say unto the disciples that He is risen from the dead, saving our souls.[^1300]

O Lord, even as Thou camest forth from the sealed tomb, so didst Thou enter, while the doors were shut, unto Thy disciples, showing them the bodily sufferings which Thou didst endure, O long-suffering Savior: for as of the seed of David, Thou didst undergo wounds; but as the Son of God, Thou didst set the world free: great is Thy mercy, O ungraspable Savior, have mercy on us and save us.[^1301]

*Other Stichera, of Anatolius.*  
O Lord, King of the ages and Creator of all, Who for our sake didst accept Crucifixion and Burial in the flesh, that Thou mightest deliver us all from hades: Thou art our God; besides Thee we know none other.[^1302]

O Lord, who shall declare Thy radiant wonders? Or who shall recount Thy dread mysteries? For having become man for our sake as Thou Thyself didst will, Thou didst manifest the dominion of Thy power: for by Thy Cross Thou didst open paradise unto the robber, and by Thy Burial Thou didst shatter the bars of hades; and by Thy Resurrection Thou didst enrich all things, O Compassionate One: Lord, glory to Thee![^1303]

The Myrrh-bearing women, having reached Thy tomb very early, sought to anoint Thee with myrrh, the immortal Word and God; and having been informed by the Angel's words, they returned with joy, to announce openly unto the Apostles: That Thou, the Life of all, art risen, and hast granted cleansing and great mercy unto the world.[^1304]

*Verse: Look upon me, and have mercy on me.*[^1305]  
*Idiomelon of the Blind Man.*  
*Tone 8:*  
Having become incarnate for the bowels of mercy, O Christ God, by the compassion of Thine ineffable mercies Thou didst vouchsafe divine radiance unto him who was deprived of light from the womb, having touched his pupils with dust by Thy creative fingers: do Thou Thyself even now, O Giver of light, illuminate also the spiritual senses of our soul, as the only begrudgeless Giver.[^1306]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 8:*  
Who shall utter Thy mighty acts, O Christ? Or who shall number the multitude of Thy wonders? For as Thou wast seen two-fold upon earth in Thy goodness, so didst Thou bestow two-fold healings upon the sick: for not only didst Thou open the bodily eyes of the man blind from the womb, but the spiritual ones also; therefore did he confess Thee as the hidden God, Who grantest great mercy unto all.[^1307]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos...*[^1308]  
*The Great Doxology. And the Dismissal.*[^1309]

*Gospel Sticheron, Tone 8:*  
Mary's tears were not shed warmly in vain; for behold, she was accounted worthy both of the instructing Angels and of the sight of Jesus Himself; yet she still minded earthly things, as a weak woman: therefore also she was sent away from touching Christ; but yet she was dispatched as a herald unto Thy disciples, bearing the good tidings unto them, announcing Thine ascent unto the Father's inheritance: with her, vouchsafe us also Thine appearance, O Master and Lord.[^1310]

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of Tone 5:*[^1311]  
The robber on the cross, having believed Thee to be God, O Christ, confessed Thee purely from the heart, crying: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom![^1312]

Him Who blossomed forth life for our race upon the tree of the Cross, and dried up the curse that came from the tree, let us sing in harmony as Savior and Creator.[^1313]

When Thou wast crucified, O Christ, between two condemned robbers, the one indeed, blaspheming Thee, was justly condemned; but the other, confessing Thee, made his abode in paradise.[^1314]

Having come unto the company of the Apostles, the venerable women cried aloud: Christ is risen! Let us worship Him as Master and Creator.[^1315]

*And from the Canon of the Blind Man, Ode 6.*[^1316]

*After the Entrance, the Resurrection Troparion. Glory: Kontakion: Blinded in the eyes of my soul... Both now: Of Pascha: Though Thou didst go down into the tomb...*[^1317]

*Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*  
Pray ye, and render thanks unto the Lord our God.[^1318]  
*Verse: In Judah is God known; His Name is great in Israel.*[^1319]

*Epistle of the Acts, Pericope 38: It came to pass, as the Apostles went to prayer...*[^1320]

*Alleluia, Tone 8:*  
*Look upon me, and have mercy on me.*[^1321]  
*Verse: Direct my steps according to Thy word.*[^1322]

*Gospel from John, Pericope 34: As Jesus passed by, He saw a man blind from birth...*[^1323]

*Communion Hymn: Receive ye the Body of Christ... Another: Praise the Lord from the heavens...*[^1324]

*The service of the saint who may occur on this Sunday we sing at Compline.*[^1325]

## ON THE SUNDAY OF THE BLIND MAN, AT VESPERS[^1326]

*At Lord, I have cried, we set 6 verses: 3 of the feast, and 3 of the Menaion.*[^1327]

*Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*  
Being Light unto all human nature, and the Eye unto the body of this world, O God and Word, and recognized as the Maker and Fashioner of eyes: now also by the mingling of clay and spittle, Thou dost ineffably refashion the blind man, Thy fingers fashioning both sight and vision; which having attained, he who was previously known among those born as one entirely blind to the sun, sees Thee, the sweet Sun, beholding the form of Him Who created him for our sake in Thine ineffable compassion.[^1328]

Deprived of limbs, and enriched by the configuration of human bodily instrumental parts, he that was previously blind from his mother’s womb considered that he was not a complete creation on account of the lack of eyes, whence by his feet came many stumblings of the body, striking against stones: yet having acquired through Thee this wealth also, so that he sees the light of the world, he announcs Thee, the Master of lights, as God and Lord over creations, and the Maker of all the universe.[^1329]

With lips, and soul, and mind, he that was formerly blind confessed Him Who creatively gave pupils of the eyes from dust and spittle and ocular enlightenment, as the all-cosmic Overseer and Master of creatures, Who became man solely for compassion on His creature; and he proclaimed Him as almighty God, Whom the scribes, being totally unable to behold through envy and because of his converse with them, acting ever more blindly, judged worthy of expulsion from the synagogue, Him Who once gave the blind man pupils, both spiritual and bodily.[^1330]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 2:*  
He that was born blind said in his reasoning: Did I then be born without eyes on account of parental sins? Was I born for a rebuke on account of the unbelief of the Gentiles? I am at a loss to ask when it is night, when it is day; my feet cannot endure the stumbling against stones; for I saw not the sun shining, nor Him Who created me in His image: but I pray Thee, O Christ God: look upon me, and have mercy on me![^1331]

*At the Aposticha, the Resurrection sticheron.*  
*Tone 5:*  
By Thy precious Cross, O Christ, Thou hast put the devil to shame; and by Thy Resurrection Thou hast blunted the sting of sin, and hast saved us from the gates of death: we glorify Thee, O Only-begotten One.[^1332]

*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in heaven.*[^1333]  
*Penitential:*  
O Lord, sinning I do not cease, and vouchsafed Thy love for mankind, I do not understand: conquer mine obscurantism, O only Good One, and have mercy on me.[^1334]

*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us.*[^1335]  
*Martyrikon:*  
Disregarding all earthly things and bravely venturing unto torments, ye failed not of your blessed hopes, but became heirs of the Heavenly Kingdom, O all-praised Martyrs: having boldness before God the Lover of mankind, entreat peace for the world, and great mercy for our souls.[^1336]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 2:*  
As Jesus passed by from the temple, He found a man blind from birth, and taking compassion, placed clay upon his eyes, and said unto him: Go, wash in Siloam; and having washed, he received sight, sending up glory unto God; but his neighbors said unto him: Who opened thine eyes, which none of those that see could heal? And he cried aloud and said: A Man called Jesus, He said unto me: Wash in Siloam, and I received sight; He is truly the One of Whom Moses spake in the Law, Christ the Messiah, He is the Savior of our souls.[^1337]

## ON MONDAY OF THE BLIND MAN, AT MATINS[^1338]

*At God is the Lord, the Resurrection Troparion of the Tone.*[^1339]  
*After the first Stichology, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезна́чальное Сло́во):*  
Let us praise the Cross of the Lord, let us honor His holy Burial with hymns, and let us supremely glorify His Resurrection: for as God He raised the dead together with Himself from the tombs, having despoiled the dominion of death and the might of the devil, and shone forth light upon those in hades.[^1340]

*Verse: O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger, nor chasten me in Thy wrath.*[^1341]  
Let us all run victoriously and meet Christ, ye faithful, with abundance of oil and radiant lamps, that we may be accounted worthy within the bridal chamber: for he that is found outside the doors shall cry in vain unto God: Have mercy on me![^1342]

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^1343]  
*Martyrikon:*  
O Lord, Thy passion-bearers, having emulated the cup of Thy Passion, abandoned worldly beauty and became companions of the Angels: by their supplications grant peace unto our souls, O Christ God, and great mercy.[^1344]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Together with the heavenly Angels, with earthly men, in a voice of rejoicing we cry unto thee, O Theotokos: Rejoice, thou who hast opened the gates of heaven; rejoice, the only salvation of earthborn mortals; rejoice, pure one; rejoice, full of grace, who didst give birth to God incarnate![^1345]

*After the second Stichology, Sessional Hymn.*[^1346]  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезна́чальное Сло́во):*  
Before Thy Cross and Resurrection, O Compassionate One, as Thou wast going on the way, the man blind from birth cried warmly: O Son of David, have mercy on me! Grant light unto mine eyes, that I also may behold Thee! And having anointed his pupils with clay made from spittle, O Word, Thou didst brightly grant him light.[^1347]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*[^1348]  
*Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ... Psalm 50.*[^1349]  
*And after this, the Canon of the Blind Man with the Heirmos to 8; and of the Menaion to 6.*[^1350]  
*Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Blind Man.*[^1351]

*At the Praises, we set 6 verses.*[^1352]  
*And we sing the stichera of the Oktoechos.*  
*Tone 5:*  
O Lord, while the tomb was sealed by the lawless, Thou camest forth from the tomb, even as Thou wast born of the Theotokos. Thine incorporeal Angels understood not how Thou wast incarnate; the soldiers who guarded Thee perceived not when Thou didst rise: for both events were sealed unto those who would search them out, but the wonders were revealed unto those who worship the mystery in faith. Unto us who hymn it, render joy and great mercy.[^1353]  
*(Twice)*

Woe is me! Unto what have I likened myself? Unto the barren fig tree; and I fear the curse together with the cutting down: but O heavenly Husbandman, Christ God, make fruitful my fallow soul, and receive me as the prodigal son, and have mercy on me![^1354]

*Martyrikon:*  
Blessed is the army of the heavenly King! For even though the passion-bearers were earthborn, yet they hastened to attain unto the angelic dignity: despising their bodies, through sufferings they were accounted worthy of the honor of the incorporeal ones. By their supplications, O Lord, send down upon us great mercy.[^1355]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 6:*  
Considering his whole life as night, the blind man cried aloud unto Thee: O Lord, open my pupils, O Son of David, our Savior, that I also with all may hymn Thy power![^1356]

*At the Aposticha, stichera of the feast.*[^1357]  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "O venerable father" (Преподо́бне ѻ́тче):*  
The blind man became blindness unto those who supposedly see, darkening their reasons, souls, and mind as they beheld his unexpected recovery of sight; and having summoned him, they questioned him with malice: How seest thou now as one of those who behold the light, being blind from birth, and sitting beside the ways and begging? Therefore he showed forth the Giver of light, Who created the luminaries in the world, by Whom the Son of God is proclaimed, Who is before all beginning, Who in these last times appeared as man from the Virgin through the divine Spirit for compassion's sake.[^1358]

*Verse: Look upon me, and have mercy on me. (Ps. 118:132)*[^1359]  
As a burden and an earthly weight he seemed, walking in the world as blind and bruising his feet along the thoroughfares, enriched without pledge of vision; therefore he ran unto the Giver of light, from Whom he received sight to behold the light, and with his eyes to see the Creator, Who in His own likeness and image created human nature from the earth at the first, and now with dust and spittle enlightened his pupils, granting him in His love for mankind to behold the sun.[^1360]

*Verse: Direct my steps according to Thy word. (Ps. 118:133)*[^1361]  
Conformed to the human image, the blind man, having seen the light, beheld the Word of the Father, from Whom he saw the light-bearing and day-originating sun even as other men, rejoicing in the newly revealed vision, by which he walked straightly, traversing the paths without labor, and acknowledging the Son of God Who enlightened him, Who became man for the sake of extreme tender mercy, and Who, remaining God as He was, became man and assumed what He was not, preserving the unconfused union of these natures.[^1362]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 5:*  
O Lord, as Thou wast passing by along the way, Thou didst find a man blind from birth; and the Disciples, being astonished, questioned Thee, saying: Teacher, who sinned: this man or his parents, that he was born blind? But Thou, my Savior, didst cry out unto them: Neither did this man sin, nor his parents, but that the works of God might be revealed in him. It behooves Me to work the works of Him that sent Me, which no man can do. And having said this, having spat on the ground and made clay, Thou didst anoint his eyes, saying unto him: Go, wash in the pool of Siloam. And having washed, he became whole, and cried unto Thee: I believe, Lord! and he worshipped Thee. Therefore we also cry aloud: Have mercy on us![^1363]

*On Monday of the Blind Man, the Three-Ode Canon, whose acrostic is: Δόξα Θεῷ πρέπει, that is: Glory is fitting unto God.*[^1364]

*Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who hath wrought marvelous wonders in the Red Sea, a song of victory: for He hath been glorified.[^1365]

*Troparion [D]:*  
By Thy precious Cross, O Lord, we all have been delivered from the ancient curse: therefore do we glorify Thy goodness.[^1366]

*Troparion [O]:*  
He that was crucified of His own will hath risen, Christ our Savior, having shattered all the captives of death: Him do we glorify![^1367]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [X]:*  
Having beheld the strange sight of the gleaming Angel, and considering the divine Resurrection of the Creator, the Myrrh-bearing women were filled with joy.[^1368]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion [A]:*  
Thou wast the East, O all-pure Virgin, of the Sun of righteousness, whereby we were delivered from the darkness of ignorance.[^1369]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
The Creator of creation, of Whom the Angels stand in awe, hymn, ye peoples, and supremely exalt unto all ages![^1370]

*Troparion [Th]:*  
Beholding God crucified in the flesh, the sun was darkened, and the earth was shaken, and the rocks were split asunder.[^1371]

*Troparion [E]:*  
On the third day Christ rose from the dead, and raised up with Himself all that sat in darkness and the shadow of death.[^1372]

*Troparion [O]:*  
After Thy Rising Thou didst appear unto the Apostles, O Compassionate One, and didst fill them with ineffable gladness.[^1373]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadikon [P]:*  
The all-holy Father, the Word co-eternal, and the uncreated divine Spirit, the Trinity, we unceasingly hymn.[^1374]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion [R]:*  
Deliver from torment and eternal condemnation us that hymn thee, O pure Birthgiver of God![^1375]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Thee, blessed among women and blessed by God, we, the human race, magnify with hymns.[^1376]

*Troparion [E]:*  
Thou didst stretch out Thy hands upon the Cross, O Compassionate One, drawing unto Thyself me who had been cast down by the many deceits of the crafty one.[^1377]

*Troparion [P]:*  
Having descended unto the gates of hades, O Christ, Thou didst raise up all that were below together with Thyself, O Lover of mankind, and didst lift up those who hymn Thy power.[^1378]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [E]:*  
Thou didst pour forth life unto the dead, O Christ, and having risen on the third day, Thou didst ascend in glory, while Thy venerable Disciples beheld Thee.[^1379]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion [I]:*  
Render Thy Son and the God of all merciful unto us, O all-pure Virgin, who hymn thee in faith.[^1380]

## ON MONDAY OF THE BLIND MAN, AT VESPERS[^1381]

*At Lord, I have cried, we set 6 verses: and we sing 3 stichera of the feast, and 3 of the Menaion.*[^1382]  
*Stichera of the feast:*  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*  
With lips, soul, and mind, he that was previously blind confessed Him Who creatively gave pupils of the eyes from dust and spittle and total enlightenment, as the cosmic Overseer of creatures and Arranger, Who became man solely out of compassion for His creation, and proclaimed Him as almighty God; Whom the scribes, being totally unable to behold through envy and because of his converse with them, acting ever more blindly, judged worthy of expulsion from the synagogue, Him Who once gave the blind man pupils, both spiritual and bodily.[^1383]

The blind man won the victory of valor over those who see unto the end, for being blind he beheld Him as Maker of this world and Creator of all, having seen the structure formed from spittle, and by this alone recognized the Enlightener, both the Son of God and the cosmic Master; Whom those seeing, blinded by the malice of envy, saw not at all, even while beholding these most marvelous workings of wonders manifoldly with one voice.[^1384]

Unto the doubting, blind scribes, he that was previously blind appeared intertwined, as though seeing not at all, but falsely feigning to see for the Savior's sake unto those wishing to be blinded, the darkened race in whom the sweet Sun truly shines, renewing the sabbath-born choir; He Who wrought its filial light, taking away the covering, and granted light unto those formerly blind among them: and now, seeing, they proclaim the Giver of light unto the world.[^1385]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 8:*  
O Christ God, noetic Sun of righteousness, Who by Thy most pure touch didst on both sides enlighten him who from the womb was deprived of light, and didst illumine our spiritual eyes: make us to appear as sons of the day, that in faith we may cry unto Thee: For Thy great and ineffable tender mercy toward us, O Lover of mankind, glory to Thee![^1386]

*At the Aposticha, stichera.*  
*Tone 5:*  
Giving Resurrection unto the human race, He was led as a sheep to the slaughter: the princes of hades were terrified of Him, and the gates of weeping were lifted up; for Christ, the King of glory, entered in, saying unto those in bonds: Come forth! and unto those in darkness: Reveal yourselves![^1387]

*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in heaven. (Ps. 122:1)*[^1388]  
O Lord, I both fear Thy judgment and cease not from doing evil. Who at the judgment seat fears not the judge? Or who, desiring to be healed, provokes the physician to wrath as I do? O Long-suffering Lord, have compassion upon my weakness, and have mercy on me![^1389]

*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us. (Ps. 122:3)*[^1390]  
*Martyrikon:*  
Armed with the shield of faith and strengthened by the sign of the Cross, Thy saints, O Lord, manfully gave themselves over unto torments, and abolished the deceit and audacity of the devil. By their supplications, as almighty God, send down peace upon the world, and great mercy upon our souls.[^1391]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 8:*  
Incarnate for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O Christ God, by the compassion of Thine ineffable compassions Thou didst account him who was deprived of light from the womb worthy of the divine radiance, having touched his pupils with dust by Thy creative fingers: Thyself also now, O Giver of light, enlighten our spiritual senses, as the only grudgeless Giver![^1392]

## ON TUESDAY OF THE BLIND MAN, AT MATINS[^1393]

*The Resurrection Troparion of the Tone.*[^1394]  
*After the first Stichology, Sessional Hymn.*[^1395]  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезна́чальное Сло́во):*  
O Lord, Thou wast called dead, Who didst put death to death; Thou wast placed in a tomb, Who didst empty the tombs: above, the soldiers guarded the tomb; below, Thou didst raise the dead from all eternity. O almighty and incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee![^1396]

*Verse: O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger, nor chasten me in Thy wrath. (Ps. 6:2)*[^1397]  
When the Judge sits and the Angels stand by, the trumpet sounding and the flame burning, what wilt thou do, O my soul, being led unto judgment? For then thy wicked deeds shall stand forth, and thy secret transgressions shall be exposed. Therefore before the end cry aloud unto the Judge: O God, cleanse me and save me![^1398]

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel. (Ps. 67:36)*[^1399]  
*Martyrikon:*  
Today the memory of the passion-bearers shines, having illumination from heaven: the choir of Angels keeps triumph, and the human race feasts together; therefore they pray unto the Lord that our souls may find mercy.[^1400]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
O all-holy Mother of God, wall of Christians: deliver thy people as is thy wont, who fervently call upon thee; stand against our enemies and lofty thoughts, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, full of grace![^1401]

*After the second Stichology, Sessional Hymn.*[^1402]  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезна́чальное Сло́во):*  
Having received sight through the spittle of Him Who fashioned the human composition, he that was previously blind, who from birth saw not the great sun, is beheld; therefore from his soul he sends up thanksgiving unto God, seeing His image, the image fashioned according to the likeness of Him Who fashioned it.[^1403]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*[^1404]  
*We sing the Canon of the Blind Man with the Heirmos to 8; and of the Menaion to 6.*[^1405]  
*Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Blind Man.*[^1406]

*At the Praises, stichera of the Oktoechos.*  
*Tone 5:*  
O Lord, Who didst shatter the eternal bars and break bonds asunder, Thou didst rise from the tomb, leaving behind Thy grave-clothes as a witness of Thy true three-day Burial; and Thou didst go before into Galilee, Thou Who wast guarded in the cave. Great is Thy mercy, O incomprehensible Savior: have mercy on us and save us![^1407]  
*(Twice)*

Overlook the multitude of my transgressions, O Lord Who wast born of the Virgin, and cleanse all mine iniquities, granting me the thought of return, as the only Lover of mankind, I pray Thee; and have mercy on me![^1408]

*Martyrikon:*  
Thy passion-bearers, O Lord, emulating the angelic ranks, endured torments as though incorporeal, having a single-minded hope: the enjoyment of promised blessings. By their supplications, O Christ our God, grant peace unto Thy world, and great mercy unto our souls![^1409]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 8:*  
Who shall declare Thy powers, O Christ? Or who shall recount the multitude of Thy wonders? For as Thou wast seen as two-fold upon earth in Thy goodness, so two-fold healings didst Thou grant unto the sick; for not only the bodily eyes didst Thou open of him who was blind from the womb, but also the spiritual: therefore he confessed Thee as the God Who was hidden, and Who grantest great mercy unto all.[^1410]

*At the Aposticha, prosomoia.*[^1411]  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "O venerable father" (Преподо́бне ѻ́тче):*  
Beholding the light-bearing radiance that shone forth clearly on the Sabbath for the light-bearing blind man, the keepers of the Law of Moses were confounded, being blinded by darkness; beholding the shadow, they understood not the overshadowing of the Law that covered them, from which they saw not the Giver of light and the Maker of the Sabbath, Who intertwined the blind man by word, by washing, and by the new clay-making from spittle; with whom let us, hearing, behold that God, rebuking the Pharisaic blindness with the sight that is from what is better.[^1412]

*Verse: Look upon me, and have mercy on me. (Ps. 118:132)*[^1413]  
The dawn shone forth unto him who remained in the darkness of the night of blindness, the long-suffering blind man washed in the streams of the pool of Siloam, light unto his eyes by the divine command; therefore the light-bearing one is seen unknowing, exposing the gloom of the darkness of the legalistic scribes who honor letters, and enlightening their darkened ignorance with the light-bearing rays that have shone forth, from which the blindness of the shadowy letters of old is intertwined by the bright radiances granted unto us by the Word.[^1414]

*Verse: Direct my steps according to Thy word. (Ps. 118:133)*[^1415]  
Unto the height of light-bearing ascent did the blind man ascend, enlightened by the light of the knowledge of God, having endured bodily and mental darkness, being now wholly enlightened by new outpourings of light from the Word, even if at first he was blinded in both; and he acknowledged the Giver of light and the Worker of light Who shone forth from the tomb on the third day and made the earth bright by His Resurrection: from Whom the light of recreation shone forth unto men held in darkness, for the sake of tender mercy and great mercy.[^1416]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 8:*  
O Christ God, noetic Sun of righteousness, Who by Thy most pure touch didst on both sides enlighten him who from the womb was deprived of light, and didst illumine our spiritual eyes: make us to appear as sons of the day, that in faith we may cry unto Thee: For Thy great and ineffable tender mercy toward us, O Lover of mankind, glory to Thee![^1417]

*On Tuesday of the Blind Man, the Three-Ode Canon, work of Joseph, whose acrostic is: ᾨδὴ αὕτη Ἰωσήφ, that is: This hymn is of Joseph.*[^1418]

*Tone 5. Ode 2. Heirmos:*  
See, see that I am God, Who clothed Myself in flesh of Mine own will, that I might save Adam who fell through transgression by the deceit of the serpent![^1419]

*Troparion [O]:*  
Even as Moses of old lifted up the serpent, so wast Thou lifted up upon the Tree, O Compassionate One, driving away the serpentine venom and malice of the crafty enemy, and saving us![^1420]

*Troparion [D]:*  
Glory to Thy glory, O supremely glorified Jesus! For Thou wast placed in the tomb of Thine own will as dead, yet Thou didst rise as God, and by Thy Rising didst raise up the world together with Thyself, as almighty![^1421]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [E]:*  
Behold, the Giver of life is risen from the dead! cried the gleaming Angel of old unto the women: Weep no longer therefore, but announce His Rising unto the Apostles![^1422]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion [A]:*  
O holy Lord, Who wast born of the pure Virgin, and wast crucified of Thine own will, and buried, and didst rise from the dead: by her supplications send down Thy mercy upon all![^1423]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
The Creator of creation, of Whom the Angels stand in awe, hymn, ye peoples, and supremely exalt unto all ages![^1424]

*Troparion [Y]:*  
We hymn Thy three-day Burial, O Christ, and with faith we unceasingly praise Thy most radiant Resurrection![^1425]

*Troparion [T]:*  
Christ, Who was crucified for our sake, and laid in the tomb, and Who by His Rising enlightened the world, do we praise in song![^1426]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [E]:*  
O Christ, unwaning Sun, Who wast buried and didst rise from the dead: enlighten the thoughts of us all, that we may glorify Thee unceasingly![^1427]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion [I]:*  
Offer supplication unto the Word incarnate of thee, O Virgin Theotokos, that He may have compassion upon us humble ones![^1428]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
We magnify Thee, O Christ, and Thy most blameless, pure Mother, for she supernaturally brought Thee forth in the flesh, Who deliverest us from all deceit and corruption![^1429]

*Troparion [O]:*  
As a lamb Thou wast led to the slaughter, O Master, Christ God, and by Thy wounds, which Thou didst endure of Thine own will, Thou didst bind all the wicked host of the ages![^1430]

*Troparion [S]:*  
The bars of hades were shattered by Thy coming, O Christ, and they that had decayed from all eternity arose, hymning Thee as Victor and Ruler over all![^1431]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [E]:*  
Thy holy Rising from the tomb, O Christ, is life, and Thine Ascension is everlasting gladness, which we all now forefeast in faith![^1432]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion [Ph]:*  
Having given birth for us unto the Light from Light, O all-pure Virgin, thou didst destroy the darkness of godlessness; therefore all we earthborn mortals magnify thee in faith![^1433]

## ON TUESDAY OF THE SIXTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA, AT VESPERS[^1434]
*Apodosis of the Feast of Pascha.*[^1435]

*After the Ninth Hour, the priest makes the beginning with the censer and with a candle, the Royal Doors being opened, and sings: "Christ is risen from the dead..." with the verses. And after the verses, the Psalm: "Bless the Lord, O my soul..." And the litany. Then the customary Kathisma.*[^1436]

*At Lord, I have cried, we sing 6 automelic stichera of the Blind Man.*[^1437]  
*Tone 2:*  
He that was born blind said in his reasoning: Did I then be born without eyes on account of parental sins? Was I born for a rebuke on account of the unbelief of the Gentiles? I am at a loss to ask when it is night, when it is day; my feet cannot endure the stumbling against stones; for I saw not the sun shining, nor Him Who created me in His image: but I pray Thee, O Christ God: look upon me, and have mercy on me![^1438]

As Jesus passed by from the sanctuary, He found a man blind from birth, and taking compassion, placed clay upon his eyes, and said unto him: Go, wash in Siloam; and having washed, he received sight, sending up glory unto God. But his neighbors said unto him: Who opened thine eyes, which none of those that see could heal? And he cried aloud and said: A Man called Jesus, He said unto me: Wash in Siloam, and I received sight. He is truly the One of Whom Moses spake in the Law, Christ the Messiah: He is the Savior of our souls![^1439]

*Tone 4:*  
Considering his whole life as night, the blind man cried aloud unto Thee: O Lord, open my pupils, O Son of David, our Savior, that I also with all may hymn Thy power![^1440]

*Tone 5:*  
O Lord, as Thou wast passing by along the way, Thou didst find a man blind from birth; and the Disciples, being astonished, questioned Thee, saying: Teacher, who sinned: this man or his parents, that he was born blind? But Thou, my Savior, didst cry out unto them: Neither did this man sin, nor his parents, but that the works of God might be revealed in him. It behooves Me to work the works of Him that sent Me, which no man can do. And having said this, having spat on the ground and made clay, Thou didst anoint his eyes, saying unto him: Go, wash in the pool of Siloam. And having washed, he became whole, and cried unto Thee: I believe, Lord! and he worshipped Thee. Therefore we also cry aloud: Have mercy on us![^1441]

*Tone 8:*  
O Christ God, noetic Sun of righteousness, Who by Thy most pure touch didst on both sides enlighten him who from the womb was deprived of light, and didst illumine our spiritual eyes: make us to appear as sons of the day, that in faith we may cry unto Thee: For Thy great and ineffable tender mercy toward us, O Lover of mankind, glory to Thee![^1442]

Incarnate for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O Christ God, by the compassion of Thine ineffable compassions Thou didst account him who was deprived of light from the womb worthy of the divine radiance, having touched his pupils with dust by Thy creative fingers: Thyself also now, O Giver of light, enlighten our spiritual senses, as the only grudgeless Giver![^1443]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 8:*  
Who shall declare Thy powers, O Christ? Or who shall recount the multitude of Thy wonders? For as Thou wast seen as two-fold upon earth in Thy goodness, so two-fold healings didst Thou grant unto the sick; for not only the bodily eyes didst Thou open of him who was blind from the womb, but also the spiritual: therefore he confessed Thee as the God Who was hidden, and Who grantest great mercy unto all.[^1444]

*Prokeimenon of the day.*[^1445]

*At the Aposticha, sticheron.*  
*Tone 5:*  
Thee, Christ the incarnate Savior, Who wast not parted from heaven, we magnify in voices of song: for Thou didst accept the Cross and death for our race, as the Lord Lover of mankind; and having overthrown the gates of hades, Thou didst rise on the third day, saving our souls.[^1446]

*Then: Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered... And we sing the Stichera of Pascha with their refrains: A sacred Pascha hath been shown forth unto us today... and the rest. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. It is the day of Resurrection... And afterwards: "Christ is risen from the dead..." with melody, once.*[^1447]

*Then...*[^1448]

*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart... The Trisagion. And after Our Father: Resurrection Troparion, Tone 5: "The Word co-unoriginate..." Glory... Both now... Its Theotokion. And the litany: Have mercy on us, O God... And the priest gives the customary dismissal without the Cross: "He Who rose from the dead..." And he commemorates the saint of the day.*[^1449]

*At Compline, after "It is truly right", and after the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the Blind Man. Glory... Both now... of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave..."*[^1450]

*The Midnight Office we sing according to custom with the censer.*

*After the 1st Trisagion, instead of: "Behold, the Bridegroom comes": we say the Resurrection Troparion: "The Word co-unoriginate..." Glory... Both now... Its Theotokion.*

*After the 2nd Trisagion, instead of: "Remember, O Lord, for Thou art good": we say the Kontakion of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave..." Thereafter: Lord, have mercy (40). And the dismissal. But the prayer: "Remember, O Lord, in hope..." we do not say.*[^1451]

## ON WEDNESDAY OF THE SIXTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA, AT MATINS[^1452]

*The priest makes the beginning: "Glory to the holy, and consubstantial..." according to custom, with the censer, and with the verses, as was indicated for the evening. And after the verses, the superior or the reader says: "Glory to God in the highest..." And the Six Psalms according to custom.*

*At "God is the Lord", the Resurrection Troparion:*[^1453]  
*Tone 5: "The Word co-unoriginate..." (Twice).*  
*Glory... Both now... Its Theotokion.*

*After the 1st Stichology, the Resurrection Sessional Hymn.*[^1454]  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion: written for the Sunday of the Blind Man (see fol. 442).*  
*And a reading of John.*

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 5:*  
Co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, Who art clothed with light as with a garment, in Thy love for mankind Thou didst clothe Thyself in our nature, and didst drive away human infirmities as Lord: enlighten also the pupils of him who was deprived of light from his mother's womb![^1455]

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ... Psalm 50.*[^1456]

*Three Canons: of Pascha without Theotokia with the heirmos to 6, and of the Blind Man to 4.*  
*And of the Forefeast of the Ascension, to 4.*

*Canon of the Forefeast of the Ascension, whose acrostic is according to the alphabet.*[^1457]

*Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
The steed and the rider hath Christ overthrown in the Red Sea, crushing wars with a high arm; but Israel hath He saved, singing a song of victory.[^1458]

*Troparion [A]:*  
Christ ascends on high unto the Father, and brings the flesh which He took from us: Him let us hymn in praise today, singing a song of victory![^1459]  
*(Twice)*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [B]:*  
The books of the divine Scriptures and the proclamations of the all-wise speakers of God have clearly received their fulfillment: for after the Rising, the Master ascends with glory into the heavens.[^1460]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Troparion [G]:*  
The earth mystically rejoices, and the heavens are filled with gladness, at the Ascension of Christ, Who united things formerly separated by grace, and tore down the grievous wall of partition.[^1461]

*Katavasia:*  
*Unto God the Savior: seek in the Ascension.*[^1462]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
O Thou Who didst establish the earth upon nothing by Thy command, and didst suspend its weight hanging without support: establish Thine unshakeable Church upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, Who alone art good and the Lover of mankind![^1463]

*Troparion [D]:*
