Having bound death by Thy death, O Word, Thou didst rise from the dead on the third day; and unto the heavenly realms, O Savior, Thou didst ascend with glory, while the incorporeal hosts praised Thy unutterable dispensation.[^1464]  
*(Twice)*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [E]:*  
Thou didst come unto the earthly, O Christ, without body, and having clothed Thyself in flesh, Thou didst endure the Cross, and having risen from the dead, Thou didst ascend with glory unto Thy Father, the Source of light, having made peace for all things.[^1465]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Troparion [Z]:*  
Having sought on earth the wandering sheep, O Christ, Thou didst join it unto the unwandering, O Word; and having ascended unto the heavens, Thou didst sit in glory at the right hand of Thy Father: glory to Thy great tender mercy![^1466]

*Katavasia:*  
*By the power of Thy Cross, O Christ...*[^1467]

*Kontakion of the Blind Man, Tone 4: "With spiritual eyes..." And the Ikos (see fol. 447).*[^1468]

*Then the Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*  
Thou didst give eyes, O Christ, unto him who was born from the womb without eyes, showing forth unto the Jews, O my Savior, Thy unutterable glory, and that Thou art the Light of all; but they who slandered Thee in envy, plotting deceit in their minds, sought to put to death the Giver of life.[^1469]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Podoben:*  
Thou wast born, as Thou Thyself didst will, O my Savior; and Thou didst appear again, as Thou Thyself didst choose; and Thou didst suffer as a man, yet as God Thou didst rise; unto the heavens with glory Thou didst ascend, and didst lift up human nature, and adorn it with glory.[^1470]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
Perceiving Thy divine condescension, Habakkuk cried with trembling unto Thee: For the salvation of Thy people art Thou come to save Thine anointed![^1471]

*Troparion [H]:*  
Our nature, formerly condemned by sin, O King of all, received mercy by Thy taking it upon Thyself, hymning with fear Thy Rising and divine Ascension.[^1472]

*Troparion [Th]:*  
The ranks of the incorporeal beings marvel, beholding Thee, O Christ, bearing an earthly body, and ascending upon a cloud, and going up unto the heavenly realms.[^1473]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [I]:*  
Seeing Thy garments dyed in crimson, O Christ, King of all, the angelic hosts were struck with awe at Thy going up, and worshipped with fear and joy.[^1474]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Troparion [K]:*  
Let us clap our hands: the Lord, having risen from the dead, went up into the heavens, while the Angels submitted unto Him as our Creator and God.[^1475]

*Katavasia:*  
*I heard the report of the power of the Cross...*[^1476]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, rising early unto Thee I cry: Enlighten my soul that is darkened, O Christ, as the only Compassionate One![^1477]

*Troparion [L]:*  
Having loosed the condemnation of mankind, Christ rose and ascended unto the heavenly realms, honoring with co-enthronement with the Father those whom He loved.[^1478]  
*(Twice)*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [M]:*  
Having made Thy Disciples glad after the Resurrection, O Christ, Thou didst ascend on high unto Thy Father, from Whom Thou wast never parted, even while conversing with men.[^1479]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Troparion [N]:*  
Having fulfilled the foretellings and proclamations of the Law, O Christ, uttered by the God-inspired prophets, Thou didst ascend unto the heavenly realms, a cloud receiving Thee, O Savior.[^1480]

*Katavasia:*  
*Rising early we cry unto Thee...*[^1481]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Raging with a soul-corrupting tempest, O Master Christ, pacify the sea of passions, and lead me up from corruption, as the Compassionate One![^1482]

*Troparion [X]:*  
A strange sight wast Thou shown unto the Angels, bearing the form of a man in Thy dreadful ascent, O Lover of mankind; therefore with fear we praise Thee in song![^1483]  
*(Twice)*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [O]:*  
The Lord of all ascends with glory radiantly unto His unoriginate Father; all creation now keeps festival, rejoicing.[^1484]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Troparion [P]:*  
Let every tongue cry aloud today: In jubilation Christ is gone up, and with the divine sound of a trumpet unto the heavens, which He forsook not![^1485]

*Katavasia:*  
*The abyss hath compassed me...*[^1486]

*Kontakion of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave..." And the Ikos (see fol. 16).*[^1487]  
*Synaxarion of the Menaion.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The supremely exalted Lord of the fathers quenched the flame, and sprinkled the youths with dew, as they sang in unison: Blessed art Thou, O God![^1488]

*Troparion [R]:*  
Having rent asunder the middle wall of enmity, O Lord, by Thy Cross and Passion, borne upon a cloud, Thou didst ascend with glory unto the unoriginate Father.[^1489]

*Troparion [S]:*  
With trembling the powers cried aloud: Lift up the gates; Christ is come, bearing an earthly body, and by His divine Passion having put Beliar to death![^1490]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [T]:*  
Why are Thy garments red, O Lover of mankind? the divine powers asked; Christ, as He ascended, cried aloud: I have now trodden the winepress![^1491]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Troparion [Y]:*  
With hymns we harmoniously glorify Thy Crucifixion, O Christ, and Thy Rising, radiantly celebrating Thine honorable Ascension unto ages of ages.[^1492]

*Katavasia:*  
*In the fiery furnace...*[^1493]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Unto Thee, the Fashioner of all, the youths in the furnace, weaving a world-wide choir, sang: All ye works, hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^1494]

*Troparion [Ph]:*  
Thee, Who art Light of Light, a radiant cloud took up from the Mount of Olives, before the eyes of all Thy divine Disciples, who hymn Thee unto all ages![^1495]

*Troparion [Ch]:*  
Let us clap our hands after the manner of the Psalms: Christ our God is risen, and went up whence He came down unto us in love for mankind, having reconciled things formerly separated![^1496]

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*  
*Troparion [Ps]:*  
The forefather David cries aloud: Christ went up upon His Cherubim, and flew as on the wings of noetic Ranks: Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages![^1497]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Troparion [O]:*  
Having risen from the tomb, Thou didst appear unto the Disciples, and didst lead them up to the Mount of Olives, whence a radiant cloud received Thee, and took Thee up unto the heights, O Christ, with glory![^1498]

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, singing and supremely exalting Him unto all ages.*  
*Katavasia:*  
*Begotten of the Father before the ages...*[^1499]

*We do not sing the Magnificat ("More honorable than the Cherubim..."), but we sing the refrains of Pascha.*[^1500]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Rejoice, O Isaiah! The Virgin hath conceived in the womb, and brought forth a Son, Emmanuel, God and Man; Dayspring is His name: Whom magnifying, we call the Virgin blessed.[^1501]

*Troparion:*  
All creation clearly brings a sacred feast unto Thine Ascension, O Word of God, wherein Thou didst present unto Thy Father the nature which Thou didst take from us, beyond understanding, O Changeless One.[^1502]

*Troparion:*  
As God, O Savior, Thou didst shatter the gates and bars of hades, and having risen from the dead, Thou didst hasten unto the heavenly angelic ranks, who cried with astonishment: Lift up the gates![^1503]

*Troparion:*  
While the divine Disciples marveled at Thy divine ascent, the Angels visibly stood by, crying unto them: This Jesus, Whom ye see ascending into heaven, shall come with glory to judge all![^1504]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion:*  
The peace, O Christ, which Thou didst grant unto the Disciples when Thou didst ascend to the heights of old, send down richly also unto us, preserving all in love: that with one accord we may magnify Thee, the Savior![^1505]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Thou wast truly the light-bearing Chariot of the noetic Sun, O undefiled, pure Maiden, through whom they that sat in darkness saw the light of knowledge, glorifying thee as is meet![^1506]

*Katavasia:*  
*Thee who art beyond understanding...*[^1507]

*Exapostilarion of Pascha: "Having fallen asleep in the flesh..." (Twice), and of the Blind Man: "My spiritual eyes..." (see fol. 440).*[^1508]

*At the Praises, we set 8 verses, and we sing the automelic stichera of the Blind Man, 4.*[^1509]  
*Tone 2:*  
He that was born blind said in his reasoning: Did I then be born without eyes on account of parental sins? Was I born for a rebuke on account of the unbelief of the Gentiles? I am at a loss to ask when it is night, when it is day; my feet cannot endure the stumbling against stones; for I saw not the sun shining, nor Him Who created me in His image: but I pray Thee, O Christ God: look upon me, and have mercy on me![^1510]

As Jesus passed by from the sanctuary, He found a man blind from birth, and taking compassion, placed clay upon his eyes, and said unto him: Go, wash in Siloam; and having washed, he received sight, sending up glory unto God. But his neighbors said unto him: Who opened thine eyes, which none of those that see could heal? And he cried aloud and said: A Man called Jesus, He said unto me: Wash in Siloam, and I received sight. He is truly the One of Whom Moses spake in the Law, Christ the Messiah: He is the Savior of our souls![^1511]

*Then, Tone 4:*  
*Considering his whole life as night...*  
*Tone 5:*  
*O Lord, as Thou wast passing by along the way...*  
*Written for Tuesday evening.*[^1512]

*Then the Stichera of Pascha with their refrains (see fol. 305). Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 5: "It is the day of Resurrection..." Then: "Christ is risen from the dead..." as was indicated for the evening.*[^1513]

*And the Great Doxology. Troparion, Tone 5: "The Word co-unoriginate..." Glory... Both now... Its Theotokion. The litany, and the dismissal without the Cross. And he commemorates the saint of the day on the dismissal.*[^1514]

*And the First Hour, in the narthex. At the First Hour we say the customary psalms. At "Glory", Troparion: "The Word co-unoriginate..." Both now... Theotokion: "What shall we call thee..."*[^1515]

*After the Trisagion, Kontakion: "With spiritual eyes..." And the final dismissal according to custom; and the other Hours before the Liturgy we say with three psalms. Troparion: "The Word co-unoriginate..." changing the Kontakia.*[^1516]

*The Beginning of the Divine Liturgy as on Pascha itself.*[^1517]

*At the Divine Liturgy, the Typika, and from the Canon, Odes 3 and 6. After the Entrance, Troparion: "The Word co-unoriginate..." Glory... Kontakion of the Blind Man. Both now... of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave..." and the rest. Prokeimenon of Pascha: "This is the day which the Lord hath made..." Alleluia of Pascha. The Epistle and Gospel of the day. And the rest of the service all resurrectional.*  
*Communion Hymn: "Receive the Body of Christ..." And we also say Psalm 33: "I will bless the Lord..."*[^1518]

*Then the priest makes the dismissal of Pascha with the Cross, and all the rest of the conclusion of the Liturgy we sing as was indicated on Pascha itself.*[^1519]

*And thus is celebrated the Apodosis of the Feast of the Holy and Life-giving Resurrection of Christ.*

*At the refectory there is great consolation for the brethren, that is: with oil, fish, and wine.*[^1520]

## ON THURSDAY OF THE SIXTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA: THE FEAST OF THE ASCENSION OF OUR LORD GOD AND SAVIOR JESUS CHRIST[^1521]

*On Wednesday Evening:*  
*The Ninth Hour, with three psalms.*  
*At the beginning the reader sings the Trisagion, and so until Pentecost Sunday at every church singing.*[^1522]

*At Small Vespers.*[^1523]

*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 4 stichera.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нб҃нымъ чинѡ́мъ):*  
Thou didst fashion human nature, renewing all things through Thyself, and having ascended in the flesh, Thou didst sit on high, O Word of God, at the right hand of the Father; therefore we all await Thee to come again as Judge of the faithful and the unfaithful.[^1524]

The Angels cried unto the Apostles of Christ: Ye men, why gaze ye upon Christ the Giver of life ascending on high? In like manner He shall come as Judge of all, being the God of all, and rendering unto all according to the desert of their deeds.[^1525]

Having accounted them that were enlightened by Thine Ascension, O Savior and Lover of mankind, worthy of immortal and divine glory, enlighten the hearts of us also who sing Thy praise, by Thine illumination![^1526]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 6:*  
Come, assembly of the faithful, having secretly learned the minds of Christ's Disciples, let us utter a ceaseless hymn, as the Apostles on the Mount of Olives, let us cry aloud with David: God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet, to deliver the human race from the snare of the proud one, and to enlighten our souls![^1527]

*At the Aposticha, stichera.*[^1528]  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
O marvelous wonder! For human nature ascended into the heavens, having been united unto the Word, God the Almighty.[^1529]

*Verse: Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing. (Ps. 46:1)*[^1530]  
The bright and all-radiant day of the Master's divine Ascent into the heavens hath shone forth, enlightening all things.[^1531]

*Verse: God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet. (Ps. 46:5)*[^1532]  
Even as Thou didst send from on high Thy consubstantial Spirit unto the Disciples: send also, O Christ Savior, Thy grace unto Thy people![^1533]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha":*  
Beholding the Lord borne upon the clouds, the Mother of God together with the Apostles sings God-befitting praise.[^1534]

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart... The Trisagion. After Our Father, the Dismissal Troparion.*[^1535]  
*Tone 4:*  
Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God, having gladdened the Disciples by the promise of the Holy Spirit, when they were assured by the blessing that Thou art the Son of God, the Redeemer of the world![^1536]

*Then the little litany.*  
*And the dismissal.*[^1537]

*[Woodcut Plate: The Ascension of our Lord Jesus Christ into heaven in the presence of the Most Holy Theotokos and the Holy Apostles]*[^1538]

*At Great Vespers.*[^1539]

*After the Introductory Psalm, we do not sing the Kathisma.*

*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 10 verses. And we sing 5 automelic stichera, repeating them.*[^1540]

*Tone 6:*  
The Lord ascended into the heavens, that He might send the Comforter unto the world; the heavens prepared His throne, the clouds His ascent; the Angels marvel, seeing a Man above themselves; the Father awaits Him Whom He hath co-eternal in His bosom; and the Holy Spirit commands all His Angels: Lift up your gates, ye princes; clap your hands, all ye nations, for Christ is gone up thither where He was before![^1541]  
*(Twice)*

O Lord, the Cherubim marveled at Thine Ascension, seeing Thee, God, ascending upon the clouds, Who sittest upon them; and we glorify Thee, for Thy mercy is good: glory to Thee![^1542]  
*(Twice)*

Beholding Thine Ascension upon the holy mountains, O Christ, Brightness of the Father's glory, we hymn the light-like appearance of Thy countenance; we worship Thy Passions, we honor Thy Resurrection, glorifying Thine Ascension: have mercy on us![^1543]  
*(Twice)*

O Lord, when the Apostles saw Thee taken up upon the clouds, filled with grief, with weeping of tears they said, weeping: O Master, leave us not orphans, Thy servants whom in Thy tender mercy Thou hast loved, for Thou art compassionate: but send, as Thou didst promise unto us, Thy Most Holy Spirit, enlightening our souls![^1544]  
*(Twice)*

O Lord, having accomplished the mystery of the dispensation, taking Thy Disciples unto the Mount of Olives, Thou wast taken up, and behold, Thou didst pass through the firmament of heaven, Thou Who for my sake didst become poor like unto me; and having ascended thither whence Thou wast never parted, send down Thy Most Holy Spirit, enlightening our souls![^1545]  
*(Twice) Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 6:*
Without being parted from the Father's bosom, O sweetest Jesus, and having lived with them that are on earth as man, today Thou didst ascend in glory from the Mount of Olives; and having mercifully lifted up our fallen nature, Thou didst seat it with the Father; therefore the heavenly Ranks of the incorporeal beings, marveling at the wonder, were struck with awe, and seized with trembling, magnified Thy love for mankind: with them we also on earth, glorifying Thy condescension toward us, and Thine Ascension from us, make supplication, saying: O Thou Who didst fill the Disciples and the Mother of God who bore Thee with boundless joy at Thine Ascension, make us also worthy of the joy of Thine elect by their prayers, for the sake of Thy great mercy![^1546]

*Entrance. The Prokeimenon of the day, and the Readings.*[^1547]

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 2 [vv. 2–3]):*[^1548]  
Thus saith the Lord: It shall come to pass in the last days, that the mountain of the Lord, and the house of God shall be manifest upon the tops of the mountains, and it shall be exalted above the hills; and all nations shall come unto it. And many peoples shall go and say: Come, and let us go up to the mountain of the Lord, and to the house of the God of Jacob; and He will announce unto us His way, and we will walk in it.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 62 [vv. 10–12] and Chapter 63 [vv. 1–3, 7–9]):*[^1549]  
Thus saith the Lord: Go through, enter through My gates, prepare My way, and make a way for My people, and cast away the stones that are in the way; lift up a standard for the nations. For behold, the Lord hath made it to be heard unto the ends of the earth: Say ye to the daughter of Sion: Behold, thy Savior comes, having His reward with Him, and His work before His face. And He shall call them a holy people, redeemed by the Lord; and thou shalt be called: A city sought out, and not forsaken. Who is this that comes from Edom, with red garments from Bosor? He that is comely in His apparel, crying out with great strength: I speak righteousness, and the judgment of salvation. Why are Thy garments red, and Thy vesture as from the treading of a winepress full of treading? I have trodden the winepress alone, and of the nations there was no man with Me. I remembered the mercy of the Lord, the virtues of the Lord, the praise of the Lord in all things wherein He recompenses us. The Lord, a good Judge to the house of Israel, brings upon us according to His mercy, and according to the multitude of His righteousness. And He said: Are they not My people? children, and they will not reject Me; and He became unto them for salvation from all their affliction. Neither an intercessor, nor an Angel, but the Lord Himself saved them, because He loves them and spars them; He Himself redeemed them, and took them up, and lifted them up all the days of the age.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Zechariah (Chapter 14 [vv. 4, 8–11]):*[^1550]  
Thus saith the Lord: Behold, the day of the Lord comes, and His feet shall stand in that day upon the Mount of Olives, which is opposite Jerusalem, toward the east of the sun. And in that day living water shall go forth out of Jerusalem, half of it toward the former sea, and half of it toward the hinder sea; in summer and in spring it shall be so. And the Lord shall be King over all the earth: in that day shall the Lord be one, and His name one, encompassing all the earth and the wilderness from Gabaa unto Remmon, opposite the south of Jerusalem; and she shall be exalted, and shall remain in her place, from the gate of Benjamin unto the place of the first gate, unto the corner gate, and from the tower of Hananeel unto the king's winepresses. Men shall dwell in her, and there shall be no more anathema, and Jerusalem shall dwell safely.

*At Great Vespers. At the Litiya, automelic stichera:*[^1551]

*Tone 1:*  
Having ascended into the heavens, whence also Thou didst descend, leave us not orphans, O Lord: let Thy Spirit come, bringing peace unto the world; show unto the sons of men the works of Thy power, O Lord, Lover of mankind![^1552]

Thou hast ascended, O Christ, unto Thine unoriginate Father, without being separated from His uncircumscribed bosom, and the hosts of praise of the thrice-holy hymn admitted no addition, but recognized Thee as the only-begotten Son unto the Father, even after Thine incarnation, O Lord: in the multitude of Thy compassions have mercy on us![^1553]

Thine Angels, O Lord, said unto the Apostles: Ye men of Galilee, why stand ye gazing into heaven? This is Christ God, Who hath ascended from you into heaven; He shall come again in the same manner as ye beheld Him going into heaven: serve Him in holiness and righteousness![^1554]

*Tone 4:*  
When Thou didst come unto the Mount of Olives, O Christ, to fulfill the Father’s good pleasure, the heavenly Angels were struck with awe, and the subterranean realms were terrified; and the Disciples stood trembling with joy as Thou didst speak unto them, while a cloud was prepared before Thee as an awaiting throne; and heaven, opening its gates, revealed its beauty, and the earth uncovered its treasures, that Adam’s descent might be made known, and again his ascent: but Thy feet were lifted up as were Thy hands, and Thy mouth abundantly blessed as they heard; the cloud took Thee up, and heaven received Thee within: this deed hast Thou wrought, O Lord, great and glorious, for the salvation of our souls![^1555]

Adam’s nature, which had descended into the lowest parts of the earth, which Thou didst renew in Thyself, Thou hast raised up today above every principality and power: for as Thou didst love it, Thou didst seat it with Thyself; as Thou didst take pity on it, Thou didst unite it unto Thyself; as Thou didst unite it, Thou didst suffer with it; as Thou being impassible didst suffer, Thou didst also glorify it with Thyself. But the incorporeal beings said: Who is this comely Man? Yet not a man only, but both God and Man, united together and made manifest. Therefore the Angels, marveling in their garments, flew around the Disciples, and others standing by cried aloud: Ye men of Galilee, this Jesus, the God-Man, Who hath departed from you, the God-Man shall come again as Judge of the living and the dead, bestowing upon the faithful forgiveness of sins and great mercy![^1556]

When Thou didst ascend in glory, O Christ God, while the Disciples were looking on, the clouds took Thee up with Thy flesh; the heavenly gates were lifted up; the choir of Angels rejoiced with gladness; the higher Powers cried aloud, saying: Lift up your gates, ye princes, and the King of glory shall enter in! And the Disciples, marveling, said: Depart not from us, O Good Shepherd, but send unto us Thy Most Holy Spirit, guiding, confirming, enlightening, and sanctifying our souls![^1557]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 4:*  
O Lord, having fulfilled as the Good One the mystery hidden from ages and from generations, Thou didst come with Thy Disciples unto the Mount of Olives, having her who bore Thee, the Creator and Fashioner of all: for it was meet that she who as a mother grieved in Thy Passion more than all, should also enjoy exceedingly abundant joy through the glory of Thy flesh, of which we also partake, and through Thine Ascent into the heavens, O Master, we glorify Thy great mercy shown unto us![^1558]

*On Thursday of the Ascension, at Great Vespers.*

*At the Aposticha, automelic stichera, Tone 2:*[^1559]  
Thou wast born as Thou Thyself didst will; Thou didst appear as Thou Thyself didst desire; Thou didst suffer in the flesh, O our God; Thou didst rise from the dead, having trampled down death; Thou didst ascend in glory, filling all things, and Thou didst send unto us the divine Spirit, that we might hymn and glorify Thy Godhead.[^1560]

*Verse: Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing. (Ps. 46:1)*[^1561]  
When Thou wast taken up, O Christ, from the Mount of Olives, the Powers, seeing it, cried one to another: Who is this? And one saith unto them: This is the Mighty and Strong One; this is the Mighty in battle; this is truly the King of glory! And why are His garments crimson? He comes from Bosor, which is the flesh! And Thou Thyself, sitting as God at the right hand of Majesty, hast sent unto us the Holy Spirit, that He may guide and save our souls.[^1562]

*Verse: God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet. (Ps. 46:5)*[^1563]  
Thou hast ascended in glory upon the Mount of Olives, O Christ God, before Thy Disciples, and Thou didst sit at the right hand of the Father, filling all things with Thy Godhead; and Thou didst send unto them the Holy Spirit, enlightening, confirming, and sanctifying our souls.[^1564]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 6:*  
God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet, to lift up the fallen image of Adam, and to send the Spirit, the Comforter, to sanctify our souls.[^1565]

*Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." And the Trisagion.*[^1566]

*At the blessing of the loaves, the Troparion of the Feast, Tone 4: "Thou hast ascended in glory..." (Thrice); and the great reading from the Acts of the Holy Apostles.*[^1567]

*And where there is no All-Night Vigil: after "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart...", the Troparion of the Feast, once.*[^1568]

*At Compline, after "It is truly right...", the Kontakion of the Feast.*[^1569]

*We sing the Midnight Office according to custom with the censer:*[^1570]

*After the first Trisagion, instead of "Behold, the Bridegroom...": we say the Troparion of the Feast.*[^1571]

*After the second Trisagion, instead of "Remember, O Lord, for Thou art good...": we say the Kontakion of the Feast.*[^1572]

*Then: Lord, have mercy (40 times). And the dismissal.*[^1573]

*The prayers: "Remember, O Lord, in hope...": we do not say. The beginning of Matins according to custom.*[^1574]

*And immediately the reader saith: "Come, let us worship..." (Thrice), and the customary two Psalms, 19 and 20, and the Six Psalms.*[^1575]

## ON THURSDAY OF THE ASCENSION, AT MATINS[^1576]

*At "God is the Lord", the Troparion of the Feast: "Thou hast ascended in glory..." (Thrice).*[^1577]

*After the first Stichology, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*  
While the Angels marveled at the strange ascent, and the Disciples were struck with awe at the fearful taking up, Thou didst ascend with glory as God, and the gates were lifted up for Thee, O Savior; therefore the heavenly Powers were astonished, crying aloud: Glory to Thy condescension, O Savior! Glory to Thy Kingdom! Glory to Thine Ascension, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind! *(Twice)*[^1578]

*After the second Stichology, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Awed by the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*  
The pre-eternal and unoriginate God, having deified the human nature which He assumed, mystically ascended today. The Angels, running before, showed Him unto the Apostles going up into the heavens with great glory; and bowing down before Him, they said: Glory to God Who hath ascended! *(Twice)*[^1579]

*Then the Polyeleos. After this, the MEGALYNARION:*[^1580]  
We magnify Thee, O Christ the Giver of life, and we honor Thy divine Ascension into heaven with Thy most pure flesh.

*Verses:*  
1. *Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing. (Ps. 46:1)*[^1581]  
2. *For the Lord Most High is terrible, a great King over all the earth. (Ps. 46:2)*[^1582]  
3. *God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet. (Ps. 46:5)*[^1583]  
4. *Lift up your gates, ye princes, and the King of glory shall enter in. (Ps. 23:7)*[^1584]  
5. *The Lord hath prepared His throne in heaven, and His kingdom rules over all. (Ps. 102:19)*[^1585]  
6. *The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand, until I make Thine enemies the footstool of Thy feet. (Ps. 109:1)*[^1586]  
7. *Be Thou exalted above the heavens, O God, and Thy glory over all the earth. (Ps. 107:5)*[^1587]  
8. *Be Thou exalted, O Lord, in Thy strength; we will sing and praise Thy mighty acts. (Ps. 20:13)*[^1588]  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Alleluia (Thrice).*[^1589]

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-unoriginate Word" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*  
O Thou Who didst descend from heaven unto the earthly realms, and didst raise with Thyself as God the fallen form of Adam lying below in the prison of hades, by Thine Ascension, O Christ, taking it up unto the heavens, Thou didst seat it with Thyself upon the throne of Thy Father, for Thou art merciful and the Lover of mankind! *(Twice)*[^1590]

*And the reading of the Feast: Laudatory homilies, the work of lord Andrew or of others.*[^1591]

*The Anavathmoi: the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*[^1592]

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet. Verse: Clap your hands, all ye nations. Let every breath...*[^1593]

*Gospel according to Mark, pericope 71: "Now when Jesus was risen early the first day of the week..." (Mk. 16:9–20).*[^1594]

*"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ...", once; Psalm 50.*[^1595]

*Glory... Tone 2: "Through the prayers of the Apostles..." Both now... "Through the prayers of the Theotokos..."*[^1596]

*Then the verse: "Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy..." And we sing the present automelic sticheron, Tone 6:*[^1597]  
Today in the heavens, the higher Powers, seeing our nature, marveling at the strange manner of its ascent, were in perplexity, saying one to another: Who is this that comes? But seeing their Master, they commanded the heavenly gates to be lifted up: with them let us ceaselessly hymn Him Who with the flesh is coming again from thence, as the Judge of all and almighty God![^1598]

*CANONS: Tone 5, with the heirmoi to 8; and Tone 4, to 6; the work of John the Monk.*[^1599]  
*Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Unto God the Savior, Who guided the people in the sea with dry feet, and drowned Pharaoh with all his army, unto Him alone let us sing: for He hath been glorified![^1600]

*Troparion:*  
Let all us people sing a song of victory unto Christ, Who ascended in glory upon the shoulders of the Cherubim and seated us at the right hand of the Father: for He hath been glorified![^1601]

*Troparion:*  
Seeing Christ, the Mediator between God and men, in the highest with His flesh, the ranks of Angels were astonished, and with one accord sang a song of victory: for He hath been glorified![^1602]

*Troparion:*  
Unto God Who appeared on Mount Sinai and gave the Law to Moses the God-seer, Who ascended with His flesh from the Mount of Olives, let us all sing: for He hath been glorified![^1603]

*Theotokion:*  
O most pure Mother of God, ceaselessly pray unto God, Who was incarnate of thee and was not separated from the Father’s bosom, to save from every circumstance those whom He created![^1604]

*Another Canon, of lord Joseph, whereof the acrostic is by the alphabet; and in the eighth and ninth odes: Ode of Joseph, that is: the song of Joseph.*[^1605]

*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall utter a word unto the Queen Mother, and I shall be seen radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders.[^1606]

*Troparion [A]:*  
Having risen on the third day, immortal by nature, Thou didst appear unto the Eleven and unto all the Disciples, and Thou didst go up, O Christ, unto the Father, borne upon a cloud, O Fashioner of all![^1607]

*Troparion [B]:*  
Divinely inspired David cries aloud clearly, singing: The Lord is gone up unto the heavenly realms with a shout and the sound of a trumpet, and hath attained unto the Father, the Source of light![^1608]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [G]:*  
Having renewed by Thy Passion and Thy Rising the world grown old in many sins, O Lord, Thou didst ascend, borne upon a cloud unto the supercelestial realms: glory to Thy glory![^1609]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Thou didst give birth unto the Master of all, O all-immaculate Lady, Who accepted a voluntary Passion and went up unto His Father, Whom He did not leave, even though He took flesh.[^1610]

*Katavasia:*  
Covered by the divine darkness, the slow-tongued one set forth the God-inscribed law: for shaking off the mire from the eye of his mind, he sees the Existing One, and is initiated into the knowledge of the Spirit, praising Him with divine songs.[^1611]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
By the power of Thy Cross, O Christ, establish my mind to hymn and glorify Thy saving Ascension.[^1612]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst ascend, O Christ the Giver of life, unto the Father, and didst lift up our race, O Lover of mankind, by Thine ineffable tender mercy.[^1613]

*Troparion:*  
Seeing human nature ascending together with Thee, O Savior, the ranks of Angels ceaselessly hymned Thee in wonder.[^1614]

*Troparion:*  
The angelic choirs were terrified, O Christ, beholding Thee ascended with the body, and they hymned Thy holy Ascension.[^1615]

*Troparion:*  
Human nature fallen into corruption Thou didst raise up, O Christ, and by Thine Ascent Thou didst exalt it, and with Thyself didst glorify us.[^1616]

*Theotokion:*  
Ceaselessly pray, O Pure One, unto Him Who came forth from thy womb, that they who hymn thee as the Mother of God may be delivered from the deceit of the devil.[^1617]

*Another Heirmos:*  
Establish thy choir, O Theotokos, living and abundant Fountain, who have assembled themselves together spiritually, and in thy divine glory account them worthy of crowns of glory.[^1618]

*Troparion [D]:*  
Lift up the heavenly gates! Behold, Christ the King of creation hath come, robed in an earthly body!—said the lower Powers unto the higher.[^1619]

*Troparion [E]:*  
Having sought out Adam who was deceived by the serpent’s deceit, having clothed Thyself in him, O Christ, Thou didst ascend and sit at the right hand as sharing the throne with the Father, while Angels sang unto Thee.[^1620]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [Z]:*  
The earth keeps festival and leaps for joy, and heaven rejoices today at the Ascension of the Creator of creation, Who visibly united things divided by His will.[^1621]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Having given birth to the Destroyer of death, the only immortal God, O Virgin Mother all-immaculate, ceaselessly pray unto Him to slay the passions which slay me, and to save me.[^1622]

*Katavasia:*  
The prayer of the Prophetess Anna of old, bearing a contrite spirit unto the Mighty One and the God of knowledges, broke the bonds of a barren womb and the unbearable reproach of her that was fruitful.[^1623]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*  
Having mounted upon the clouds of heaven, leaving peace unto those on earth, Thou didst ascend and sit at the right hand of the Father, as being consubstantial with Him and with the Spirit: for even though Thou didst appear in the flesh, yet Thou didst remain unchangeable. Therefore Thou awaitest the fulfillment of the end, coming upon earth to judge all the world. O righteous Judge, Lord, spare our souls, granting forgiveness of transgressions as the merciful God unto Thy servants![^1624]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. (The same). And the reading of the Feast.*[^1625]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
I heard the report of the power of the Cross, that paradise was opened thereby, and I cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^1626]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst ascend in glory, O King of Angels, having sent the Comforter unto us from the Father; therefore we cry aloud: Glory to Thine Ascension, O Christ![^1627]

*Troparion:*  
When the Savior went up unto the Father with the flesh, the angelic hosts marveled at Him, and cried aloud: Glory to Thine Ascension, O Christ![^1628]

*Troparion:*  
The angelic Powers cried unto the higher ranks: Lift up the gates for Christ our King, Whom we hymn together with the Father and the Spirit![^1629]

*Theotokion:*  
A Virgin gave birth and knew not a mother's travail; yet she is truly a Mother, and remained a Virgin: whom hymning, we cry aloud: Rejoice, O Theotokos![^1630]

*Another Heirmos:*  
Sitting in glory upon the throne of Godhead, on a light cloud, Jesus the most divine hath come, and by His incorrupt hand hath saved them that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^1631]

*Troparion [H]:*  
Jesus the Giver of life, taking those whom He loved, goes up unto the Mount of Olives and blessed them, and borne upon a cloud, reached the Father’s bosom, which He had in no wise left.[^1632]

*Troparion [Th]:*  
The whole world, visible and invisible, keeps festival: in joy the Angels leap and men ceaselessly glorify the Ascension of Him Who united Himself unto us in the flesh through His goodness.[^1633]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [I]:*  
Having broken the dominion of death as the Immortal One, O Lord, Thou didst bestow immortality upon all, O Lover of mankind, and Thou didst ascend in glory while Thy venerable Disciples beheld Thee, O all-powerful Jesus![^1634]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Blessed was thy womb, O all-immaculate one, for thou wast accounted worthy ineffably to contain Him Who gloriously emptied the womb of hades: pray unto Him to save us who hymn thee.[^1635]

*Katavasia:*  
O King of kings, One from the Only One, Word Who didst proceed from the uncaused Father, Thou hast truly sent unto the Apostles Thine equally powerful Spirit as Benefactor, while they sing: Glory to Thy dominion, O Lord![^1636]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Rising early, we cry unto Thee, O Lord: Save us, for Thou art our God, and we know no other besides Thee.[^1637]

*Troparion:*  
Having filled all things with gladness, O Merciful One, Thou didst come with the flesh unto the heavenly powers.[^1638]

*Troparion:*  
The angelic Powers, seeing Thee taken up, cried: Lift up the gates for our King![^1639]

*Troparion:*  
The Apostles, seeing the Savior lifted up, cried with trembling unto our King: Glory to Thee![^1640]

*Theotokion:*  
We hymn thee as Virgin after childbirth, O Theotokos, for thou didst bear God the Word in the flesh unto the world.[^1641]

*Another Heirmos:*  
All things were amazed at thy divine glory: for thou, O unwedded Virgin, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst bear the timeless Son, granting peace unto all that hymn thee.[^1642]

*Troparion [K]:*  
Having put death to death by Thy Death, O Lord, taking those whom Thou didst love, Thou didst ascend unto the holy Mount of Olives, and from thence Thou didst hasten, O Christ, unto Thy Father, borne upon a cloud.[^1643]

*Troparion [L]:*  
Strange was Thy Nativity, strange was Thy Resurrection, strange and fearful was Thy divine Ascension from the mountain, O Giver of life, which Elijah prefigured when he ascended with a four-horse chariot, hymning Thee, the Lover of mankind.[^1644]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [M]:*  
As the Apostles were looking on, the Angels said: Ye men of Galilee, why marvel ye at the Ascension of Christ the Giver of life? He shall come again upon earth to judge all the world, as the most righteous Judge![^1645]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Having preserved thee an incorrupt Virgin after childbirth, Christ goes up unto the Father, O Birthgiver of God, Whom He did not leave, even though He took from thee an intelligent and animate flesh through Thine ineffable mercy.[^1646]

*Katavasia:*  
Receive the fire-breathing dew of the Spirit as an absolving purification from sins, O radiant children of the Church: for now from Sion hath gone forth the law, the grace of the Spirit in tongues of fire.[^1647]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
The abyss compassed me, the whale became a grave unto me; but I cried unto Thee, the Lover of mankind, and Thy right hand saved me, O Lord.[^1648]

*Troparion:*  
The Apostles leaped for joy, seeing the Creator taken up on high today with the hope of the Spirit, and with fear they cried: Glory to Thine Ascent![^1649]

*Troparion:*  
The Angels stood by, crying unto Thy Disciples, O Christ: In like manner as ye saw Christ ascending with the flesh, He shall come again as the righteous Judge of all![^1650]

*Troparion:*  
When the heavenly Powers saw Thee, our Savior, taken up unto the height with the body, they cried, saying: Great is Thy love for mankind, O Master![^1651]

*Theotokion:*  
We worthily glorify thee, O glorious Mary, praise of the Orthodox, as the unburnt Bush, and the Mountain, and the living Ladder, and the heavenly Gate.[^1652]

*Another Heirmos:*  
Celebrating this divine and all-honorable feast of the Mother of God, come, ye godly-minded, let us clap our hands, and glorify God Who was born of her.[^1653]

*Troparion [N]:*  
Let the clouds drop down eternal gladness upon us from above: for Christ, borne upon a cloud as upon the Cherubim, goes up today unto His Father.[^1654]

*Troparion [X]:*  
Having appeared in the likeness of flesh, Thou didst gather into one what was formerly divided, O Lover of mankind, and Thou didst ascend, O Compassionate One, unto the heavenly realms, while Thy Disciples beheld Thee.[^1655]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [O]:*  
Why are the garments of the flesh of Him Who united Himself to gross matter crimson?—the holy Angels, beholding Christ, said, bearing the divine images of His venerable Passion.[^1656]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
We hymn thy Conception, O Maiden; we hymn thine ineffable Childbirth, whereby we were delivered, O Pure One, from corruption, and afflictions, and the dark prison of hades.[^1657]

*Katavasia:*  
As a purification and salvation for us, O Master Christ, Thou didst shine forth from the Virgin, that as Thou didst snatch the Prophet Jonah from the belly of the sea monster, Thou mightest deliver from corruption all Adam fallen with his whole race.[^1658]

*Kontakion, automelic, Tone 6:*  
Having accomplished the dispensation for our sakes, and united things on earth unto the heavenly, Thou didst ascend in glory, O Christ our God, in no wise being separated, but remaining inseparable, and crying unto them that love Thee: I am with you, and no one is against you![^1659]

*Ikos:*  
Leaving things of the earth upon the earth, letting ashes yield to dust, come, mortals, let us recover ourselves, and let us lift up our eyes and thoughts unto the height; let us wing our sight together with our senses toward the heavenly gates; let us imagine ourselves to be at the Mount of Olives, and to gaze upon the Redeemer borne upon the clouds: for from thence the Lord ascended into heaven, and there He gave loving gifts unto His Apostles, consoling them as a father, and establishing them, guiding them as sons, and saying unto them: I am not separated from you; I am with you, and no one is against you![^1660]

*Synaxarion on the Ascension of the Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ:*[^1661]

*Verses:*  
*Thou didst sit at the right hand of the Father, O Word,*  
*Showing unto the Disciples a most sure faith.*[^1662]

On this same day, on Thursday of the Sixth Week after Pascha, we celebrate the Ascension of our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ. For while He was with the Disciples before the Passion, He promised the coming of the All-Holy Spirit, saying: It is expedient for you that I go away; for if I go not away, the Comforter will not come. And again: When He is come, He will teach you all truth. And after His Resurrection from the dead, appearing unto them during forty days, not continually, but being present separately, eating and drinking with them, and presenting His most certain Resurrection. Finally, having promised many things concerning the Kingdom of God, He commanded them not to depart from Jerusalem, but to remain there, awaiting the coming of the Most Holy Spirit, and to be baptized therewith: for your baptism formerly from John was with water and single, although afterwards Epiphanius of Cyprus related that John the Theologian baptized the Mother of God, and Peter baptized him and the other Apostles. And He commanded them to remain in Jerusalem, so that the preaching of the Gospel might first be confirmed there, lest going to foreign places they might easily be slandered, and as was meet, that as certain soldiers they should from thence be prepared with the weapon of the Spirit, and so go forth to battle against the enemies of Christ. But when the time of the Ascension arrived, He led them out unto the Mount of Olives: and it is so called because it was planted with very many olive trees. And having spoken unto them concerning the preaching unto the end of this age, and also concerning His incomprehensible Kingdom in the age to come, seeing that they were wishing to hold Him back, His all-pure Mother also being there, He appointed Angels for them, showing them His ascent into heaven. And while they looked on, He was taken up from the midst of them, a cloud having received Him; and thus being sent up by the Angels, who commanded one another the lifting up of the heavenly doors, and marveled at His flesh being crimson from the blood, He ascended and sat at the right hand of the Father: having deified the flesh, and—I dare to say—having made it truly divine, whereby we have been reconciled, the ancient enmity being abolished. And Angels in the form of men stood before the Apostles, saying: Ye men of Galilee, why stand ye marveling and gazing into heaven? This Jesus, Whom ye see as God in the flesh, this same shall come again manifestly in the flesh; yet not as before, in poverty and silence, but with great glory, as ye now see Him escorted by Angels. Then the Apostles, leaving off their gazing, returned from the Mount of Olives, which is near Jerusalem, being distant two thousand and forty foot-paces: this is a Sabbath-day’s journey. For so it was ordained by Moses to walk so many paces on the Sabbath, because the Tabernacle of the Covenant also stood at that number of paces distant from the camp of the Jews. For it was permitted for anyone to walk that far on the Sabbath to worship, and not to extend their journey further; for this reason it was called a Sabbath-day’s journey. Hence it seems to some that Christ’s Ascension took place on the Sabbath, but they are mistaken. And having returned, the Apostles entered into the upper room, where they had gathered together with the Myrrh-bearing women and the Mother of the Word, occupying themselves in fasting, and prayers, and supplication, and awaiting the descent of the All-Holy Spirit, according as was the promise.[^1663]

*O Christ God, Who didst ascend in glory, have mercy on us. Amen.*[^1664]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
O Thou Who didst save the hymn-singing youths in the fiery furnace, blessed is the God of our fathers![^1665]

*Troparion:*  
O Thou Who didst ascend upon the clouds of light and didst save the world, blessed is the God of our fathers![^1666]

*Troparion:*  
Having taken upon Thy shoulders our straying nature, O Savior, Thou didst ascend and bring it unto God and the Father.[^1667]

*Troparion:*  
O Thou Who didst ascend in the flesh unto the incorporeal Father, blessed is the God of our fathers![^1668]

*Troparion:*  
Having taken our nature which was slain by sin, Thou didst bring it, O Savior, unto Thine own Father.[^1669]

*Theotokion:*  
O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, whom Thou didst make the Mother of God, blessed is the God of our fathers![^1670]

*Another Heirmos:*  
The godly-minded worshiped not the creature rather than the Creator, but manfully trampling upon the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers, praised above all![^1671]

*Troparion [P]:*  
A radiant cloud received Thee, the radiant Light, O Lord, taken up from earth beyond understanding, and the heavenly Assemblies praised Thee with the Apostles, saying: O God, blessed art Thou![^1672]

*Troparion [R]:*  
Let us all clap our hands rejoicing at the Ascension of Christ, and let us cry aloud: God is gone up with the sound of a trumpet, and sat at the right hand, sharing the throne of the Father unto the ages![^1673]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Troparion [S]:*  
Singing of old, great Moses cried aloud: Let the heavenly Angels worship Christ as He ascends, as King of all, unto Whom we cry: O Lord and God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^1674]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
O most glorious wonders! How didst thou contain the uncontainable God, O thou who art full of divine grace, Who was impoverished in the flesh, and ascended today into the heavens with great glory, and gave life unto men?[^1675]

*Katavasia:*  
The harmonious organ song resounded to venerate the golden-fashioned soulless idol; but the light-bearing grace of the Comforter honors the crying aloud: O Trinity, One, equal in power, unoriginate, blessed art Thou![^1676]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Unto the Son and God, begotten of the Father before the ages, and incarnate in latter times of the Virgin Mother, ye priests hymn, ye people supremely exalt unto all ages![^1677]

*Troparion:*  
Unto Christ the Giver of life, Who soared up in two natures into heaven with glory and sits with the Father, ye priests hymn, ye people supremely exalt unto all ages![^1678]

*Troparion:*  
Unto Thee, O Savior, Who didst deliver creation from the bondage of idols and didst present it free unto Thy Father, we sing, and Thee we supremely exalt unto all ages![^1679]

*Troparion:*  
Unto Him Who by His descent cast down the adversary, and by His ascent lifted up mankind, ye priests hymn, ye people supremely exalt unto all ages![^1680]

*Theotokion:*  
Thou hast appeared higher than the Cherubim, O pure Theotokos, having carried in thy womb Him Who is borne by them: Whom, with the incorporeal beings, we men glorify unto all ages![^1681]

*Another Heirmos:*  
The Childbirth of the Theotokos saved the godly youths in the furnace: then prefigured, but now in act, it moves all the world to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^1682]

*Troparion [T]:*  
The Angels appeared unto the Apostles at the Ascension, and said: Why stand ye gazing in wonder? This One Who ascends unto the heavenly realms shall come again to judge the men who are upon earth, being the only Judge![^1683]

*Troparion [U]:*  
Let us give majesty unto God, harmony of praise; let us shout, let us sing, let us leap for joy, and let us clap our hands: our God is gone up into heaven from earth, while Angels and Archangels hymn Him as Master and Creator of all![^1684]

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*[^1685]

*Troparion [Ph]:*  
Our nature, which of old had fallen away, was lifted up above the Angels, and was seated divinely upon the throne, beyond understanding. Come, let us keep festival, and let us cry aloud: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^1686]

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:* This Thy Son, O Theotokos, having captive taken death by His Cross, is risen on the third day, and appeared unto His Disciples, and ascended unto the heavenly realms; together with them, worshipping thee, we sing and glorify Him unto all ages.[^1687]

*Then: We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord...*[^1688]

*Katavasia:* The thrice-radiant image of the Godhead looses the bonds and bedews the flame; the youths sing, and all created nature blesses the only Savior and Creator as Benefactor.

*We sing not "More honorable", but we sing the refrains:*[^1689]

*Refrain:* Magnify, O my soul, Christ the Giver of life, Who ascended from earth to heaven.

*Ode 9, Tone 5. Heirmos:* Thee, the Mother of God beyond mind and word, who in time ineffably gavest birth unto the Timeless One, we the faithful magnify with one accord.[^1690]

*Troparion:* Beholding Thee, Christ God, the Redeemer of the world, divinely exalted, the Angels leaped with awe and magnified Thee.[^1691]

*Troparion:* Beholding Thy deified flesh on high, O Christ, the Angels said one to another: Truly this is our God.[^1692]

*Troparion:* Beholding Thee taken up upon the clouds, O Christ God, the ranks of the bodiless cried out: O King of glory, lift up the gates.[^1693]

*Troparion:* Thee, Who didst descend even unto the uttermost parts of the earth, and didst save man, and by Thine ascent didst exalt him, we magnify.[^1694]

*Theotokion:* Rejoice, O Theotokos, Mother of Christ God: Whom thou didst bear, beholding Him today ascending from earth with the Angels, thou didst magnify.[^1695]

*Second refrain:* The Angels, beholding the ascension of the Master, were in awe, seeing how He was taken up with glory from earth unto the heights.[^1696]

*Second Canon, Tone 4. Another Heirmos:* Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened by the Spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless Minds feast, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and crying aloud: Rejoice, all-blessed Theotokos, pure Ever-Virgin.[^1697]

*Troparion:* O gifts beyond comprehension! O fearful mystery! For He Who rules over all, departing from earth unto the heavenly realms, sent the Holy Spirit unto the Disciples, Who enlightened their mind and made them fiery by grace.[^1698]

*Troparion:* Ye ranks of Disciples, said the Lord: Tarry ye in Jerusalem, and I will send down unto you another Comforter, enthroned with the Father and equal in honor with Me, even as ye see Me taken up and borne by a radiant cloud.[^1699]

*Glory...*

*Troparion:* The majesty of Him Who became poor in the flesh is manifestly exalted above the heavens, and our fallen nature is honored by sitting with the Father: let us celebrate, and let us all shout in unison, and clap our hands rejoicing.[^1700]

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:* The Light that shone forth from Light, O All-immaculate Lady, shone forth from thee, and destroyed all the blackness of godlessness, and enlightened those sleeping in the night: therefore we all, as is meet, ever bless thee unto the ages.[^1701]

*Katavasia:* Rejoice, O Queen, maternal and virgin glory! For every fluent, eloquent tongue hath not the power to sing thee worthily, and every mind is confounded to comprehend thy Childbearing; therefore with one accord we glorify thee.[^1702]

*Exapostilarion, automelic:* While the Disciples beheld Thee, Thou didst ascend, O Christ, to sit with the Father; the Angels running before cried out: Lift up the gates, lift them up! For the King is gone up unto the primal light of glory. *Thrice.*[^1703]

*At the Praises, "Let every breath praise the Lord", we set 4 verses, and sing stichera: Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^1704]

*Sticheron:* Let us on earth celebrate in angelic fashion, crying aloud a song unto God borne upon the throne of glory: Holy art Thou, O heavenly Father, co-ever-existing Word; holy art Thou also, O Most Holy Spirit.[^1705]

*Sticheron:* The commanders of the Angels, beholding the strange ascent, O Savior, were perplexed, saying one to another: What is this sight? For a man indeed visible in form, as God, ascends above the heavens with flesh.[^1706]

*Sticheron:* The Galileans, beholding Thee ascended with the body from the Mount of Olives, O Word, heard the Angels crying unto them: Why stand ye gazing? This same Jesus shall come again with flesh in like manner as ye have seen Him.[^1707]

*Repeating the first sticheron.*[^1708]

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*

*Doxastikon:* Thou wast born as Thou Thyself didst desire; Thou didst appear as Thou Thyself didst will; Thou didst suffer in the flesh, O our God; from the dead Thou didst rise, having trampled down death; Thou didst ascend in glory, filling all things, and Thou hast sent unto us the Divine Spirit, that we may hymn and glorify Thy Godhead.[^1709]

*The Great Doxology. Holy oil is also distributed to the brethren. And we read the catechesis of the Studite. And the First Hour in the narthex. And the complete dismissal.*[^1710]

*At the Divine Liturgy, we sing the following Antiphons, Psalm 46 LXX, Tone 2:*[^1711]

*Antiphon 1:* Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing.[^1712]

*Refrain:* Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Verse 2:* For the Lord Most High is terrible, a great King over all the earth.[^1713]

*Refrain:* Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Verse 3:* He subdued peoples under us, and nations under our feet.[^1714]

*Refrain:* Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Verse 4:* God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet.[^1715]

*Refrain:* Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Glory... Both now...*[^1716]

*Refrain:* Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Antiphon 2, Psalm 47 LXX, Tone 2:*[^1717]

*Verse 1:* Great is the Lord, and exceedingly to be praised, in the city of our God, in His holy mountain.[^1718]

*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who didst ascend in glory, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia.

*Verse 2:* Mount Zion on the sides of the north, the city of the great King.[^1719]

*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God...

*Verse 3:* God is known in her palaces, when He defends her.[^1720]

*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who didst ascend...

*Verse 4:* For, lo, the kings of the earth assembled, they came together.[^1721]

*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who didst ascend...

*Glory... Both now... Only-begotten Son...*[^1722]

*Antiphon 3, Psalm 48 LXX, Tone 4:*[^1723]

*Verse 1:* Hear these things, all ye nations; give ear, all ye inhabitants of the world.[^1724]

*Troparion, Tone 4:* Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God, having gladdened Thy Disciples by the promise of the Holy Spirit, when they were confirmed by the blessing: for Thou art the Son of God, the Redeemer of the world.[^1725]

*Verse 2:* Both the earthborn and the sons of men, the rich and the poor together.[^1726]

*Troparion:* Thou hast ascended in glory...

*Verse 3:* My mouth shall speak wisdom, and the meditation of my heart understanding.[^1727]

*Troparion:* Thou hast ascended in glory...

*Verse 4:* I will incline mine ear unto a parable; I will open my riddle upon the harp.[^1728]

*Troparion:* Thou hast ascended...

*Introit:* God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet.[^1729]

*Troparion:* Thou hast ascended... *Glory... Both now... Kontakion:* Having accomplished for us the dispensation, and united the earthly with the heavenly, Thou didst ascend in glory, O Christ our God, in no wise parting thence, but remaining inseparable, and crying unto them that love Thee: I am with you, and no one can be against you! *Then the Trisagion:*[^1730]

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:* Be Thou exalted above the heavens, O God, and Thy glory over all the earth.[^1731]

*Verse:* Ready is my heart, O God, ready is my heart; I will chant and sing in my glory.

*Epistle in the Acts, pericope 1 (Acts 1:1–12):* The former treatise have I made, O Theophilus...[^1732]

*Alleluia, Tone 2:* God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet.[^1733]

*Verse:* Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing.

*Gospel of Luke, pericope 121 (Lk. 24:36–53):* Jesus, having risen from the dead, stood in the midst of His disciples... *In place of "It is truly right", until the Apodosis we sing the Heirmos: Thee, the Mother of God beyond mind...*[^1734]

*Communion Verse:* God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet. Alleluia, thrice.[^1735]

*At the refectory, great consolation for the brethren.*[^1736]

## ON THURSDAY OF THE ASCENSION, AT VESPERS[^1737]

*We say not a kathisma. But if anywhere an All-Night Vigil did not take place, we say the appointed kathisma.*[^1738]

*On "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 verses, and sing automelic stichera of the feast, 3: Tone 1:*[^1739]

*Sticheron 1, Automelon:* Having ascended into heaven, whence Thou also didst descend, leave us not orphans, O Lord: let Thy Spirit come, bringing peace unto the world; show unto the sons of men the works of Thy power, O Lord, Lover of mankind.[^1740]

*Sticheron 2:* Thou didst ascend, O Christ, unto Thine unoriginate Father, not departing from His uncircumscribed bosom; and the heavenly hosts received no addition to the Thrice-Holy praise, but recognized Thee as the one Son even after Thine incarnation, O Lord, only-begotten of the Father: in the multitude of Thy tender mercies, have mercy on us.[^1741]

*Sticheron 3:* Thine Angels, O Lord, said unto the Apostles: Ye men of Galilee, why stand ye gazing into heaven? This is Christ God Who ascended from you into heaven; He shall come again in like manner as ye have seen Him going into heaven: serve Him in holiness and righteousness.[^1742]

*And from the Menaion, 3. Glory of the Saint, if there be one. But if not:*

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*

*Doxastikon:* Thou wast born as Thou Thyself didst desire; Thou didst appear as Thou Thyself didst will; Thou didst suffer in the flesh, O our God; from the dead Thou didst rise, having trampled down death; Thou didst ascend in glory, filling all things, and Thou hast sent unto us the Divine Spirit, that we may hymn and glorify Thy Godhead.[^1743]

*Entrance. O Joyful Light.*

*Great Prokeimenon, Tone 7:* Our God is in heaven and on earth; all whatsoever He hath willed, He hath done.[^1744]

*Verse 1:* In the exodus of Israel from Egypt, of the house of Jacob from a barbarous people.

*Verse 2:* The sea saw and fled; Jordan was driven back.

*Verse 3:* What ailed thee, O sea, that thou didst flee? and thou, Jordan, that thou wast driven back?

*And again:* Our God is in heaven and on earth; all whatsoever He hath willed, He hath done.

*Litanies: Let us all say... and Let us complete our evening prayer...*[^1745]

*At the Aposticha, stichera: Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^1746]

*Sticheron 1:* He Who fills all things, having ascended Mount Olivet, saith unto the Disciples: O My friends, the time of My departure is come; go ye and teach the nations the word which ye have heard from My voice. Then Thou didst ascend in glory as upon a chariot; therefore the Apostles were struck with awe in trembling.[^1747]

*Verse:* Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing.[^1748]

*Sticheron 2:* When Thy Mother and the Disciples walked together to behold Thee, O Christ, Thou didst lift up Thy hands to bless them; and while Thou wast blessing them, a cloud of light suddenly took Thee from their eyes; then Thou didst ascend in glory and wast truly revealed sitting at the right hand, adored together with the Father.[^1749]

*Verse:* God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet.[^1750]

*Sticheron 3:* Come, ye faithful, let us ascend together unto the high Mount of Olives, ascending with the Apostles; and having lifted up our hearts and thoughts on high, let us behold the Lord now borne aloft, and rejoicing thankfully let us cry unto Him: Glory to Thine Ascension, O Thou Plenteous in mercy![^1751]

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*

*Doxastikon:* Beholding Thine Ascension upon the holy mountains, O Christ, Radiance of the Father's glory, we hymn the light-bearing aspect of Thy countenance; we venerate Thy Passions, we honor Thy Resurrection, and glorifying Thy glorious Ascension: have mercy on us.[^1752]

*Troparion of the feast: Thou hast ascended in glory... And the dismissal.*

*The service of the Menaion for the Saint of the day falling on the feast of the Ascension we sing at Compline.*[^1753]

*[Woodcut engraving of the Ascension of our Lord Jesus Christ in glory above the Mount of Olives.]*[^1754]

## ON FRIDAY AFTER THE ASCENSION, AT MATINS[^1755]

*At "God is the Lord", the Troparion of the Feast.*

*After the first stichology, Sessional Hymn: Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*

Having gone up unto the Mount of Olives, O merciful Jesus, from thence Thou wast taken up: for a radiant cloud lifted Thee up from the earth, while Thy Disciples beheld a fearful marvel, and the bodiless higher hosts in the heavens cried aloud: Lift up your gates with fear! Together with them, all creation praises Thee as King and God of all.[^1756]

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn: Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости сло́ва):*

Having ascended upon the clouds of heaven, leaving peace unto those upon earth, Thou didst ascend and sit at the right hand of the Father, as being consubstantial with Him and with the Spirit; for though Thou didst appear in the flesh, yet Thou didst remain unchangeable; therefore Thou awaitest the consummation of the end, coming to judge all the world upon earth. O Righteous Judge and Lord, spare our souls, granting remission of sins, as a merciful God unto Thy servants.[^1757]

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*And Psalm 50. The Canon of the Feast of the Ascension: the heirmos twice; the troparia to 6, alternating one by one on each day; and of the Menaion to 4. After Ode 3, the Kontakion of the Saint, and the Ikos, and his Sessional Hymn. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. After Ode 6, the Kontakion of the Feast and the Ikos. After Ode 9, the Exapostilarion of the Feast. Glory... of the Saint, if there be one, and Both now... of the Feast. But if not, Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*[^1758]

*At the Aposticha, stichera of the feast: Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^1759]

*Sticheron 1:* O new and seemly marvel! For human nature is gone up into heaven, having been united unto the Word, God the Almighty.[^1760]

*Verse:* Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing.[^1761]

*Sticheron 2:* The bright and all-radiant day of the Master hath shone forth, the day of His divine Ascension into heaven, enlightening all things.[^1762]

*Verse:* God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet.[^1763]

*Sticheron 3:* Even as unto the Disciples Thou didst send down from on high Thy Spirit that heals hearts, so unto Thy people, O Christ Savior, send down Thy grace.[^1764]

*Glory... Both now... Tone 5:*

*Doxastikon:* O Lord, when Thou wast taken up whence Thou hadst not departed, the hosts of Angels and of all the bodiless with joy cried aloud unto the celestial Powers: Lift up your gates, ye princes, and the King of glory shall enter in! And the Cherubic Throne received Thee with flesh; O Lord, glory to Thee![^1765]

*And the rest of Matins, and the dismissal.*[^1766]

*On Friday after the Ascension, the Three-Ode Canon, Tone 4:*[^1767]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ; but we, waking early out of the night, hymn Thee, O Only-begotten, the Radiance of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Lover of mankind.[^1768]

*Troparion:* Ascending from earth unto heaven, Thou wast not separated from the Father, and united as Spirit, Whom Thou didst send from heaven unto the Disciples as Comforter.[^1769]

*Troparion:* The ranks of the bodiless, when they saw Thee, O Jesus, ascended into heaven with the flesh, rejoicing marvelled at Thy great condescension unto men, O Lord.[^1770]

*Glory...*

*Troparion:* Our corrupted nature, O Master, Thou didst seat and adorn upon the Father's throne, O Lord, with which Thou shalt come to judge the world, O Almighty One.[^1771]

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:* Let us mentally honor the pure and all-immaculate one, the beauty of Jacob, and adorn her with divine deeds, singing piously, as the Mother of our God.[^1772]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* O Redeemer of all, Almighty One, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew the pious youths, and didst teach them to sing: All ye works, bless and praise the Lord.[^1773]

*Troparion:* Borne upon the Cherubic shoulders, Thou didst ascend in glory while Thy Disciples beheld Thee, O Lord, and cried unceasingly: All ye works of the Lord, praise the Lord and exalt Him above all unto the ages.[^1774]

*Troparion:* Having broken down the enmity of the middle wall of partition, Thou didst reconcile those who were dead unto Thy Father, and having ascended, Thou didst draw up together with Thyself the flesh which Thou hadst assumed, O unoriginate Word, higher than Principalities and Powers.[^1775]

*Glory...*

*Troparion:* Having blessed the Disciples, Thou wast taken up on high, O Christ, sending them the Comforter, while they sang and cried with one accord: All ye works of the Lord, praise the Lord and exalt Him above all unto the ages.[^1776]

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:* The common Propitiation of the faithful, the ready Help for mankind, and the Bridge unto the Creator leading all unto salvation, the Mother of God, we glorify.[^1777]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Eve indeed, through the affliction of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Theotokos, through the sprout of thy childbearing, didst cause blessing to blossom for the world; therefore we all magnify thee.[^1778]

*Troparion:* From the Mount of Olives, O Christ, a cloud received Thee as a throne, while all Thy divine Disciples beheld, and in trembling cried unto Thee: Leave not orphans those whom Thou hast loved in Thine ineffable compassion.[^1779]

*Troparion:* Thou didst descend at first without flesh, but Thou didst ascend bearing the flesh which Thou hadst assumed; and with fear the heavenly gates were opened unto Thee, and the heavenly Minds, beholding, marvelled, saying: Glory to Thine ineffable dispensation, O Master.[^1780]

*Glory...*

*Troparion:* Why stand ye gazing at the fearful ascension? said the Angels of old unto the divine Apostles: In like manner as ye see Him ascending, He shall come again upon the earth to judge all with ineffable glory.[^1781]

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:* Being present with the divine Disciples, O All-pure One, and beholding Him Whom thou didst bear being lifted up from the earth, thou didst cry aloud: Thou ascendest unto Thine unoriginate Father, filling all things with Thy glory, O only most-glorified Lord.[^1782]

## ON FRIDAY AFTER THE ASCENSION, AT VESPERS[^1783]

*On "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 verses: of the feast, 3; and of the Menaion, 3. Stichera of the feast: Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^1784]

*Sticheron 1:* Thou didst suffer as man, being impassible in Thy Godhead, and Thou didst rise on the third day, having captured death and raised together all who had decayed; and Thou didst ascend, O Christ, unto the Father, having promised to send the Comforter unto Thy sacred Apostles, O Jesus Almighty, Savior of our souls.[^1785]

*Sticheron 2:* Why stand ye gazing into heaven? said the Angels seen as men unto the initiates of the Word: This Jesus Whom ye behold ascending in a radiant cloud, the same shall come in like manner as ye have seen Him, to judge the world, even as He said: Go ye therefore, and do all things that have been commanded.[^1786]

*Sticheron 3:* After Thy Rising from the grave beyond comprehension, O Almighty Lord, having taken those whom Thou didst love, Thou didst lead them out as far as Bethany, O Word, and having come unto the Mount of Olives, Thou didst bless them, and so didst ascend, while Angels were subject unto Thee, O Jesus Almighty, Savior of our souls.[^1787]

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*

*Doxastikon:* Today in the heavens the celestial Powers, beholding our nature, marvelled at the strange manner of Thine ascent, and were in doubt, saying one to another: Who is this that is come? But seeing their Master, they commanded the heavenly gates to be lifted up; with them let us unceasingly hymn Him Who shall come again with flesh from thence as Judge of all and almighty God.[^1788]

*At the Aposticha, stichera: Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^1789]

*Sticheron 1:* Having fulfilled the Father's good pleasure, O God, and united the heavenly things with the earthly, Thou didst ascend in glory unto the primal heights.[^1790]

*Verse:* Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing.[^1791]

*Sticheron 2:* Thou didst ascend unto Thy Begetter, O Compassionate One, whence Thou hadst not departed, and Thou didst exalt our nature that lay below, O Master.[^1792]

*Verse:* God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet.[^1793]

*Sticheron 3:* A cloud of light received Thee on high, and the Angels with fear and trembling ministered unto Thy divine Ascension.[^1794]

*Glory... Both now... Tone 7:*

*Doxastikon:* Thou didst come unto the Mount of Olives, having mercy on the human race; and a cloud lifted Thee up from the eyes of Thy Disciples, who trembled indeed at the sight, yet rejoiced in the expectation of the Holy Spirit, by Whom having confirmed them, O Savior, have mercy on us.[^1795]

## ON SATURDAY AFTER THE ASCENSION, AT MATINS[^1796]

*At "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, thrice.*[^1797]

*After the first stichology, Sessional Hymn:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*  
While the Angels marveled at the strange ascent, and the Disciples were struck with awe at the fearful taking up, Thou didst ascend in glory as God, and the gates were lifted up before Thee, O Savior. Therefore the heavenly Powers marveled, crying aloud: Glory to Thy condescension, O Savior! Glory to Thy kingdom! Glory to Thy Ascension, O only Lover of mankind![^1798]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*  
Hades having been despoiled by Thee, O Giver of life, and the world having been enlightened at Thy Resurrection, Thou didst ascend in glory, O Savior, Who holdest all things in Thy hand. Therefore with the Angels we glorify Thee, O Almighty Lord: Glory to Thy Ascension, O Christ! Glory to Thy kingdom! Glory to Thy compassions, O only Lover of mankind![^1799]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*And Psalm 50. The Canon of the Feast with its heirmoi to 8, and of the Menaion to 4. Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Feast.*[^1800]

*At the Aposticha, stichera.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
I will not leave you orphans, whom I have gathered, the Lord said unto His friends: but I will send unto you the Holy Spirit.[^1801]

*Verse: Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing.* (Ps. 46:1 LXX)[^1802]  
The Angels cried aloud unto the all-wise Apostles: O ye men of Galilee, in like manner as ye behold Him, He shall come again.[^1803]

*Verse: God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet.* (Ps. 46:5 LXX)[^1804]  
Thy Disciples, O Word, came down with joy from the Mount of Olives, glorifying and hymnodying Thy divine Ascension.[^1805]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 8:*  
Thou didst ascend in glory from earth unto the heavens, filling all things with Thy Godhead, and Thou didst sit at the right hand of the Father, Who art from the beginning God the Word; therefore the heavenly Powers, beholding, said with awe unto the Apostles: Whom do ye look for, gazing into heaven? This One, Whom ye have seen, He shall come again with glory, to judge the whole universe, and to reward each according to his works; unto Whom let us cry aloud: Incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee![^1806]

*And the usual dismissal.*[^1807]

*On Saturday after the Ascension, the Four-Ode Canon.*  
*Tone 8, Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my sorrows; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^1808]

*Troparion:*  
Having filled the earth with Thy glory, Thou didst ascend with ineffable glory, and didst bring unto the Father the flesh wherein for our sake out of tender compassion Thou didst clothe Thyself; and the heavenly Powers cried aloud unto Thee, O only Lover of mankind.[^1809]

*Troparion:*  
Be ye lifted up, O heavenly gates, and receive God the Flesh-bearer! The spiritual Powers cried aloud unto those who asked: Who is this of Whom ye speak, and unto Whom as Creator of all things ye unceasingly offer praise?[^1810]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*  
*Troparion:*  
Beholding the fearful ascent of the Word, the Disciples cried aloud: Send down upon us the Most Holy Spirit, and leave not as orphans Thy servants who know Thee to be true God and sinless Man.[^1811]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:*  
Thou hast deified human nature, being ineffably born of the Virgin, O consubstantial Word of the Father, and hast ascended with glory: grant peace unto Thy Disciples, and unto the world rich mercy.[^1812]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The Hebrew children boldly trod down the flame in the furnace, and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^1813]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst endure passion and death, and didst arise on the third day as Thou saidst, and didst ascend unto the heavens, while Thy Disciples sing unto Thee: O Lord God, unto the ages![^1814]

*Troparion:*
