Send down upon us the Most Holy Spirit, as Thou didst promise, O Compassionate One! The wise Disciples cried aloud: He Who makes wise and gives understanding unto all the faithful unto the ages.[^1815]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*  
*Troparion:*  
Clap your hands, all ye nations, shout unto God with rejoicing: Christ hath ascended, and sits in glory at the right hand, co-enthroned with the Father, unto the ages.[^1816]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:*  
Without emptying the Father's bosom, the Word was incarnate in thy womb, delivering us from unreason, O Virgin, who sing unto the Lord God unto the ages.[^1817]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
The Chaldean tyrant furiously heated the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher Power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye peoples, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^1818]

*Troparion:*  
Having been configured, as it is written, beyond mind in what was alien, and by His Passions visibly granting impassibility unto all the faithful, He ascends from the earth now to appear before the Father's face, while Angels, Archangels, Thrones, and Dominions glorify Him unto all ages.[^1819]

*Troparion:*  
Angels in white array stood before the Apostles, who were at a loss before the new vision of Thy Ascension, O Word co-unoriginate with the Father, saying: Why do ye gaze, thus wondering, into the heights? This same Jesus shall come again to judge the world, in like manner as ye behold Him.[^1820]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*  
*Troparion:*  
Having accomplished all things, O Lord, as immutable God, Thou didst ascend unto the heavens with the flesh, while Angelic Powers submit unto Thee, and the Disciples with joy glorify Thee, and with fear sing: Ye priests, hymn; ye peoples, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^1821]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:*  
O All-pure Mother of God, cleanse the wounds of my soul and the sores of my sin, pouring forth the fountain from the ribs of Thy Childbearing, and cleansing them with the streams that flow therefrom: for unto thee do I cry, and unto thee do I fall down, and thee do I invoke, the only Savior-protectress.[^1822]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Every hearing was struck with awe at the ineffable condescension of God, how the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, having become man from a Virgin womb: therefore we faithful magnify the all-pure Theotokos.[^1823]

*Troparion:*  
Lift up the spiritual gates, O heavenly Powers: our King hath come, having assumed mortal substance, and He, being God and man, shall enter unto His Father, having wrought fearful and most glorious things.[^1824]

*Troparion:*  
Sing all with understanding, shout unto God Who hath done most glorious works, and Who hath ascended unto the heavens, and hath sent down the Holy Spirit unto the Apostles, enlightening us who glorify His Godhead.[^1825]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*  
*Troparion:*  
Hades was destroyed by Thy Burial, and resurrection was granted unto men by Thy Arising, and the earth was illumined, and the heavens by Thy Ascension, O Christ, only immutable and almighty One, Giver of good things, and our Savior.[^1826]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:*  
The greeting of Gabriel with all the Angels do we offer unto thee: Rejoice, longed-for Palace of the King! Rejoice, thou who hast united earthly things with the heavenly: for whose sake we are saved, knowing thee to be the pure Mother of God.[^1827]

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes: Ode 3 of the feast, to 6.*[^1828]  
*Prokeimenon of the feast:*  
*Be Thou exalted above the heavens, O God... (Ps. 56:5, 11 LXX [Ps. 57:5, 11])*[^1829]  
*Epistle from the Acts, pericope 33: On the first day of the week, when the disciples came together... (Acts 20:7–12).*[^1830]  
*Alleluia of the feast.*[^1831]  
*Gospel from John, pericope 48: The words that I speak unto you... (Jn. 14:10–21).*[^1832]  
*Communion verse of the feast.*[^1833]

## SEVENTH SUNDAY AFTER PASCHA, OF THE HOLY AND GOD-BEARING THREE HUNDRED AND EIGHTEEN FATHERS WHO ASSEMBLED IN NICAEA[^1834]

*On Saturday evening, at Small Vespers.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 4 verses, and we sing Resurrection stichera:*  
*Tone 6:*  
*Holding victory, O Christ...*  
*Today Christ, having trampled down death...*  
*Unto Thee, O Lord, Who art throughout all creation...*  
*Repeating the first.*  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone the same:*  
*Today the mystical trumpets of the Spirit...*  
*(See all these at Great Vespers.)*[^1835]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
It is truly meet to bless thee, the Theotokos: for the Creator of all, having entered into thy most pure womb, became flesh, not being altered in nature, nor imagining in His dispensation, but uniting Himself hypostatically unto the rationally ensouled flesh received from thee; therefore piously in both natures do we acknowledge the distinction of what is manifested. Him entreat, O pure and all-holy Lady, to send down upon us peace and great mercy.[^1836]

*At the Aposticha, Resurrection sticheron:*  
*Tone 6:*  
Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior, the Angels hymn in the heavens: and make us worthy on earth to glorify Thee with a pure heart.[^1837]

*Then the present prosomoia stichera of the Most Holy Theotokos.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*  
*Verse: I will remember thy name in every generation and generation.* (Ps. 44:18 LXX [Ps. 45:17])  
That which God, Who is praised above all, of old promised with an oath unto thy forefather, He accomplished in these latter times, coming forth, O All-pure One, from thy divine womb; for truly the Lord Who holds the ends of the earth in His palm took flesh from thee: Whom make propitious unto me in the hour of judgment, O blessed Lady, that I may obtain His kingdom, through the exaltation of virtues and the mortification of passions.[^1838]

*Verse: Hear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear.* (Ps. 44:11 LXX [Ps. 45:10])  
By purity of mind, Isaiah of old foretold thee, O Virgin, that thou shouldest give birth unto the Creator of all creation, O pure and all-immaculate Lady: for thou alone hast been shown forth all-blameless throughout the ages; therefore I entreat thee: cleanse my defiled soul, and show me forth a partaker of the Divine brightness, and of standing at the right hand of thy Son, when He shall sit, as it is written, to judge the whole world.[^1839]

*Verse: The rich among the people shall entreat thy face.* (Ps. 44:13 LXX [Ps. 45:12])  
The dissolution of death appeared through thy Childbearing, for thou art, O Maiden, the dwelling-place of never-failing Life; therefore I entreat thee: raise me up who lie in the places of the evils of my passions, and guide me, O Virgin, unto rejoicing and quickening; and vouchsafe me the blessed recompense and the eternal and divine joy, where is ever-existing sweetness, where is never-waning light.[^1840]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 4:*  
*The supplicatory memory:*  
*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Of the feast:*  
*O Lord, the mystery:*  
*(See the Doxastika at the Aposticha of Great Vespers.)*[^1841]  
*Resurrection Troparion. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Troparion of the feast. And the litany and dismissal.*[^1842]

## AT GREAT VESPERS[^1843]

*Blessed is the man (Kathisma 1).*[^1844]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses. And we sing 3 Resurrection stichera, 3 of the Ascension, and 4 of the Fathers.*[^1845]

*Resurrection stichera, Tone 6:*  
Holding victory over hades, O Christ, Thou didst ascend upon the Cross, that Thou mightest raise up with Thyself those who sat in the darkness of death, Thou Who art free among the dead, pouring forth life from Thine own light: O Almighty Savior, have mercy on us![^1846]

Today Christ, having trampled down death as He said, arose, and granted rejoicing unto the world, that all crying aloud might say this song: O Fountain of life, unapproachable Light, Almighty Savior, have mercy on us![^1847]

Whither shall we sinners flee from Thee, O Lord, Who art throughout all creation? In heaven Thou dwellest; in hades Thou hast trampled down death; into the depths of the sea? There is Thy hand, O Master! Unto Thee do we flee, falling down before Thee we pray: O Thou Who didst rise from the dead, have mercy on us![^1848]

*Other Idiomelon stichera of the Ascension, the same Tone:*  
The Lord ascended unto the heavens, that He might send the Comforter unto the world; the heavens prepared His throne, the clouds His ascent; the Angels marvel, beholding a man higher than themselves; the Father awaits Him Whom He hath co-eternal in His bosom; and the Holy Spirit commands all His Angels: Lift up your gates, O ye princes; clap your hands, all ye nations, for Christ is gone up where He was before![^1849]

O Lord, at Thy Ascension the Cherubim were astonished, beholding Thee, God, ascending upon the clouds, sitting upon them; and we glorify Thee, for Thy mercy is good: glory to Thee![^1850]

Beholding Thy Ascension upon the holy mountains, O Christ, the Radiance of the Father's glory, we hymn the light-fashioned appearance of Thy countenance; we bow down in worship before Thy Passions, we honor Thy Resurrection, glorifying Thy glorious Ascension: have mercy on us![^1851]

*Other stichera of the Holy Fathers.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Despairing on account of her life" (Ѿча́ѧннаѧ):*  
Thou wast born from the womb before the morning star, motherless of the Father before the ages (Ps. 109:3 LXX): even though Arius glorifies Thee as a creature and not God, foolishly and daringly confounding Thee the Creator with creatures, treasuring up for himself the fuel of eternal fire; but the council which assembled in Nicaea proclaimed Thee to be the Son of God, O Lord, co-enthroned with the Father and the Spirit.[^1852]

Who tore Thy garment, O Savior? Arius, Thou hast said, who cut the equally honored dominion of the Trinity into divisions: he denied Thee to be One of the Trinity; he also teaches Nestorius not to say "Theotokos": but the council which assembled in Nicaea proclaimed Thee to be the Son of God, O Lord, co-enthroned with the Father and the Spirit.[^1853]

Into the precipice of sin falls Arius, having hated to see the light, and by a divine hook is torn asunder in his bowels, forcibly pouring out all his substance and his soul, becoming another Judas in character and in manner: but the council which assembled in Nicaea proclaimed Thee to be the Son of God, O Lord, co-enthroned with the Father and the Spirit.[^1854]

The foolish Arius cut the monarchia of the Most Holy Trinity into three unlawful and unjoined substances; therefore the God-bearing Fathers, coming together zealously, kindled with zeal like Elijah the Tishbite, with the spiritual sword cut down the blasphemer who taught shame, even as the Spirit spoke.[^1855]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 6:*  
*Doxastikon:*  
Today let us praise the mystical trumpets of the Spirit, the God-bearing Fathers, who sang in the midst of the Church a harmonious song of theology: one unchanging Trinity, one substance and Godhead; the overthrowers of Arius and champions of the Orthodox, who ever pray unto the Lord that our souls may be saved.[^1856]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Dogmatic Theotokion, Tone 6:*  
Who would not call thee blessed, O All-holy Virgin? Who would not sing the praise of thy childbearing without travail? For the only-begotten Son Who shone forth timelessly from the Father, the same came forth from thee, pure Lady, having become ineffably incarnate, being God by nature, and having become man by nature for our sake; not divided into two persons, but recognized in two natures without confusion: Him entreat, O pure and all-blessed Lady, that our souls may be saved.[^1857]

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day: The Lord is King (Ps. 92:1 LXX). And the Readings.*[^1858]

*The Reading from Genesis (14:14–20):*  
When Abram heard that his nephew Lot had been taken captive, he numbered his household servants, three hundred and eighteen, and pursued after them as far as Dan. And he fell upon them by night, he and his servants with him; and he smote them, and pursued them unto Hobah, which is on the left of Damascus. And he brought back all the horses of Sodom, and he brought back Lot his nephew, and all his goods, and the women, and the people. And the king of Sodom went out to meet him, after he had returned from the slaughter of Chedorlaomer and of the kings who were with him, unto the valley of Shaveh; this was the king's vale. And Melchizedek king of Salem brought forth bread and wine; and he was priest of the Most High God. And he blessed Abram, and said: Blessed be Abram of the Most High God, Who created heaven and earth; and blessed be the Most High God, Who hath delivered thine enemies into thy hands.[^1859]

*The Reading from Deuteronomy (1:8–11, 15–17):*  
In those days, Moses said unto the children of Israel: Behold, I have delivered the land before your face; enter in and inherit the land which the Lord sware unto your fathers, to Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob, to give it unto them and to their seed after them. And I spoke unto you at that time, saying: I am not able to bear you alone. The Lord our God hath multiplied you, and behold, ye are this day as the stars of heaven in multitude. The Lord God of our fathers make you a thousand times so many more as ye are, and bless you, as He hath promised you! And I took of you wise men, and known, and understanding, and I set them to rule over you, rulers over thousands, and rulers over hundreds, and rulers over fifties, and rulers over tens,[^1860] and officers among your tribes, as judges for you. And I commanded your judges at that time, saying: Hear the causes between your brethren, and judge righteously between every man and his brother, and the stranger that is with him. Ye shall not respect persons in judgment; ye shall judge the small and the great alike; ye shall not be afraid of the face of man, for the judgment is God's.[^1861]

*The Reading from Deuteronomy (10:14–21):*  
In those days, Moses said unto the children of Israel: Behold, the heaven and the heaven of heavens belongs unto the Lord thy God, the earth also, with all that therein is. Yet the Lord had a delight in your fathers to love them, and He chose their seed after them, even you above all nations, as at this day. Circumcise therefore the hardness of your heart, and be no more stiff-necked. For the Lord our God is God of gods, and Lord of lords, a great God, a mighty, and a terrible, Who regards not persons, nor accepts bribes: He executes the judgment of the stranger, the fatherless, and the widow, and loves the stranger, in giving him food and raiment. Love ye therefore the stranger: for ye were strangers in the land of Egypt. Thou shalt fear the Lord thy God; Him shalt thou serve as His servant, and to Him shalt thou cleave, and swear by His Name. He is thy praise, and He is thy God, Who hath done for thee these great and glorious things, which thine eyes have seen.[^1862]

*At the Litiya, sticheron of the feast.*  
*Tone 1:*  
Having ascended into the heavens, whence also Thou didst descend, leave us not orphans, O Lord: let Thy Spirit come, bearing peace unto the world; show forth unto the sons of men the works of Thy power, O Lord, Lover of mankind![^1863]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Of the Fathers, Tone 3:*  
True keepers of the apostolic traditions were ye, O holy Fathers: for having piously taught the consubstantiality of the Holy Trinity, ye synodically cast down the blasphemy of Arius; and with him having refuted Macedonius the fighter against the Spirit, ye condemned Nestorius, Eutyches, and Dioscorus, and Sabellius, and Severus the Acephalite: pray that we may be delivered from their delusion, that our life may be preserved undefiled in the faith, we entreat you.[^1864]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Of the feast, Tone 6:*  
O Lord, having fulfilled the mystery of Thy dispensation, taking Thy Disciples unto the Mount of Olives, Thou didst ascend, and lo, Thou didst pass through the firmament of heaven, Thou Who for my sake didst become poor as I am; and having ascended whence Thou wast not parted: send down Thy Most Holy Spirit, enlightening our souls.[^1865]

*At the Aposticha, stichera.*  
*Tone 6:*  
Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior, the Angels hymn in the heavens: and make us worthy on earth to glorify Thee with a pure heart.[^1866]

*Other stichera according to the Alphabet.*  
*Verse: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty.* (Ps. 92:1 LXX)  
Having shattered the brazen gates, and broken the evil bars, as almighty God Thou didst raise up the fallen human race; therefore we also cry aloud with one accord: O Thou Who didst rise from the dead, Lord, glory to Thee![^1867]

*Verse: For He hath established the world, which shall not be moved.* (Ps. 92:1 LXX)  
Desiring to set right our ancient corruption, Christ was nailed unto the Cross, and was laid in a tomb: Whom the Myrrh-bearing women sought with tears, and lamenting said: Woe unto us! O Savior of all, how didst Thou consent to dwell in a tomb? And having dwelt therein willingly, how wast Thou stolen away? How wast Thou moved? What place hath concealed Thy life-bearing Body? But O Master, as Thou didst promise, appear unto us, and stay from us the lamentation of tears! And while they were weeping, an Angel cried aloud unto them: Ceasing from tears, say unto the Apostles: The Lord is risen, granting purification and great mercy unto the world![^1868]

*Verse: Holiness befits Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days.* (Ps. 92:5 LXX)  
Crucified as Thou didst will, O Christ, and having captive taken death by Thy Burial, Thou didst rise on the third day as God with glory, granting unto the world endless life, and great mercy.[^1869]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Of the Fathers, Tone 4:*  
*Doxastikon:*  
Today let us, the assemblies of the Orthodox, piously and faithfully celebrate the prayerful memory of the God-bearing Fathers, who assembled from the whole universe in the radiant city of Nicaea: for they, with pious and venerable wisdom, cast down the godless doctrine of fierce Arius, and synodically expelled him from the Catholic Church; and they clearly taught all to confess the Son of God to be consubstantial and co-eternal, existing before the ages, having clearly and piously set this forth in the Symbol of Faith. Therefore we also, following their divine ordinances, believing with certainty, serve the Son with the Father and the All-holy Spirit, in one Godhead, the Consubstantial Trinity.[^1870]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Of the feast, the same Tone:*  
O Lord, having fulfilled the mystery hidden from the ages and from generations as Good, Thou didst come with Thy Disciples unto the Mount of Olives, having her who gave birth unto Thee, the Creator and Fashioner of all; for unto her who at Thy Passion suffered maternal grief more than all, it was fitting also with the glory of Thy flesh to enjoy exceedingly great joy: of which we also partaking, at Thine ascent into the heavens, O Master, glorify Thy great mercy that hath been upon us.[^1871]

*Then, "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." Trisagion prayers, and after "Our Father":*  
*Troparion of the Fathers, Tone 8: "Thou art supremely glorified, O Christ our God..." twice; and of the feast, once.*  
*(Written at Matins.)*[^1872]  
*And the blessing of loaves, and the reading of the Acts of the Holy Apostles.*  
*If there is no vigil: Resurrection Troparion: "Angelic Powers..." Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Of the Fathers. Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Of the Ascension.*  
*At Compline: Kontakion of the Fathers. Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Of the Ascension.*  
*At Midnight Office: we sing the Trinity Canon of the Octoechos of the Tone, and the rest as usual. And the dismissal.*[^1873]

## ON THE SEVENTH SUNDAY AFTER PASCHA, OF THE HOLY FATHERS, AT MATINS[^1874]

*At "God is the Lord," Resurrection Troparion:*  
*Tone 6:*  
The Angelic Powers were at Thy tomb, and the guards became as dead men; and Mary stood in the tomb, seeking Thine all-pure Body; Thou didst take hades captive, not being tempted by it; Thou didst meet the Virgin, granting life: O Thou Who didst rise from the dead, Lord, glory to Thee![^1875]  
*Twice.*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Of the Fathers, Tone 8:*  
Thou art supremely glorified, O Christ our God, Who didst establish our Fathers as luminaries upon the earth, and through them didst guide us all unto the true faith: O Most Compassionate One, glory to Thee![^1876]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Troparion of the Ascension, Tone 4:*  
Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God, having gladdened the Disciples with the promise of the Holy Spirit, they having been assured by the blessing: that Thou art the Son of God, the Redeemer of the world.[^1877]

*After the first stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 6:*  
When the tomb was opened and hades was lamenting, Mary cried aloud unto the hidden Apostles: Come forth, ye laborers of the vineyard, preach the word of the Resurrection: the Lord is risen, granting great mercy unto the world![^1878]

*Verse: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor for ever.* (Ps. 9:33 LXX [Ps. 10:12])  
Mary Magdalene stood before Thy tomb, O Lord, and wept crying aloud, supposing Thee to be the gardener, saying: Where hast Thou laid the eternal Life? Where hast Thou hidden Him Who sits upon the Cherubic throne? For the guards on account of Him became as dead men with fear; either give me my Lord, or cry out with me: O Thou Who wast among the dead, and didst raise the dead, glory to Thee![^1879]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Having called Thy Mother blessed, Thou camest unto the Passion by voluntary counsel, shining forth upon the Cross, desiring to seek out Adam, saying unto the Angels: Rejoice with Me, for the lost drachma is found! (Lk. 15:9) O our God, Who hast wisely ordered all things, glory to Thee![^1880]

*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn, the same Tone [Tone 6]:*  
Life lay in the tomb, and a seal was upon the stone; as a sleeping king the soldiers guarded Christ, and having invisibly smitten His enemies, the Lord is risen.[^1881]

*Verse: I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart.* (Ps. 9:2 LXX [Ps. 9:1])  
By Thy voluntary death we have found immortal life, O Almighty and only Savior of all; for unto Thy precious Resurrection Thou hast called all, having destroyed the victory of hades and the sting of death.[^1882]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
O Virgin Theotokos, pray to Thy Son, Christ our God, Who of His own will was nailed upon the Cross, and rose from the dead, that our souls may be saved.[^1883]

*After the Evlogitaria: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord..." The assembly of the Angels...*  
*And immediately the Hypakoe, Tone 6:*  
By Thy voluntary and life-creating Death, O Christ, having shattered the gates of hades as God, Thou hast opened unto us ancient paradise; and having risen from the dead, Thou hast delivered our life from corruption.[^1884]

*The Hymns of Ascent, Tone 6.*  
*Antiphon 1:*  
Unto heaven I lift up mine eyes, unto Thee, O Word: take pity on me, that I may live unto Thee.[^1885]

Have mercy on us who are despised, making us Thy well-fitted vessels, O Word.[^1886]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
Unto the Holy Spirit belongs every all-saving cause: if upon anyone He breathes worthily, speedily He lifts him from earthly things, wings him, causes him to grow, and establishes him on high.[^1887]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*Antiphon 2:*  
If the Lord had not been in us, none of us could have withstood the enemy’s struggles: for they who conquer are from hence exalted.[^1888]

May not their teeth lay hold upon my soul, as a young bird, O Word: woe is me! How shall I escape from the enemy, being a lover of sin?[^1889]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
Through the Holy Spirit is deification unto all, good pleasure, understanding, peace, and blessing: for He is equal in working with the Father and the Word.[^1890]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*Antiphon 3:*  
They who trust in the Lord are terrible to enemies, and marvelous unto all: for they gaze on high.[^1891]

Unto iniquities the lot of the righteous, having Thee as Helper, O Savior, stretches not forth its hands.[^1892]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
The dominion of the Holy Spirit is over all: Whom the armies above worship together with every breath below.[^1893]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 6:*  
O Lord, stir up Thy might, and come to save us. (Ps. 79:3 LXX [Ps. 80:2])

*Verse: Give ear, O Shepherd of Israel, Thou Who leadest Joseph like a sheep.* (Ps. 79:2 LXX [Ps. 80:1])

*Let every breath.*

*Resurrection Gospel 10, pericope 96 (Jn. 21:1–14): "Jesus showed Himself to His disciples after He was risen from the dead..."*[^1894]

*Then: Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ... Psalm 50. And the rest as usual.*

*The Canons: The Resurrection Canon, with the heirmos, to 4; of the Ascension, to 4; and of the Holy Fathers, to 6.*

*Resurrection Canon, Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
When Israel traversed the deep on foot as upon dry land, seeing Pharaoh the pursuer drowned, he cried aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory![^1895]

*Troparion:*  
With outstretched hands upon the Cross, O good Jesus, Thou didst fulfill all the Father's good pleasure; therefore we all sing unto Thee a song of victory.[^1896]

*Troparion:*  
Death, having been commanded, approached Thee, the Master of life, in fear as a handmaid, through her granting unto us endless life and resurrection.[^1897]

*Theotokion:*  
Having received Thy Creator, as He Himself willed, incarnate beyond understanding from thy seedless womb, O pure Lady, thou hast truly been shown forth as Sovereign Lady of creatures.[^1898]

*Canon of the Ascension, Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos: Unto God the Savior:*  
Let all of us people sing a song of victory unto Christ, Who ascended in glory upon the shoulders of the Cherubim, and seated us at the right hand of the Father: for He hath been glorified![^1899]

*Troparion:*  
The ranks of Angels, seeing Christ the Mediator between God and men in the highest with flesh, marveled, and with one accord sang a song of victory: for He hath been glorified![^1900]

*Troparion:*  
Unto God Who appeared on Mount Sinai and gave the Law to Moses the beholder of God, Who ascended in the flesh from the Mount of Olives, let us all sing: for He hath been glorified![^1901]

*Theotokion:*  
O all-pure Mother of God, unceasingly pray unto God, Who was incarnate from thee and departed not from the bosom of the Begetter, to save those whom He created from every distress.[^1902]

*Canon of the Holy Fathers, the acrostic whereof is:*  
*Τὸν πρῶτον ὑμνῶ σύλλογον τῶν ποιμένων: that is:*  
*I sing the first assembly of the shepherds.*

*Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos: When Israel traversed the deep on foot:*

*Troparion:*  
Praising the all-holy Council of the holy Fathers, crying aloud unto its all-holy prophecy within me, I pray Thee, O Christ, that I may be preserved. *Twice.*[^1903]

*Troparion:*  
The God-bearing Fathers, having assembled today like lightning, clearly confessed Thee, O Christ, as the Only-begotten Son, co-unoriginate with the Father, and Consubstantial. *Twice.*[^1904]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
The glorious adorners of the bridal chamber of Thy Church, O Master, having clearly set forth the golden definition of the faith, adorned her therewith as with churchly adornment.[^1905]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
All-adorned with divine glory, the Honorable Queen stands before her Son and God, asking spiritual salvation for us.[^1906]

*Katavasia: Covered by the divine darkness:*[^1907]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.[^1908]

*Troparion:*  
Creation, beholding God crucified in the flesh, broke apart in fear; but was held firmly together by the holding hand of Him Who was crucified for our sake.[^1909]

*Troparion:*  
Death, having been destroyed by death, lies wretched and breathless; for not enduring the divine assault of Life, that mighty one is made dead, and resurrection is granted unto all.[^1910]

*Theotokion:*  
The miracle of thy divine Childbearing, O pure Lady, surpasses every order of nature; for thou didst conceive God in thy womb beyond nature, and having given birth, thou remainest ever-Virgin.[^1911]

*Another Heirmos: By the power of Thy Cross, O Christ:*

*Troparion:*  
Thou hast ascended unto the Father, O Life-Giver Christ, and hast exalted our race, O Lover of mankind, by Thine ineffable dispensation.[^1912]

*Troparion:*  
The angelic ranks, O Savior, seeing human nature ascending together with Thee, unceasingly marveled and praised Thee.[^1913]

*Troparion:*  
The choirs of Angels were amazed, O Christ, seeing Thee ascended with the body, and they sang of Thy holy Ascension.[^1914]

*Theotokion:*  
Unceasingly pray, O pure Lady, unto Him Who came forth from thy womb, that we who sing of thee as Mother of God may be delivered from the deception of the devil.[^1915]

*Another Heirmos: There is none holy as Thou, O Lord:*

*Troparion:*  
Mad Arius, impiously attributing flux, passion, and division unto the divine Generation, is cut off by the cutting sword of the Fathers. *Twice.*[^1916]

*Troparion:*  
As of old divine Abraham made war, all the all-honorable God-proclaiming ones powerfully destroyed Thine enraged enemies by Thy power, O Good One. *Twice.*[^1917]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
The first assembly of Thy hierarchs, having gathered together, piously proclaimed Thee, O Savior, as Consubstantial with the unoriginate Father, and Creator of all, begotten.[^1918]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
The word of earthborn mortals nor tongue is able to praise thee worthily, O Virgin; for without seed, Christ the Life-Giver was pleased to be incarnate from thee, O all-pure Lady.[^1919]

*Katavasia: Burst asunder the bonds of the childless womb:*[^1920]

*Kontakion of the Ascension, Tone 6: Having fulfilled the dispensation for our sake: And Ikos. (see fol. 472)*[^1921]

*Then Sessional Hymn of the Fathers, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*  
As most radiant luminaries of the truth of Christ did ye appear to the world upon the earth, O truly most blessed God-proclaiming Fathers, having quenched the heresies of blasphemous babblers of tongues, and having extinguished the flame-like tumults of the ill-famed; therefore as hierarchs of Christ, pray that we may be saved.[^1922]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Another Sessional Hymn, the same Tone, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*  
The radiant city of Nicaea today summoned to itself from all the earth three hundred and eighteen hierarchs against Arius who spoke blasphemy and diminished the One of the Trinity, the Son and Word Who is of God; and having cast down that Arius, they confirmed the faith.[^1923]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Of the Ascension, the same Podoben:*  
Having ascended into the heavens in glory, and having sat down at the right hand of the Father, Thou wast in no wise separated, O Christ Lover of mankind; and having promised to send the Holy Spirit unto Thy most wise Disciples, and having illumined our minds, grant enlightenment, that we may unceasingly praise Thee, O Master.[^1924]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
Christ is my power, God and Lord: the honorable Church piously sings, crying aloud: from a pure mind keeping festival in the Lord.[^1925]

*Troparion:*  
The Tree of true life blossomed forth, O Christ: for the Cross was set up, and being watered with Blood and water from Thine incorruptible Side, blossomed life unto us.[^1926]

*Troparion:*  
No longer does the serpent falsely propose deification unto me: for Christ, the Deifier of human nature, hath now unobstructed made smooth for me the path of life.[^1927]

*Theotokion:*  
Truly unutterable and incomprehensible are the mysteries of thy divinely-befitting Childbearing, O Ever-Virgin Mother of God, for those on earth and in heaven.[^1928]

*Another Heirmos: I heard the report of the power of the Cross:*

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst ascend in glory, O King of Angels, having sent the Comforter unto us from the Father; therefore we cry: Glory to Thy Ascension, O Christ![^1929]

*Troparion:*  
When the Savior went up unto the Father with flesh, the angelic hosts marveled at Him and cried aloud: Glory to Thy Ascension, O Christ![^1930]

*Troparion:*  
The angelic Powers cried unto the higher ranks: Lift up the gates for Christ our King, Whom we sing together with the Father and the Spirit![^1931]

*Theotokion:*  
The Virgin gave birth and knew not motherly travail; for she is indeed Mother, yet remained Virgin; singing unto whom, we cry: Rejoice, O Theotokos![^1932]

*Another Heirmos: Christ is my power:*

*Troparion:*  
Arius, who adulterated the Orthodox faith with senseless mind, was driven out from the Church by the judgments of the Fathers as a rotten and ill-named member. *Twice.*[^1933]

*Troparion:*  
For Thy sake, O Master, the assembly of the Fathers was moved, completely conquered Thine enemies, and glorified Thee as consubstantial with the Father and the Spirit. *Twice.*[^1934]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
Thou wast Mediator, O God and Man, between God and men, O Christ; therefore the God-wise proclaimed Thee in two natures, knowing Thee as one Son.[^1935]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
The tasting of the garden showed me dead, but the Tree of Life which appeared from thee, O all-pure Lady, raised me up, and showed me an heir of the delight of paradise.[^1936]

*Katavasia: O King of kings:*[^1937]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray, the souls of those who dawn early with longing unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, calling them from the darkness of sin.[^1938]

*Troparion:*  
The Cherubim now yields to me, and the flaming sword, O Master, turns its back to me, having seen Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who didst make a way into paradise for the thief.[^1939]

*Troparion:*  
No longer do I fear the return into the earth, O Master Christ: for from the earth Thou hast raised me up who was forgotten, by reason of Thy great tenderheartedness, unto the height of incorruption by Thy Resurrection.[^1940]

*Theotokion:*  
Save those who from the soul confess thee as Theotokos, O good Sovereign Lady of the world: for we have thee as invincible advocate, the true Mother of God.[^1941]

*Another Heirmos: Dawning early we cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord:*

*Troparion:*  
Having filled all things with joy, O Merciful One, Thou camest with the flesh unto the heavenly Hosts.[^1942]

*Troparion:*  
The angelic Hosts, seeing Thee taken up, cried aloud: Lift up the gates for our King![^1943]

*Troparion:*  
The Apostles, seeing the Savior lifted up, cried with trembling unto our King: Glory to Thee![^1944]

*Theotokion:*  
We sing of thee as Virgin after childbearing, O Theotokos; for thou didst bear God the Word in the flesh for the world.[^1945]

*Another Heirmos: With Thy divine light:*

*Troparion:*  
Beautiful indeed are the feet of those who now proclaim Thee as Peace Which surpasses all understanding of all Angels and men, O Christ, and Who unitest the world by a multitude of peace. *Twice.*[^1946]

*Troparion:*  
The divine Teachers, having assembled, proclaimed Thee, O Christ, as the Wisdom, Power, and hypostatic Word of the Father, having been honorably sealed by the law of the all-holy Priesthood. *Twice.*[^1947]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
Having watered the Orthodox Church with pure streams of doctrines, they now take delight beside the waters of rest, rejoicing everlastingly.[^1948]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Knowing thee, O all-pure Lady, as a radiant lampstand illumining Christ, the Sun of righteousness, unto all, O only pure Mother of God, we now call upon thy protection.[^1949]

*Katavasia: The purifying cleansing:*[^1950]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One![^1951]

*Troparion:*  
Crucified, O Master, by the nails Thou didst blot out the curse that was against us; and pierced in the Side by the spear, having torn up the handwriting of Adam, Thou didst set the world free.[^1952]

*Troparion:*  
Adam was brought down, tripped by deceit, unto the pit of hades; but being God by nature and merciful, Thou didst descend to seek him, and taking him upon Thy shoulders, didst raise him up with Thyself.[^1953]

*Theotokion:*  
O all-pure Sovereign Lady, who didst give birth unto the Lord the Helmsman of men, drive away the unstable and cruel tumult of my passions, and grant stillness unto my heart.[^1954]

*Another Heirmos: The deep encompassed me:*

*Troparion:*  
The Apostles leaped for joy, beholding the Creator taken up into the height today; and with hope of the Spirit, and with fear they cried: Glory to Thine Ascent![^1955]

*Troparion:*  
The Angels stood by, crying unto Thy disciples, O Christ: In like manner as ye have seen Christ ascending with the flesh, so shall the righteous Judge of all come again.[^1956]

*Troparion:*  
When certain Powers saw Thee, our Savior, taken up into the height with the body, they cried aloud, saying: Great is Thy love for mankind, O Master![^1957]

*Theotokion:*  
As the unburnt Bush, and the Mountain, and the ensouled Ladder, and the heavenly Gate, we worthily glorify thee, O glorious Mary, the boast of the Orthodox.[^1958]

*Another Heirmos: Beholding the sea of life:*

*Troparion:*  
The sower of tares, who was named after madness, could not hide from ineffable Providence; for having rivaled Judas, like him that all-evil one burst asunder. *Twice.*[^1959]

*Troparion:*  
The honorable and divine Assembly of the Fathers proclaims Thee, O Master, as the only-begotten Radiance Who shone forth from the essence of the Father, and as the Son begotten before all ages. *Twice.*[^1960]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
The belly, according to the word, which poured forth an arch-like and undrinkable fountain of impious heresies, is torn asunder, O Providential One, by the plow of the prayer of the divine Priests.[^1961]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
The great Moses prefigured thee among the Prophets as the Ark, and Table, and Lampstand, and Jar, typically signifying the incarnation of the Most High from thee, O Mother and Virgin.[^1962]

*Katavasia: A cleansing unto us:*[^1963]

*Kontakion of the Fathers, Tone 8, Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*  
The preaching of the Apostles and the dogmas of the Fathers have sealed the one faith of the Church; which also wearing the garment of truth, woven from the theology from on high, uprightly sets forth and glorifies the great mystery of piety.[^1964]

*Ikos:*  
Let us hear the Church of God crying aloud with elevated proclamation: He that thirsts, let him come unto Me and drink! (Jn. 7:37) The chalice which I hold is the chalice of wisdom; this drink of truth have I drawn with the word, pouring forth the water not of contradiction, but of confession; drinking which, present-day Israel sees God speaking: Behold, behold, that I am He, and I change not: (Mal. 3:6) I am God the first, and I also after these, and beside Me there is no other at all. (Is. 44:6) They who partake thereof shall be satisfied, and shall praise the great mystery of piety.[^1965]

*Synaxarion for the Sunday of the 318 God-bearing Fathers who were at Nicaea.*[^1966]

*Verses:*  
*O radiant chosen assembly:*  
*Illumine my thoughts with light-bearing rays.*[^1967]

*Verse:*  
*Saying the Son is alien to the essence of the Father:*  
*Thus Arius is made an alien to the glory of God.*[^1968]

On this same day, on the Seventh Sunday after Pascha, we celebrate the first Council at Nicaea of the three hundred and eighteen God-bearing Fathers, for the following reason: For since our Lord Jesus Christ, having clothed Himself in flesh, ineffably accomplished the entire dispensation for our sake, and went up again unto the Father’s throne, the Saints, wishing to show that the Son of God was truly made man, and as perfect man ascended as God, and sat at the right hand of the Majesty on high, this Council of the holy Fathers thus proclaimed Him, and confessed Him Consubstantial and of equal honor with the Father. By this word after the glorious Ascension, they instituted the present festival, as it were anticipating the gathering of so many Fathers, proclaiming Him Who ascended in the flesh as true God, and in the flesh as perfect man. This Council took place under Constantine the Great, the emperor, in the twentieth year of his reign, persecution having ceased. He formerly held dominion in Rome, but afterwards the all-glorious Constantine built a city named after himself. In the year 5838 from the creation of the world, they began to speak against Arius. This Arius was indeed from Libya, and having come to Alexandria, was ordained a deacon by the hieromartyr Peter of Alexandria; but because he began to blaspheme against the Son of God, proclaiming Him to be a creature, brought forth from things that were not, and far removed from divine dignity, and that He was called the Wisdom and Word of God only in an abusive sense, assimilating himself to the impious Sabellius who spoke of the Godhead as of one person and one hypostasis, being sometimes the Father, sometimes the Son, and sometimes the Holy Spirit. When the great Peter learned these things concerning Arius, he excommunicated him from the priesthood, having seen Christ as an infant upon the holy altar clothed in a torn garment, and saying: Arius hath torn it. But Achillas, who after Peter received the patriarchate of the city of Alexandria, upon promises again absolved Arius; and furthermore ordained him a presbyter, and sent him forth as a teacher in Alexandria. But when Achillas had reposed, Alexander became patriarch; who, finding Arius likewise blaspheming and even worse, cast him out from the Church, deposing him by a Council, as Theodoret saith: that he also said the nature of Christ was mutable, and that the Lord took a soulless and mindless flesh; this man was the first to belch forth this. Arius indeed brought many under his impiety; he wrote and fled unto Eusebius of Nicomedia, Paulinus of Tyre, Eusebius of Caesarea, and others, and armed himself against Alexander. But Alexander, having sent the blasphemy and deposition of that man everywhere throughout the universe, stirred up many to vengeance. The dispute being thus agitated, and many wishing to accept Christianity being hindered by the contention of doctrine, the great Constantine summoned the holy Fathers from everywhere throughout the universe by public carriages and litters unto Nicaea, and came thither himself. And all the Fathers having sat down, he commanded and himself sat, not upon the imperial throne, but upon a lowly seat of dignity. And the things spoken against Arius having taken place, he and all who agreed with him in opinion were subjected to anathema. And the Word of God was proclaimed by the holy Fathers to be Consubstantial, of equal honor, and co-unoriginate with the Father. And they set forth this holy Symbol of Faith, having carried it even unto the phrase: And in the Holy Spirit, the Lord; which the second Council afterwards completed. And in addition to these, the first Council confirmed the festival of Pascha: when, and how it is fitting for us to celebrate it, and not with the Jews, as had formerly been customary. And they set forth also twenty canons for the correction of ecclesiastical affairs. And the holy Symbol of Faith, the great and equal-to-the-apostles Constantine confirmed last of all with crimson letters. There were indeed among these holy Fathers two hundred and thirty-two hierarchs; and of priests and deacons, and with these also monks, seventy-six: in all, three hundred and eighteen. And these were the notable ones: Sylvester, hierarch of Rome; Metrophanes of Constantinople, being sick; these indeed were there through their locum tenentes; Alexander of Alexandria, with Athanasius the Great, who was then archdeacon; and Eustathius of Antioch, and Macarius of Jerusalem; Paphnutius the Confessor, Nicholas the Myrrh-streamer, and Spyridon of Trimythous, who overthrew the philosopher's cup of clay, having shown him the three suns therein. In the midst of the Council, two sacred hierarchs having reposed in God, the great Constantine placed the decrees of the holy Council into their coffins, and having closed them firmly, found them sealed and subscribed by them also with ineffable words of God. And the Council having ended, because Constantinople which was being built was also now completed, Constantine the Great summoned all those holy men; who, having walked around the whole city and prayed sufficiently, decreed it to be the imperial city among cities, and dedicated it to the Mother of the Word, the emperor having commanded. And thus the saints departed, each to his home. But while the great Constantine was not yet departed unto God, holding the scepters together with his son Constantius, Arius came unto the emperor, saying: I leave all things, and wish to be united unto the Church of God. Having written his blasphemies, he hung them around his neck, and as though submitting to the Council, striking his chest with his hand, he said: To this I submit. And so the emperor commanded the patriarch of Constantinople to receive Arius into communion. There was then after Metrophanes, Alexander, who knowing the evil disposition of the man, did not believe him, and prayed to God to reveal unto him whether Arius should be received in communion according to His will. But when the time arrived at which he was to concelebrate with him, he became more fervent in prayer; but Arius, going toward the Church, somewhere near the market pillar, his bowels burst, and he entered into a public latrine, and there he ruptured, and vomited forth his entire inner substance downward, having suffered the bursting asunder of Judas, and by an end equal to his betrayal of the Word, and tearing away the divine Son from the essence of the Father, he was torn asunder himself, and was found dead: and thus the Church of God was delivered from that man’s destruction.[^1969]

*Through the prayers of the three hundred and eighteen God-bearing Fathers, O Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen.*[^1970]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The Angel made the furnace dew-yielding for the venerable youths, but the burning command of God consumed the Chaldeans, persuading the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^1971]

*Troparion:*  
Lamenting at Thy Passion, the sun clothed itself in darkness, and in daytime the light over all the earth grew dark, O Master, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^1972]

*Troparion:*  
The lowest regions were clothed in light by Thy descent, O Christ, and the Forefather, having appeared full of joy, leaped for joy and danced, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^1973]

*Trinity:*  
Singing of the Three-hypostatic Nature, Which reigns and rules over all, the abyss of goodness and boundless Light, let us faithful cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^1974]

*Another Heirmos: In the fiery furnace:*

*Troparion:*  
O Thou Who didst ascend upon the clouds of light and didst save the world, blessed is the God of our fathers![^1975]

*Troparion:*  
Having taken upon Thy shoulders our straying nature, O Christ, Thou didst ascend and bring it unto God and the Father.[^1976]

*Troparion:*  
O Thou Who didst ascend in the flesh unto the incorporeal Father, blessed is the God of our fathers![^1977]

*Theotokion:*  
Having taken our nature which was slain by sin, Thou didst bring it, O Savior, unto Thine own Father.[^1978]

*Another Heirmos: Dew-yielding:*

*Troparion:*  
Ye conquer him who is named after madness, Arius who raged grievously, and spoke unrighteous things against the height: for it was appointed to cry unto the Son of God: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^1979]

*Troparion:*  
Imitating the Son of Thunder, O marvelous ones, with your fire-bright lips ye taught all to cry unto the Word, co-unoriginate and co-enthroned with the Father: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers! *Twice.*[^1980]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
As winged ones, having helped the Word, ye arrived, O divinely blessed ones: for from the ends of the universe the Holy Spirit gathered you to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^1981]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
The furnace did not burn the three youths, prefiguring thy Childbearing: for the divine Fire, without burning thee, dwelt in thee, and enlightened all to cry: Blessed art thou, who didst bear God in the flesh![^1982]

*Katavasia: The concordant music of instruments sounded forth:*[^1983]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and with water didst burn the sacrifice of the righteous one; for Thou dost accomplish all things, O Christ, by the mere act of willing: Thee do we exalt supremely unto all ages![^1984]

*Troparion:*  
The people who anciently were the slayers of the prophets, envy hath now made the slayers of God, having lifted Thee upon the Cross, O Word of God: Whom we exalt supremely unto all ages.[^1985]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst not leave the heavenly spheres, and having descended unto hades, Thou didst raise up together all man who lay in the mire, O Christ; therefore we sing unto Thee unto all ages.[^1986]

*Theotokion:*  
From the light-bestowing Word Thou didst conceive, and ineffably gavest birth unto the Transcendent One: for the divine Spirit dwelt in thee, O Virgin; therefore we sing unto thee unto all ages.[^1987]

*Another Heirmos: From the Father before the ages:*

*Troparion:*  
O priests, sing, and ye people, exalt supremely unto all ages Christ the Life-Giver, Who ascended into the heavens in glory in two essences, and sits with the Father.[^1988]

*Troparion:*  
Thee, O Savior, Who didst deliver creation from the bondage of idols, and didst present it free unto Thy Father, we sing, and Thee do we exalt supremely unto all ages.[^1989]

*Troparion:*  
O priests, sing, and ye people, exalt supremely unto all ages Him Who by His descent cast down the adversary, and by His ascent exalted man.[^1990]

*Theotokion:*  
Thou hast been shown higher than the Cherubim, O pure Theotokos, having carried in thy womb Him Who is borne by them: Whom together with the incorporeal hosts, we mortals glorify unto all ages.[^1991]

*Another Heirmos: From the flame for the venerable ones:*

*Troparion:*  
Illumined by the rays of Thy Godhead, Thy good Shepherds confessed Thee to be the Maker of all and Lord by essence: Whom we exalt supremely unto all ages. *Twice.*[^1992]

*Troparion:*  
The ever-memorable assembly of Shepherds, having gathered together, divinely theologizing the uncreated Trinity, now teaches all to cry: Thee do we exalt supremely unto all ages! *Twice.*[^1993]

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*

*Troparion:*  
The Hierarchs, the highly renowned Shepherds, enlighten the Church of Christ, announcing this to one another, and exalting Him supremely unto all ages.[^1994]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Mystically in figures all the Prophets foresaw thee, who gavest birth to the Word: for taking flesh from thee, He came forth twofold: Whom we exalt supremely unto all ages.[^1995]

*Then:*  
*We praise, bless, and worship the Lord, singing, and exalting Him supremely unto all ages.*

*Katavasia:*[^1996]

*Katavasia:*  
*Looses the burning bonds...*[^1997]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
God it is impossible for men to see, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate appeared unto men: Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.[^1998]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst remain untouched by passions, O Word of God, having partaken of passions in the flesh; but Thou loosest man from passions, having become a passion unto passions, O our Savior: for Thou alone art impassible and almighty.[^1999]

*Troparion:*  
Accepting mortal corruption, Thou hast preserved Thy Body untouched by decay; and Thy life-creating and divine soul, O Master, was not left in hades; but having risen as from sleep, Thou hast raised us up together with Thyself.[^2000]

*Triadikon:*  
All we men glorify with pure lips God the Father, and the Son co-unoriginate; and we venerate the ineffable and most glorious power of the all-holy Spirit: for one is the all-powerful, indivisible Trinity.[^2001]

*Another Heirmos [of the Feast]:*  
*Thee, who beyond mind and word art the Mother of God:*[^2002]

*Troparion:*  
Beholding Thee, the Redeemer of the world, Christ God, divinely exalted, the Apostles with fear leaping for joy magnified Thee.[^2003]

*Troparion:*  
Beholding Thy deified flesh on high, O Christ, the Angels said one to another: Truly this is our God.[^2004]

*Troparion:*  
Beholding Thee, O Christ God, borne upon the clouds, the ranks of the bodiless hosts cried aloud: Lift up your gates, O King of glory![^2005]

*Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, O Theotokos, Mother of Christ God: beholding Him Whom thou didst bear being today taken up from the earth with the Apostles, thou didst magnify Him.[^2006]

*Another Heirmos [of the Holy Fathers]:*  
*God it is impossible for men to see:*[^2007]

*Troparion:*  
Having found you as champions, the Word, co-unoriginate and enthroned with the Father, being before the ages, gathered you together, having armed you with the sovereign power of the Spirit: Whom now, O most sacred ones, together with the heavenly hosts ye ever glorify. *Twice.*[^2008]

*Troparion:*  
As physicians of souls and bodies, ye put an end to the pasture of the cruel heresy of Arius, having set forth for all the sacred Symbol of faith: which we now holding fast, O sacred ministers, ever glorify your memory. *Twice.*[^2009]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
As Thou art the light, O most pure Christ, cleanse my soul from the mist of passions, through the prayers of Thy ministers, O Master, who now have proclaimed Thee, the unoriginate, uncreated, the Maker of all and God, co-unoriginate with the Father.[^2010]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Resurrection hath now been granted unto the dead by thine ineffable and unutterable childbearing, O Virgin Lady: for Life, clothed in flesh from thee, hath shone forth unto all, and hath manifestly destroyed the peril of death.[^2011]

*Katavasia:*  
*Rejoice, O Queen...*[^2012]

*Holy is the Lord our God. Thrice.*[^2013]

*Resurrectional Exapostilarion [Tenth]:*  
The Sea of Tiberias with the children of Zebedee, and Nathanael with Peter, and two others of old, and Thomas, had them fishing; who at Christ's bidding having cast on the right side, drew forth a multitude of fish; Whom Peter having recognized, swam unto Him: unto whom He appeared for the third time, and showed bread, and fish upon the coals.[^2014]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Of the Fathers.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*  
Today celebrating the memory of the divine Fathers, by their supplications we entreat Thee, O All-merciful One: Deliver Thy people, O Lord, from every harm of heresies, and vouchsafe all to glorify the Father, the Word, and the all-holy Spirit.[^2015]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Of the Ascension:*  
While the Disciples were beholding Thee, Thou didst ascend, O Christ, to sit beside the Father; the Angels running before cry aloud: Lift up your gates, lift them up! For the King hath ascended unto the primal light of glory.[^2016]

*At the Praises: "Let every breath..." We set 8 stichoi. And we chant 4 Resurrectional stichera, and 4 of the Holy Fathers.*[^2017]  
*Resurrectional Stichera, Tone 6:*  
Thy Cross, O Lord, is life and resurrection unto Thy people; and hoping thereon, we sing to Thee, our risen God: Have mercy on us.[^2018]

Thy burial, O Master, opened paradise unto the race of mankind; and being delivered from corruption, we sing to Thee, our risen God: Have mercy on us.[^2019]

With the Father and the Spirit let us hymn Christ Who is risen from the dead, and let us cry aloud unto Him: Thou art our Life and Resurrection: Have mercy on us.[^2020]

On the third day Thou didst rise, O Christ, from the tomb, as it is written, having raised up together with Thyself our forefather: therefore the human race also glorifies Thee, and hymns Thy Resurrection.[^2021]

*Other Stichera of the Holy Fathers.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*  
Having gathered all spiritual art, and having examined together by the divine Spirit, the venerable Fathers inscribed in God-written manner the heavenly and venerable Symbol of faith; wherein most manifestly they teach that the Word is co-unoriginate with the Begetter, and truly consubstantial, following clearly the apostolic teachings, being truly glorious and most rich, and wise in God.[^2022]

*Sticheron:*  
Having received all the spiritual illumination of the Holy Spirit, the most supernatural blessing, in brief words and with much understanding, they divinely uttered as preachers of Christ, blessed champions of the evangelical teachings and of pious traditions; having received from on high their revelation manifestly, and being enlightened, they set forth the God-taught faith.[^2023]

*Verse: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our Fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy name unto the ages.* (Dan. 3:26)[^2024]  
*Sticheron:*  
Having gathered all pastoral skill, and having now stirred up most righteous wrath, with vengeance they drove away the grievous and destructive wolves with the sling of the Spirit, casting them out from the fullness of the Church, fallen as unto death and incurably diseased, the divine Shepherds, as truest servants of Christ and most sacred initiates of divine proclamation.[^2025]

*Verse: Gather together unto Him His venerable ones, who have established His covenant upon sacrifices.* (Ps. 49:5 LXX)  
*And again the 1st sticheron:*  
*Having gathered all spiritual art...*[^2026]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 8:*  
The choir of the Holy Fathers, gathered together from the ends of the universe, taught one essence and nature of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, and clearly handed down the mystery of theology to the Church: whom praising in faith, we call blessed, saying: O divine company, God-proclaiming weapons of the camp of the Lord, most radiant stars of the spiritual firmament, invincible towers of the mystical Sion, fragrant flowers of paradise, all-golden mouths of the Word, boast of Nicaea, adornment of the universe, fervently pray for our souls.[^2027]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos...*[^2028]

*The Great Doxology.*  
*The Resurrection Troparion alone. Litanies.*  
*And the Dismissal.*[^2029]

*And the departure into the narthex:*  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Gospel Sticheron [Tenth], Tone 6:*  
After Thy descent into hades and Thy Resurrection from the dead, sorrowing as was meet over Thy departure, O Christ, the Disciples returned to their work, and again there were boats and nets and no catch anywhere; but Thou, O Savior, having appeared as Master of all, commandest them to cast the nets on the right side, and the word straightway became deed: and there was a great multitude of fish, and a strange supper ready upon the earth; of which Thy Disciples then partaking, vouchsafe us also now to be spiritually filled with delight, O Lord, Lover of mankind.[^2030]

*And the Catechesis of the Studite, and the First Hour:*  
*At the First Hour, Resurrection Troparion: Glory, of the Ascension: Both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Ascension.*[^2031]  
*At the Third Hour, Resurrection Troparion: Glory, of the Fathers: Both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Fathers.*  
*Likewise at the other Hours, we say the Troparia and Kontakia of the Ascension and of the Fathers, alternating.*[^2032]

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes are chanted.*  
*Tone 6.*  
Remember me, O God my Savior, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom: and save me as the only Lover of mankind.[^2033]

Through a tree Adam was deceived, through the tree of the Cross Thou didst again save him, and the robber who cried aloud: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom.[^2034]

O Life-giver, Who didst smash the gates and bars of hades, Thou didst raise up all, O Savior, who cry aloud: Glory to Thy Rising.[^2035]

Remember me, O Thou Who by Thy Burial didst lead death captive, and by Thy Resurrection didst fill all things with joy: for Thou art tender-hearted.[^2036]

*And of the Feast, Ode 3 on 4; and of the Fathers, Ode 6 on 4.*  
*After the Entrance we say the Resurrection Troparion; then of the Ascension, and of the Fathers.*  
*Glory, Kontakion of the Fathers: Both now, of the Ascension.*[^2037]

*Instead of the Prokeimenon, the Hymn of the Fathers, Tone 4:*  
Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our Fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy name unto the ages.[^2038]  
*Verse: For Thou art righteous in all that Thou hast done for us.* (Dan. 3:27)  

*Epistle in the Acts of the Apostles, pericope 44: "Paul had decided to sail past Ephesus..."* (Acts 20:16-18, 28-36)[^2039]  
*Alleluia, Tone 1:*  
The God of gods, the Lord, hath spoken, and hath called the earth from the rising of the sun unto the going down thereof. (Ps. 49:1 LXX)[^2040]  
*Verse: Gather together unto Him His venerable ones, who have established His covenant upon sacrifices.* (Ps. 49:5 LXX)  

*Gospel according to John, pericope 56: "Jesus lifted up His eyes to heaven..."* (John 17:1-13)[^2041]  
*Communion Hymn: Praise the Lord from the heavens.* (Ps. 148:1 LXX) *Another: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous.* (Ps. 32:1 LXX)[^2042]  
*Alleluia. Thrice.*

## ON THE SUNDAY OF THE HOLY FATHERS, IN THE EVENING[^2043]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 stichoi. And we chant 3 stichera of the Holy Fathers:*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Despairing on account of her life" (Ѿча́ѧннаѧ):*  
From the womb before the morning star Thou wast born of the Father without a mother before the ages, although Arius glorifies Thee as a creature and not God, presumptuously confounding Thee, the Maker, with creatures foolishly, treasuring up for himself the fuel of eternal fire; but the council that was in Nicaea proclaimed Thee, O Lord, Son of God, enthroned with the Father and the Spirit.[^2044]

*Sticheron:*  
Who rent Thy garment, O Savior? Arius, Thou didst say: who cut the equally honored head of the Trinity into divisions; he denied that Thou art one of the Trinity; he also teaches Nestorius not to say "Theotokos"; but the council that was in Nicaea proclaimed Thee, O Lord, Son of God, enthroned with the Father and the Spirit.[^2045]

*Sticheron:*  
Arius, hating to see the light, falls into the pit of sin, and by a divine hook his innards are torn asunder, so that he forcibly gave up his whole being and soul, having become another Judas in character and form; but the council that was in Nicaea proclaimed Thee, O Lord, Son of God, enthroned with the Father and the Spirit.[^2046]

*And of the Menaion of the Saint, 3.*[^2047]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 6:*  
Let us praise today the mystical trumpets of the Spirit, the God-bearing Fathers, who sang in the midst of the Church a harmonious song of theology: one unchangeable Trinity, both essence and Godhead; the overthrowers of Arius and champions of the Orthodox, who ever pray unto the Lord that our souls may be saved.[^2048]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Of the Feast, the same Tone [Tone 6]:*  
Beholding Thy Ascension, O Christ, upon the holy mountains, O Brightness of the Father's glory, we hymn the light-bearing aspect of Thy countenance, we worship Thy Passions, we venerate Thy Resurrection, glorifying Thy glorious Ascension: Have mercy on us.[^2049]

*At the Aposticha, stichera.*  
*Tone 6:*  
*Having gathered all spiritual art...*  
*With their verses. Written on this day at the Praises.*[^2050]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, Tone 8:*  
Ye were reliable keepers of the apostolic traditions, O holy Fathers: for having taught orthodoxly the consubstantiality of the Holy Trinity, ye conciliary cast down the blasphemy of Arius; with whom having also refuted Macedonius the fighter against the Spirit, ye condemned Nestorius, Eutyches, and Dioscorus, Sabellius, and Severus the headless: entreat that we be delivered from their delusion, and we pray that our life be preserved undefiled in the faith.[^2051]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 6:*  
God is gone up in jubilation, the Lord with the sound of the trumpet, to raise up the fallen image of Adam, and to send the Spirit, the Comforter, to sanctify our souls.[^2052]

*Dismissal Troparion, Tone 8:*  
*Most glorified art Thou, O Christ our God... Glory, of the Saint, if there be one; if not, Glory, both now, of the Feast, Tone 4: Thou hast ascended in glory...*  
*And the office of the Holy Fathers is concluded.*[^2053]

## ON MONDAY AFTER THE SUNDAY OF THE HOLY FATHERS, AT MATINS[^2054]

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Ascension, thrice.*

*After the 1st stichology, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*  
While the Angels were marveling at Thy strange ascent, and the Disciples were terrified at Thy fearful taking up, Thou didst ascend in glory as God, and the gates were lifted up before Thee, O Savior; therefore the heavenly Powers marveled, crying aloud: Glory to Thy condescension, O Savior! Glory to Thy Kingdom! Glory to Thy Ascension, O only Lover of mankind![^2055]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*After the 2nd stichology, Sessional Hymn, the same Podoben.*  
When hades was led captive by Thee, O Life-giver, and the world was enlightened at Thy Resurrection, Thou didst ascend in glory, O Savior, holding all things in Thy hand; therefore with the Angels we glorify Thee, O Lord Almighty: Glory to Thy Ascension, O Christ! Glory to Thy Kingdom! Glory to Thy tender-heartedness, O only Lover of mankind![^2056]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same. Psalm 50.*  
*Canon of the Feast of the Ascension, Tone 4, with the heirmos on 8; and of the Menaion on 4.*  
*Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Feast.*[^2057]

*At the Aposticha, stichera,*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
O faithful, let us ascend together with the Disciples of the Word unto the mountains of virtues, that we may be vouchsafed to see the glory of Christ.[^2058]

*Verse: All ye nations, clap your hands, shout unto God with a voice of rejoicing.* (Ps. 46:2 LXX)  
The minds that clearly put to shame the Manichaean conceit, concerning which they were in perplexity, clearly proclaiming the Ascension of Christ with the flesh.[^2059]

*Verse: God is gone up in jubilation, the Lord with the sound of the trumpet.* (Ps. 46:6 LXX)  
Cry aloud, O David, rejoice with thy harp: for Christ Who is ascended confirms in every way the words of thy prophecy.[^2060]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 6:*  
Thou didst ascend in glory upon the Mount of Olives, O Christ God, before Thy Disciples, and sattest at the right hand of the Father, filling all things with Godhead; and Thou didst send them the Holy Spirit, Who enlightens, and confirms, and sanctifies our souls.[^2061]

*On Monday after the Sunday of the Holy Fathers, the Three-Ode Canon [Tripesnets],*  
*Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Thy victorious right hand hath been glorified in power in God-befitting manner; for that hand, O Immortal One, being all-powerful, shattered the adversaries, making anew for the Israelites a path through the deep.[^2062]

*Troparion:*  
Not understanding then Thy priceless descent to earth, the Angels now perceive Thee ascending unto the heavens, saying: Who is this King of powers?[^2063]

*Troparion:*  
Having endured voluntary Crucifixion, O Christ, Thou didst rise on the third day, and then didst ascend unto the heavens, while the Disciples beheld Thee and marveled beyond mind at Thy power.[^2064]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
O Incomprehensible One, Who by Thine all-powerful will makest the clouds Thy chariot, Thou hast now ascended from the Mount of Olives on high unto the heights, borne upon a cloud.[^2065]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Truly, O pure one, thou hast surpassed the ranks of heavenly Angels, having given flesh unto the Word of the Father: Whom now fervently entreat, that we may be delivered from irrational deeds, O all-immaculate one.[^2066]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Sing and supremely exalt unto all ages Christ God, Who saved the children singing hymns in the furnace, and turned the flaming thunder into dew.[^2067]

*Troparion:*  
Sing, bless, and supremely exalt unto all ages Him Who ascended in glory unto the Father of unoriginate light, and sent down the Holy Spirit upon the Apostles.[^2068]

*Troparion:*  
Sing and make melody unto the Lord, cry out in fear, ye earthly kindreds: for He Who ascended in glory today hath united earthly things with the heavenly unto all ages.[^2069]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
Ye men of Galilee, why stand ye thus in fear looking into the heights? The Bodiless Ones said unto the Disciples: In like manner as ye see Him, Christ shall come again in glory.[^2070]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
O Virgin, who untilled didst put forth the heavenly Ear of Grain, Which by divine power feeds the ends of the earth: satisfy my hungry, humble soul.[^2071]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
The burning bush that was not consumed showed forth a type of thy pure childbearing; and now we pray thee to quench the raging furnace of temptations that comes upon us: that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos.[^2072]

*Troparion:*  
Having endured passion, and Crucifixion, and Death for our sake, O Sinless One, and having risen in God-befitting manner, Thou didst ascend unto Thine unoriginate Father, O Word: therefore we worship Thine incomprehensible Godhead.[^2073]

*Troparion:*  
Moses calls out in the Spirit: Let the Angelic Powers be strengthened, as the King of all bodily ascends from the earth to the heavenly courts, and seats upon the Father's throne our fallen nature.[^2074]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
Lift up the spiritual gates, David prophetically cried in the Spirit: The only-begotten Son of God hath reigned over all nations, and ascends in the flesh unto the Father, Whom He did not leave when He came down.[^2075]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
All sweetness and desire is Thy Son, God and Lord, O most holy and pure Virgin: Whom fervently entreat, with the supreme, bodiless hosts, that we may be delivered from the bitterness of soul-destroying sins.[^2076]

## ON MONDAY AFTER THE SUNDAY OF THE HOLY FATHERS, IN THE EVENING[^2077]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the Feast, 3:*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*  
Thou didst suffer as man, being impassible in Godhead; and Thou didst rise on the third day, having led death captive, and having raised up all who had decayed; and Thou didst ascend, O Christ, unto the Father, having promised to send the Comforter unto Thy sacred Apostles, O Jesus all-powerful, Savior of our souls.[^2078]

*Sticheron:*  
Why stand ye looking into heaven? The visible Angels spoke as men unto the initiates of the Word: This Same Whom ye behold being taken up in a bright cloud shall come again in like manner as ye have seen Him, to judge the world, as He said: go therefore and do all that hath been spoken.[^2079]

*Sticheron:*  
After Thine incomprehensible Resurrection from the tomb, O all-powerful Lord, having taken those whom Thou lovedst, Thou didst lead them out as far as Bethany, O Word; and being on the Mount of Olives, Thou didst bless them, and so didst ascend, while the Angels were subject unto Thee, O Jesus all-powerful, Savior of our souls.[^2080]

*And of the Menaion, 3.*[^2081]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 4:*  
When Thou camest unto the Mount of Olives, O Christ, to fulfill the good pleasure of the Father, the heavenly Angels were amazed, and those in the nether regions trembled; and the Disciples stood by trembling with joy, as Thou didst speak unto them, as a throne was prepared over against them, a cloud awaiting; and heaven having opened its gates showed forth beauty, and the earth reveals treasures, that the descent of Adam might be made known, and again his ascent: but Thy footsteps were lifted up as were Thy hands, and Thy mouth abundantly blessed as was heard; a cloud took Thee up, and heaven received Thee within: this deed Thou hast wrought, O Lord, great and most glorious, unto the salvation of our souls.[^2082]

*At the Aposticha, stichera.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
O Good One, Who didst fulfill the good pleasure of the Father, and didst unite things on high with things below, Thou didst ascend in glory unto those who were first.[^2083]

*Verse: All ye nations, clap your hands, shout unto God with a voice of rejoicing.* (Ps. 46:2 LXX)  
Thou didst ascend unto Thy Begetter, O Compassionate One, from Whom Thou wast never separated, and Thou didst lift up our nature that lay below, O Master.[^2084]

*Verse: God is gone up in jubilation, the Lord with the sound of the trumpet.* (Ps. 46:6 LXX)  
A cloud of light received Thee into the heights, and the Angels with fear and trembling ministered unto Thy divine Ascension.[^2085]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 4:*  
The nature of Adam that descended into the nethermost parts of the earth, O God Who didst renew it in Thyself, Thou hast today exalted above all principality and power: for having loved it, Thou didst seat it with Thyself; as having had mercy, Thou didst unite it unto Thyself; as having united it, Thou didst suffer with it; as being impassible, having suffered, Thou didst glorify it together with Thyself. But the Bodiless Ones said: Who is this comely Man? Yet not man alone, but God and man, united and manifested! Therefore the Angels, marveling, flying in white garments around the Disciples, and others standing by, cried aloud: Ye men of Galilee, this Jesus Who went away from you, God-man, shall come again as God-man, the Judge of the living and the dead, granting unto the faithful forgiveness of sins and great mercy.[^2086]

## ON TUESDAY AFTER THE SUNDAY OF THE HOLY FATHERS, AT MATINS[^2087]

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Ascension.*

*After the 1st stichology, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*  
From on high Thou camest down to earth, O Christ, and again from earth Thou didst ascend into the heights unto Thy Begetter, while the Disciples beheld Thy Ascension: therefore celebrating together with them, we sing of Thy Ascension, O Savior. *Twice.*[^2088]

*After the 2nd stichology, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*  
The all-comely Word and co-unoriginate, having assumed human nature and deified it mystically, today ascended: the Angels running before showed Him unto the Apostles, going unto the heavens with much glory; and worshiping Him, they said: Glory to God Who hath ascended! *Twice.*[^2089]

*Psalm 50. Canon of the feast with the heirmos to 10; and of the Menaion to 4. Kontakion and exapostilarion of the feast. At the aposticha, stichera.*[^2090]

*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
I will not leave you orphans, whom I have gathered, the Lord spake unto His friends, but I will send you the Holy Spirit.[^2091]

*Verse: All ye nations, clap your hands, shout unto God with a voice of rejoicing.* (Ps. 46:2 LXX)  
The Angels cried aloud unto the all-wise Apostles: O ye men of Galilee, in like manner as ye behold Him, He shall come again.[^2092]

*Verse: God is gone up in jubilation, the Lord with the sound of the trumpet.* (Ps. 46:6 LXX)  
Thy Disciples, O Word, came down with joy from the Mount of Olives, glorifying and praising Thy divine Ascension.[^2093]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 6:*  
When Thou didst ascend in glory, O Christ God, while the Disciples beheld, clouds took Thee up with the flesh; the heavenly gates were lifted up; the choir of Angels rejoiced with gladness; the higher Powers cried aloud, saying: Lift up your gates, ye princes, and the King of glory shall enter in!
