And the Disciples, marveling, said: Depart not from us, O Good Shepherd, but send unto us Thy most Holy Spirit, guiding, confirming, enlightening, and sanctifying our souls.[^2094]

*On Tuesday after the Sunday of the Holy Fathers, the Three-Ode Canon [Tripesnets],*  
*Tone 2. Ode 2. Heirmos:*  
See, see that I am He that saved in the sea, and satisfied the Israelite people in the wilderness, and brought forth water from the rock for men: that taking upon Me him who had fallen of old, I might draw him unto Myself through ineffable mercy.[^2095]

*Troparion:*  
Abiding with the Disciples for forty days after Thy divine Rising from the tomb, O Savior, promising to send them the divine Spirit, Thou didst manifestly ascend in glory, dreadfully borne upon the shoulder of the Cherubim.

*Troparion:*  
Lift up the gates, understand the most glorious things, the noetic Powers cried unto the higher ranks: the King of men, bearing an earthly body, hath come unto us into His rest, having deified mankind through His great tenderheartedness and boundless mercy.[^2096]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
He that makes darkness His secret place, God Who walks upon the wings of the wind, being bodiless in nature, having taken flesh of the Virgin, a radiant cloud, ascended from earth into the heights upon a bright cloud, while the Disciples beheld the glorious Ascension.[^2097]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
O untilled Earth, who didst sprout the Nourisher of all, Who opens His hand and by His favor satisfies every living thing with divine strength: establish with the living Bread our hearts enfeebled by the vain malice of our fallings into sin.[^2098]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Disregarding the golden image, the thrice-blessed youths, beholding the unchanging and living image of God, cried aloud in the midst of the fire: Let all substantial creation hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*  
Having assumed the nature of Adam and deified it, O Word, Thou didst raise it up unto Thy Father, the Primal Source of light, while all the bodiless Ranks were amazed and cried aloud: Let all creation hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*  
Elijah, prefiguring Thy dread Ascension, O Giver of life, was shown forth by the divine Spirit as an armed rider, and was borne away wholly from the earth unto the heights, beneath the Master: let all creation hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
Praise God, shout, ye nations, clap your hands, sing together in harmony: with glory Christ is gone up, whence He came unto us to save those who cry aloud: Let all creation hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^2099]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Triadicon:*  
Let us all glorify the Father, and the Son, and the co-unoriginate Spirit, the uncreated Trinity, united in essence, yet distinguished in three Persons, singing: Let all substantial creation hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^2100]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Every tongue is at a loss to praise thee according to thy worth, and every mind, even of the world above, is dazed to hymn thee, O Theotokos; yet being good, accept our faith, for thou knowest our divine love: for thou art the intercessor of Christians; thee do we magnify.[^2101]

*Troparion:*  
Ascending now in glory upon the clouds, Who then is this? the higher Ranks said unto those crying: Take away the noetic gates! It is God alone, Who hath united earthly things with the heavenly: unto Him let us offer majesty and glory with fear!

*Troparion:*  
Behold, Thou didst say, O Word, unto the Disciples grieving at Thine Ascension: Sit ye waiting in faith, until the divine Comforter comes unto you from on high: the ungrudging fountain of gifts unto all, He shall teach you all things, as He is all-good.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
Our nature that fell in Eden is seen in the highest, established most gloriously upon the Father's Throne, and ever worshiped by Angels and Archangels, by Thrones, Powers, and Principalities: glory to our only God Who was thus well-pleased!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Fair in beauty, the fair Virgin gave birth unto Thee, Whom beholding, O Savior, having neither form nor comeliness when Thou didst suffer, weeping she spake: I marvel beyond mind at Thy humility, O my Son, whereby Thou savest humbled human nature![^2102]

## ON TUESDAY AFTER THE SUNDAY OF THE HOLY FATHERS, IN THE EVENING[^2103]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera prosomoia of the feast, 3; and of the Menaion, 3. Stichera of the feast.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*  
While Thy divine Disciples beheld, Thou didst ascend unto the heavens, and sattest at the right hand of the Begetter, O supersubstantial Savior, never separated from Him; and as Thou didst say, Thou didst send the Comforter, making heavenly out of earthly ones Thy wise, God-proclaiming, and sacred Apostles.

*Sticheron:*  
Thou didst become incarnate for Thine ineffable goodness, O Jesus, and didst willingly endure the Cross and death, O Immortal One; and having risen on the third day from the dead, after forty days Thou didst ascend whence Thou camest down at first, having brought peace to earthly things, and brought all unto the Father.

*Sticheron:*  
When the wise Apostles beheld Thee, O Lord, being taken up upon the clouds, weeping and filled with grief, they cried aloud with tears: Leave not orphans those whom Thou didst love in Thy tenderheartedness, but send upon us, as Thou didst promise, O all-compassionate One, Thy most Holy Spirit, Who enlightens our souls.[^2104]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 5:*  
Come, assembly of the faithful, our minds mystically instructed by the Disciples of Christ, let us speak a ceaseless song, and as the Apostles upon the Mount of Olives, let us cry aloud with David: God is gone up in jubilation, the Lord with the sound of the trumpet, that He might deliver the human race from the snares of the proud one, and enlighten our souls.[^2105]

*At the Aposticha, stichera.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
A newly-befitting wonder! For human nature, having been united unto the Word, God the Ruler of all, hath gone up into the heavens.

*Verse: All ye nations, clap your hands, shout unto God with a voice of rejoicing.* (Ps. 46:2 LXX)  
The clear and all-radiant day of the Master's divine Ascent into the heavens hath shone forth, enlightening all things.

*Verse: God is gone up in jubilation, the Lord with the sound of the trumpet.* (Ps. 46:6 LXX)  
Even as Thou didst send down from on high upon the Disciples Thy connatural Spirit, so also send Thy grace upon Thy people, O Christ Savior.[^2106]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 5:*  
O Lord, as Thou wast being taken up whence Thou wast never separated, the armies of Angels and of all the Bodiless Ones cried with joy unto the higher Powers: Lift up your gates, ye princes, and the King of glory shall enter in! And the Cherubic Throne received Thee with the flesh: O Lord, glory to Thee![^2107]

## ON WEDNESDAY AFTER THE SUNDAY OF THE HOLY FATHERS, AT MATINS[^2108]

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Ascension.*

*After the 1st stichology, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*  
Having gone up again unto Thy Begetter, the Creator of all, leave not Thy servants orphans, the Disciples cried aloud, as they beheld Thee ascending, O Lover of mankind.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*After the 2nd stichology, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*  
Beholding the glorious adornment of the divine Minds, the Lord of glory manifestly ascending in the flesh, they cried one to another: Lift up the gates!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*[^2109]

*And Psalm 50. Canon of the feast of the Ascension, with heirmos to 10; and of the Menaion to 4. Kontakion, and exapostilarion of the feast. At the aposticha, stichera.*[^2110]  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ воскре́слъ є҆сѝ):*  
For the sake of Thine extreme loving-kindness, O Christ, Thou wast born in the flesh of the Virgin; and having willingly endured the Cross, on the third day Thou didst rise from the tomb, O all-compassionate One, raising us up together with Thyself and giving us life.

*Verse: All ye nations, clap your hands, shout unto God with a voice of rejoicing.* (Ps. 46:2 LXX)  
While Thy divine Disciples manifestly beheld Thee, O Christ God, Thou didst ascend from earth to heaven; and Thou Thyself, O Giver of life, didst promise to send from the Father the Holy Spirit.

*Verse: God is gone up in jubilation, the Lord with the sound of the trumpet.* (Ps. 46:6 LXX)  
The multitude of Angels marvels at Thy strange and dread Ascension, O Jesus, King of all, and they cried unto the heavenly Powers to lift up the heavenly gates.[^2111]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 6:*  
Without departing from the Father's bosom, O sweetest Jesus, and having lived among earthly mortals as man, today Thou didst ascend in glory from the Mount of Olives, and having mercifully lifted up our fallen nature, Thou didst seat it together with the Father. Therefore the heavenly Ranks of the Bodiless Ones, marveling at the miracle, were filled with awe, and seized with trembling, they magnified Thy love for mankind; with whom also we earthborn, glorifying Thy condescension toward us and Thine Ascension from us, pray, saying: O Thou Who at Thine Ascension didst fill the Disciples and the Theotokos who bore Thee with boundless joy, vouchsafe also unto us the joy of Thine elect through their supplications, for the sake of Thy great mercy![^2112]

*On Wednesday after the Sunday of the Holy Fathers, the Three-Ode Canon [Tripesnets].*  
*Tone 3. Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
O Thou Who art the confirmation of them that hope in Thee, confirm, O Lord, the Church which Thou hast purchased with Thy precious Blood.

*Troparion:*  
Human nature, condemned and corrupted by transgression, Thou hast led up above the Angels, O Master, as Thou art merciful.

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst ascend into the heavens with glory, while the Bodiless Ones sang praises unto Thee, O Lord, as Creator of all and our God.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
Having ascended into the heavens, O all-good Master, Thou didst send unto Thy Disciples the Comforter, the Spirit of truth.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
The Word, taking flesh from thee, O pure one, came forth, righting the fall of the first-created, through the tender mercy of His compassions.[^2113]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
United with the unbearable fire, the youths championing piety, unhurt by the flame, sang the divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*  
Having accomplished as God the entire dispensation concerning us, Thou didst ascend while Thy Disciples beheld and cried unto Thee in fear: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*  
O ye men of Galilee in truth, why gaze ye thus? the Angels said as the Master ascended unto heaven: In like manner as ye behold Him, this Same shall come again to judge all creation, Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
The right Spirit, the divine Spirit, the Spirit Who is inseparable from Thee, as Thou didst promise, send unto us, O Word of God, borne upon the clouds of glory, the divine Apostles cried aloud, that we may hymn and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages![^2114]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Divine Wisdom, having built for Herself a house from thee, became incarnate through ineffable descent, O Maiden unwedded: for thou alone among all generations wast chosen to be the dwelling of Christ God.[^2115]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
A new and God-befitting wonder! For through the shut virginal gate the Lord manifestly passes; bodiless at the entrance, and God became flesh-bearer at the exit, while the gate remains shut: this Maiden do we ineffably magnify as Mother of God![^2116]

*Troparion:*  
He that ascends with glory in jubilation, as the Prophet said of old, this is the Lord and Master of all: sing unto Him with faith, ye nations, sing, and with trembling glorify Him, for He hath wrought unsearchable and ineffable things!

*Troparion:*  
Unto the sorrowing Disciples Thou didst say: I will not be parted from you for ever, though I go unto the Father Who sent Me: go, teach the whole world, to know Me as true God, Who have wrought most glorious things, which ye have seen and heard!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
Peace unto all! Thou didst say, O Christ, and straightway a cloud of light having taken Thee up, while all beheld, Thou didst ascend from earth; and having gone up, Thou sattest above every principality and power: therefore we glorify Thy much-hymned power.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Thou hast truly been shown forth as the Receptacle of Light, for from thee God became incarnate ineffably without change, exalting Adam's nature upon the Cross, and becoming the firstborn among the dead; and God remains in the highest, glorified together with the Father and the Divine Spirit.[^2117]

## ON WEDNESDAY AFTER THE SUNDAY OF THE HOLY FATHERS, IN THE EVENING[^2118]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera prosomoia of the feast, 3; and of the Menaion, 3. Of the feast.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*  
Having accomplished salvation for all, Thou camest unto the Mount of Olives, O Christ, whence Thou wast also taken up before Thy Disciples, borne into the heavens with glory; therefore marveling at the mystery, the lower Ranks cried unto the celestial ones: Lift up the gates, and He that reigns over all as God shall enter in whence He came forth, as He was well-pleased, having wrought most gloriously salvation for the world!

*Sticheron:*  
The choir of Disciples, as they beheld Thee being taken up, spake: O Master, how dost Thou now leave Thy servants? And whither goest Thou, Who holdest the ends of the earth in Thy hands? But having left all things, we followed Thee as God, rejoicing, having hope to be with Thee unto the ages: leave us not orphans, as Thou didst promise, O our compassionate Savior; send the Comforter, the Savior of our souls![^2119]

*Sticheron:*  
Giving the most perfect blessing unto Thy friends, O Master, Thou didst mystically instruct them: Behold, friends, I go unto the Father, and unto you I will send another Comforter; cast away all sorrow: for I leave not the sheep whom I have gathered, neither will I forget those whom I loved; clad with divine power from on high, go ye, proclaiming unto all nations the glad tidings of salvation!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 6:*  
God is gone up in jubilation, the Lord with the sound of the trumpet, to lift up the fallen image of Adam, and to send the Spirit, the Comforter, to sanctify our souls.[^2120]

*At the Aposticha, stichera.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
I will not leave you orphans, whom I have gathered, the Lord spake unto His friends: but I will send you the most Holy Spirit.

*Verse: All ye nations, clap your hands, shout unto God with a voice of rejoicing.* (Ps. 46:2 LXX)  
The Angels cried unto the all-wise Apostles: O ye men of Galilee, in like manner as ye behold Him, He shall come again.

*Verse: God is gone up in jubilation, the Lord with the sound of the trumpet.* (Ps. 46:6 LXX)  
Thy Disciples, O Word, came down with joy from the Mount of Olives, glorifying and singing of Thy divine Ascension.[^2121]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 1:*  
Having ascended into the heavens, whence also Thou didst descend, leave us not orphans, O Lord: let Thy Spirit come, bringing peace unto the world; show forth unto the sons of men the works of Thy power, O Lord, Lover of mankind![^2122]

*Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace."*

*And the Dismissal.*

## ON THURSDAY AFTER THE SUNDAY OF THE HOLY FATHERS, AT MATINS[^2123]

*After the 1st stichology, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*  
O Thou Who didst descend from heaven unto earthly things, and as God didst raise up with Thyself the form of Adam lying below in the prison of hades, and by Thine Ascension, O Christ, didst lead it up unto the heavens and make it to sit together with Thee upon the Father's Throne, as Thou art merciful and the Lover of mankind.[^2124]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*After the 2nd stichology, Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*  
Having gone up upon the Mount of Olives, O merciful Jesus, Thou wast taken up thence: for a cloud of light took Thee up from the earth, while Thy Disciples beheld a dread wonder, and the Bodiless Ones cried unto the highest armies in the heavens: With fear let the gates be lifted up! With whom all creation praises Thee, the King and God of all.[^2125]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*And Psalm 50. Canon of the feast, with heirmos to 10; and of the Menaion to 4. Kontakion, and exapostilarion of the feast. At the aposticha, stichera.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
Having fulfilled the Father's good pleasure, O Good One, and having united things on high with things below, Thou didst ascend in glory unto those who were first.

*Verse: All ye nations, clap your hands, shout unto God with a voice of rejoicing.* (Ps. 46:2 LXX)  
A cloud of light received Thee into the heights, and the Angels with fear and trembling ministered unto Thy divine Ascension.

*Verse: God is gone up in jubilation, the Lord with the sound of the trumpet.* (Ps. 46:6 LXX)  
Together with the Apostles the Theotokos sings a God-befitting praise, beholding the Lord borne upon the clouds.[^2126]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 7:*  
Thou camest unto the Mount of Olives, O Thou Who hast mercy upon the human race, and a cloud took Thee up from the eyes of Thy Disciples, who trembled at the vision, yet rejoiced at the expectation of the Holy Spirit, by Whom having confirmed us, O Savior, have mercy on us![^2127]

*On Thursday after the Sunday of the Holy Fathers, the Three-Ode Canon [Tripesnets].*  
*Tone 4. Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
Sitting in glory upon the Throne of the Godhead, upon a light cloud, came Jesus the most divine, with His incorrupt hand, and saved them that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^2128]

*Troparion:*  
Inaccessible in nature, becoming accessible unto me, being clothed in me, Thou didst enlighten my whole nature, and by Thine Ascension didst lead it up above every principality and power.

*Troparion:*  
The chariot-driver Elijah traversed the air, prefiguring of old Thine Ascension, O Word of God: for as upon a chariot, O Savior, borne by a cloud, Thou didst reach the Father's bosom.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
As the Disciples gazed upon the dread taking up, and marveled, the divine Angels proclaimed: Ye men of Galilee, as ye behold Him, so shall Jesus come again to judge all.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Having renewed human nature, O all-pure one, which was crushed and bowed down under corruption, by taking flesh of God, and having given birth beyond nature, O unwedded Maiden, unto Him we cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^2129]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
The Childbearing of the Theotokos saved the pious children in the furnace, then indeed prefigured, but now in actuality, it stirs up the whole world to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*  
Walking upon the wings of the wind, borne by a cloud, He is now received into the heavenly realms where He was before, and sends the Comforter unto the Disciples who praise and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*  
Having willingly endured Crucifixion and burial, Thou didst rise, O All-powerful One, and appearing unto them that love Thee during forty days, Thou didst make them joyful; then having blessed them, Thou didst ascend unto Thy Father with glory.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
Having manifestly taught things beyond words unto the Theologians, O Word, and having ascended, Thou Thyself didst send, as Thou didst promise, the all-working and co-enthroned Spirit unto them that sing: Hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
O shut gate of God, through which the Most High alone passed, direct me unto the divine paths, and open unto me the gates of salvation, O full of God's grace: for unto thee do I flee, O Virgin, the only protectress of the human race.[^2130]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
The cornerstone, Christ, was cut out without hands from the unhewn mountain, from thee, O Virgin, uniting the natures that were separated: therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos.[^2131]

*Troparion:*  
Until ye are endued with power from on high, Thou didst say unto the Disciples: Sit ye gathered together: for I am already taken up, and I will send unto you the Comforter Who is co-enthroned with the Father.[^2132]

*Troparion:*  
Come mystically, having all gone up unto the height of vision, with spiritual eyes let us behold the Word ascending and sitting at the right hand, co-enthroned with the Father, co-everlasting.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
The Master, having shone forth as the fair Sun from the tomb, like light-bearing rays unto the whole world, sent you forth as Apostles, shedding light and driving away the gloom.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Spare me, O Christ, when Thou comest to judge the world with glory, dispersing the gloom of my passions, through the supplications of her that bore Thee, and make me an inheritor of Thy heavenly Kingdom.[^2133]

## ON THURSDAY OF THE SEVENTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA, IN THE EVENING[^2134]
*Apodosis [Apodosis] of the Feast of the Ascension.*[^2135]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we chant the entire office of the Feast unchanged, even as on the divine day of the Ascension, except for the readings and the Polyeleos, both at Vespers and at Matins on Friday; and at the Liturgy the Beatitudes: the sixth ode of the Feast from both canons, to 8.*

*After the Entrance, the Troparion. Glory, both now: Kontakion.*

*Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion Verse of the Feast; but the Epistle and Gospel of the day.*

*The service of the Menaion we chant at Compline.*

*On Friday of the Seventh Week after Pascha, the Three-Ode Canon [Tripesnets].*  
*Tone 6. Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Early in the morning do I rise unto Thee Who for Thy mercy's sake didst empty Thyself without change, and didst impassibly submit unto the passions, O Word of God: grant peace unto me who am fallen, O Lover of mankind![^2136]

*Troparion:*  
Having overthrown the dominion of death by divine power, Thou didst rise on the third day, O my Christ, and didst ascend with glory unto the heavenly realms, while Thy Disciples sang praises unto Thee, God and Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*  
Leave us not orphans, O Compassionate One, but as Thou didst promise, send Thy Holy Spirit: Thy Disciples cried aloud unto the Deliverer, when they beheld Thee ascending from the earth, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
Lo, the Spirit is about to come down upon earth in tongues of fire, that He may manifestly reveal as fiery the clay-formed, honorable Disciples of the Word, who proclaim His love for mankind.[^2137]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Having clothed Thyself in human nature from the unwedded Maiden, O Word, for the sake of Thine extreme loving-kindness, Thou hast given her unto us who believe Thee to be God and man as a firm wall and shelter for ever.[^2138]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Enduring peril for their fathers' laws, the blessed youths in Babylon spat upon the senseless decree of the ruler; and united, unconsumed by the fire, they raised a worthy song to the Almighty: Praise the Lord, ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^2139]

*Troparion:*  
Having united what was divided by Thy goodness, O all-good Word, and having smitten the adversary by the Passion of Thy flesh, Thou didst ascend in glory unto the Father, from Whom Thou wast never separated, while the Disciples beheld: therefore with them we bless Thee, and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages.[^2140]

*Troparion:*  
Having had mercy, O Savior, upon those whom Thou didst fashion, mistreated by the cruelty of the enemy, Thou didst ascend from the Mount of Olives, O Master, while Thy Disciples beheld and marveled how a cloud took Thee up with the flesh, as it were a Cherubic Throne: therefore with them we bless Thee, and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages.[^2141]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
I will not leave you orphans, O sons and heirs of My Kingdom; but having ascended, I will send down from on high the Comforter, Who instructs you in secret mysteries beyond understanding and mind, and Who reveals you, clay-formed mortals, as fiery by participation: so didst Thou cry unto Thy Disciples, O Compassionate One: therefore with them we hymn Thee, and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages.[^2142]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Apart from the laws of nature, O Virgin, thou bearest the Lawgiver Who takes away the shadow of the Law and grants the brightness of grace: therefore by the law of sin, O Pure One, deliver me who am now sorely overcome, that I may sing: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^2143]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Every tongue is at a loss to praise thee according to thy worth, and every mind, even of the world above, is dazed to hymn thee, O Theotokos; yet being good, accept our faith, for thou knowest our divine love: for thou art the intercessor of Christians; thee do we magnify.[^2144]

*Troparion:*  
Having pitied our rejected and mistreated nature, as Thou alone art merciful and the Lover of mankind, O Benefactor, Thou didst take upon Thyself human flesh, and having revealed the strong one by Thy Passion, Thou didst ascend on high unto the Father, O Christ, filling all things with glory.[^2145]

*Troparion:*  
Why grieve ye? Why are ye astonished? the Angels cried unto the Apostles, as they stood with them upon the mountain: This Jesus Whom ye behold taken up, shall come again thus in great glory, to judge the inhabited earth and to render unto all according to their deeds.[^2146]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Troparion:*  
Having given peace unto Thy sacred Disciples, and having blessed all with Thy Spirit, O Compassionate One, as Benefactor keep them unharmed from the evil one, that having done Thy will, O Christ, we may all together be accounted worthy of Thy Kingdom.[^2147]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Receptacle of the Light that hath enlightened all creation, thou wast shown forth alone most pure, all-immaculate Mary, Theotokos: therefore I beseech thee: enlighten my passionate soul, darkened by the pleasures of life, that as in the day I may walk in the divine paths.[^2148]

## ON SATURDAY BEFORE PENTECOST (OF SOULS), AT VESPERS ON FRIDAY EVENING[^2149]

*In which we perform the commemoration of all Orthodox Christians from the ages fallen asleep in piety, our fathers and brethren.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 stichera: Martyr stichera of the Octoechos, 3.*  
*Tone 6:*  
Thy Martyrs, O Lord, did not deny Thee, nor depart from Thy commandments: through their intercessions have mercy on us.

*Sticheron:*  
They that suffered for Thy sake, O Christ, endured many torments: by their prayers and supplication, O Lord, preserve us all.

*Sticheron:*  
The passion-bearing Martyrs, and citizens of heaven, who suffered upon earth, endured many torments: and they have received a perfect crown in the heavens, that they may intercede for our souls.[^2150]

*And 3 stichera of the departed.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "The Paradise in Eden" (И҆́же во Є҆де́мѣ):*  
Performing today the memory of all the dead from the ages by name, who lived in faith and piety, let us, the faithful, hymn the Savior and Lord, earnestly beseeching that in the hour of judgment a good answer be given unto them before our very God, Who judges all the earth; that they may obtain with joy a place at His right hand, in the portion of the righteous and in the radiant lot of the saints, and be made worthy of His heavenly Kingdom.[^2151]

*Sticheron:*  
O Savior, Who hast redeemed mankind by Thy Blood, and by Thy death hast delivered us from bitter death, and grantest unto us eternal life by Thy Resurrection: give rest, O Lord, unto all who have fallen asleep in piety, whether in deserts, or in cities, or in the sea, or on land, or in any place: emperors, priests, hierarchs, monastics, and laymen of every age of the whole human race, and vouchsafe them Thy heavenly Kingdom.[^2152]

*Sticheron:*  
By Thy Resurrection from the dead, O Christ, death no longer rules over those who have fallen asleep in piety; therefore we earnestly pray: Give rest unto Thy servants in Thy courts and in the bosom of Abraham, who from Adam even unto this day have purely served Thee: our fathers and brethren, friends and kinsmen together, every person who in life served faithfully and hath departed unto Thee in diverse manners and fashions, O God, and account them worthy to obtain Thy heavenly Kingdom.[^2153]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 8:*  
I weep and lament when I contemplate death, and behold our beauty, created according to the image of God, lying in the graves, formless, inglorious, having no shape. O wonder! What is this mystery that hath come to pass concerning us? How have we been surrendered unto corruption? How have we been yoked unto death? Truly by the command of God, as it is written, Who grants rest unto the departed.[^2154]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion. Tone 6:*  
Who shall not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin? Who will not hymn thy birthgiving without defilement? For the only-begotten Son, Who shone forth timelessly from the Father, came forth from thee, O Pure One, having become ineffably incarnate: being God by nature, and having become man by nature for our sake: not divided into two persons, but known in two natures without confusion. Him do thou beseech, O pure, all-blessed Maiden, to have mercy on our souls.[^2155]

*O Gladsome Light:*  
*Instead of the prokeimenon we sing: Alleluia, in Tone 8.*  
*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord.* (Ps. 64:5 LXX)  
*Verse: And their remembrance is unto generation and generation.* (Ps. 101:13 LXX)  
*Vouchsafe, O Lord:*

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 6.*  
*Martyricon:*  
Thy Cross, O Lord, was an invincible weapon unto the Martyrs: for beholding the death that lay before them, and foreseeing the life to come, they were strengthened by hope in Thee: by their prayers have mercy on us.[^2156]

*For the departed.*  
*Verse: Their souls shall dwell in prosperity.* (Ps. 24:13 LXX)  
Thou didst honor me with Thine image, O Savior, the work of Thy hands, having depicted in a material form the likeness of noetic essence, whereof Thou didst also make me a partaker, appointing me by the sovereign authority of reason to hold dominion over earthly things: therefore, O Savior, give rest unto Thy servants in the land of the living, in the tents of the righteous.[^2157]

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord.* (Ps. 64:5 LXX)  
That the inheritance of the rest of life might be determined for me, Thou didst plant a garden in Eden adorned with every kind of orchard, making me free from sorrow and care, a partaker of divine life, an earthly angel, created as a composite being: therefore, O Savior, give rest unto Thy servants in the land of the living, in the tents of the righteous.[^2158]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 6:*  
Thy creative command was my beginning and foundation: for wishing to compose me as a living creature out of invisible and visible nature, Thou didst fashion my body from the earth, and gavest me a soul by Thy divine and life-creating inbreathing: therefore, O Savior, give rest unto Thy servants in the land of the living, and in the tents of the righteous.[^2159]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
By the prayers of her that bore Thee, O Christ, and of Thy Martyrs, Apostles, Prophets, Hierarchs, Venerable ones, and Righteous ones, and of all the saints, give rest unto Thy departed servants.[^2160]

*Dismissal Troparion, Tone 8:*  
O Thou Who with wisdom profound mercifully orderest all things, and providest unto all what is profitable, O only Creator: give rest, O Lord, unto the souls of Thy servants; for they have placed their hope in Thee, our Maker and Fashioner and God.[^2161]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Thee do we have as a wall and a haven, and an acceptable intercessor before God Whom thou didst bear, O unwedded Theotokos, salvation of the faithful.[^2162]

*And the dismissal takes place.*

*After the dismissal of Vespers, we perform in the narthex a PANIKHIDA for the departed:*[^2163]  
*Then the priest makes:* **Priest:** Blessed is our God...  
*And we say Psalm 90:* He that dwells in the help of the Most High... (Ps. 90 LXX)  
*Alleluia, thrice.*  
*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord.* (Ps. 64:5 LXX)  
*Then the troparion:* O Thou Who with wisdom profound...  
*And the Theotokion:* Thee do we have as a wall...  
*Then the funeral troparia:* Blessed art Thou, O Lord...  
*And the priest commemorates all our fathers and brethren who have previously fallen asleep; and the prayer.*  
*And after the exclamation, Psalm 50.*  
*And straightway we sing the funeral canon of the Octoechos in the cemetery chapel, Tone 6.*  
*Trisagion, All-holy Trinity: after Our Father: troparion:* O Thou Who with wisdom profound...  
*And the Theotokion:* Thee do we have as a wall...  
*And the priest commemorates as usual.*  
*And the dismissal.*[^2164]

## ON SATURDAY OF SOULS, AT MATINS[^2165]

*After the Six Psalms, Alleluia, in Tone 8, with its verses.*  
*Troparion:*  
O Thou Who with wisdom profound mercifully orderest all things, and providest unto all what is profitable, O only Creator: give rest, O Lord, unto the souls of Thy servants; for they have placed their hope in Thee, our Maker and Fashioner and God.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion, the same Tone:*  
Thee do we have as a wall and a haven, and an acceptable intercessor before God Whom thou didst bear, O unwedded Theotokos, salvation of the faithful.[^2166]

*Then the customary stichology: The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand (Ps. 109 LXX). And at all the litanies of the stichology and of the canon, all our departed fathers and brethren are commemorated.*[^2167]

*After the 1st stichology we say funeral and martyr sessional hymns, Tone 6.*  
The martyrs' resistances in the contest, the tormentors' wounds upon the Martyrs! And the choirs of the Bodiless Ones were standing by, holding the awards of victory: the all-wise ones astonished torturers and emperors; they cast down Belial by the confession of Christ: O Lord Who didst strengthen them, glory to Thee!

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.* (Ps. 67:36 LXX)  
Having endured the contest of martyrdom, O holy ones, and received from Thee the awards of victory, they abolished the cunning devices of the lawless, and received crowns of incorruption: through them, O God, save our souls.[^2168]

*Verse: The righteous cried, and the Lord gave ear to them.* (Ps. 33:18 LXX)  
The remembrance of Thy saints, O Lord, is shown forth like the garden in Eden: for in it all creation rejoices: and grant unto us by their prayers peace and great mercy.[^2169]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. For the departed:*  
In the tents of the elect, and in the land of the living, place those who have fallen asleep in piety, whom Thou hast received, O Jesus, as the God Who is easily entreated; and fill them with unwaning light, and account them worthy of Thine eternal joy.[^2170]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Calling Thy Mother blessed, Thou camest to the Passion by Thy voluntary will, shining forth upon the Cross, desiring to seek out Adam, saying unto the Angels: Rejoice with Me, for the lost drachma is found: O our God, Who hast wisely ordered all things, glory to Thee![^2171]

*Then: Blessed are the blameless (Ps. 118 LXX): In two stases.*[^2172]  
*Funeral Troparia, Tone 5:*  
*Refrain: Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.* (Ps. 118:12 LXX)  
The choir of the Saints hath found the fountain of life and the door of paradise: let me also find the way through repentance: I am the lost sheep, call me back, O Savior, and save me.

*Refrain: Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.* (Ps. 118:12 LXX)  
Ye who preached the Lamb of God, and were slain as lambs, and have passed unto life ageless and everlasting, O holy ones: Him earnestly beseech, O Martyrs, to grant us remission of our debts.[^2173]

*Refrain: Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.* (Ps. 118:12 LXX)  
Ye who walked the narrow way of sorrow, all ye who in life took up the Cross as a yoke, and followed Me by faith: come, enjoy the awards and heavenly crowns which I have prepared for you.

*Refrain: Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.* (Ps. 118:12 LXX)  
I am an image of Thine ineffable glory, though I bear the wounds of transgressions: have compassion upon Thy creature, O Master, and cleanse me by Thy tenderheartedness; and grant unto me the longed-for homeland, making me again a citizen of paradise.[^2174]

*Refrain: Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.* (Ps. 118:12 LXX)  
O Thou Who of old didst fashion me from non-existence, and honored me with Thy divine image, but because of the transgression of the commandment didst return me again unto the earth whence I was taken: lead me back unto that which is according to Thy likeness, that I may be refashioned in my ancient beauty.

*Refrain: Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.* (Ps. 118:12 LXX)  
Give rest, O God, unto Thy servants, and place them in paradise, where the choirs of the Saints, O Lord, and the righteous shine as the stars; give rest unto Thy departed servants, overlooking all their transgressions.[^2175]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadicon:*  
The tri-radiant light of the one Godhead let us piously hymn, crying aloud: Holy art Thou, O unoriginate Father, co-unoriginate Son, and divine Spirit; enlighten us who serve Thee in faith, and snatch us from the eternal fire.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, O pure one, who didst bear God in the flesh for the salvation of all, through whom the human race hath found salvation: through thee may we find paradise, O pure and blessed Theotokos.

*Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia: Glory to Thee, O God. Thrice.*[^2176]

*After this, the aforesaid litany for the departed: Again and again: And we: Lord, have mercy (40). The priest reads the prayer: O God of spirits and of all flesh:*[^2177]  
*And after the exclamation, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, for the departed:*  
Give rest, O our Savior, unto Thy servants with the righteous, and establish them in Thy courts, as it is written, overlooking, as Thou art good, their transgressions, voluntary and involuntary, and all things done in knowledge and in ignorance, O Lover of mankind.[^2178]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
O Christ God, Who didst shine forth unto the world from the Virgin, and through her hast revealed sons of light, have mercy on us.[^2179]

*Psalm 50.*  
*Canon of the patron saint of the monastery, with the heirmos to 6; and the present canon to 8.*  
*Canon, the composition of the Studite, having the acrostic in the Triadica and Theotokia: "Of the unworthy Theodore."*[^2180]  
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Let us send up a song, O ye peoples, unto our marvelous God, Who delivered Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master![^2181]

*Troparion:*  
Let us all pray unto Christ, performing today the memory of the dead from the ages, that He may deliver from the eternal fire those who have fallen asleep in faith and in the hope of eternal life.

*Troparion:*  
By the depth of Thy judgments, O Christ, in all-wise manner Thou hast preordained the end of life for each, its term and manner: therefore, O all-compassionate One, save at the judgment those whom the grave hath covered in every land.[^2182]

*Troparion:*  
Our life is a shadow: therefore, O Lord, show forth those who have fallen asleep from the night of this life to be sons of the day that knows no evening: Orthodox priests, emperors, and all Thy people.[^2183]

*Troparion:*  
Those whom water covered, and war mowed down, whom earthquake engulfed, whom murderers slew, and whom fire consumed, being believers, O Merciful One, place in the portion of the righteous.[^2184]

*Troparion:*  
Overlooking all our carnal debts, O our Savior, at every age of all the race of men, set them uncondemned before Thy judgment seat, as they make answer unto Thee, the Creator.[^2185]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadicon:*  
I sing of the three Persons of one essence, self-hypostatic, the unbegotten Father, the begotten Son, and the Holy Spirit: unoriginate Kingdom, dominion, one Godhead.[^2186]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Truly thou hast been shown forth as a Heaven upon earth, greater than the highest heaven, O unwedded Virgin: for from thee the Sun of righteousness hath shone forth in the world, reigning in righteousness.[^2187]

*Katavasia: Let us send up a song...*  
*Ode 2. Heirmos:*  
See, see that I am your God, begotten of the Father before the ages, and conceived in these latter times without a husband from the Virgin, Who have destroyed the sin of forefather Adam, as Lover of mankind.[^2188]

*Troparion:*  
See, see that I am your God, Who by righteous judgment hast fixed the bounds of life, and Who receivest from corruption unto incorruption all who have fallen asleep in the hope of eternal resurrection.[^2189]

*Troparion:*  
O Lord, Who from the four corners of the earth receivest those who have faithfully fallen asleep: whether in the sea, or on land, or in rivers, springs, or lakes, or in pits, or who became food for beasts, and birds, and creeping things: give rest unto all.[^2190]

*Troparion:*  
All things are in Thy palm, O Lord: having foreordained those who were dissolved into the four elements, gather them together at Thy coming, resurrect them, forgiving them all their transgressions done in ignorance and in knowledge.[^2191]

*Troparion:*  
O Thy dread Second Coming, O Lord! For when Thou comest upon earth in the likeness of lightning, Thou shalt raise up all Thy creation to be judged: account those who then lived in faith toward Thee, having gone forth to meet Thee, worthy to be with Thee.[^2192]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadicon:*  
All-perfect Unity, supersubstantial, tripersonal: unbegotten Father, and only-begotten Son, and Spirit proceeding from the Father and manifested through the Son: one Essence and Nature, Lordship, Kingdom: save all of us.[^2193]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Ineffable is the wonder of thy birthgiving, O Mother and Virgin: for how dost thou both give birth and yet remainest pure? How dost thou bear a Child, and knowest no experience of man at all? As the Word of God born of me knows, Who was incarnate beyond nature.[^2194]

*Katavasia: See, see...*  
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
O Word of God, Who with Thy hand didst establish the heavens, by the enlightenment of Thine authentic knowledge establish also our hearts that hope in Thee.[^2195]

*Troparion:*  
O God, vouchsafe those who have completed the course of this life in pious glory to be adorned with the crown of the righteous, and to enjoy eternal good things.

*Troparion:*  
Give rest, O God, unto those suddenly snatched away, those consumed by lightnings, and those frozen by frost, and those struck down by every kind of wound, when Thou shalt try all things with fire.[^2196]

*Troparion:*  
O Christ, vouchsafe those who have crossed the ever-surging sea of life, piloted by an Orthodox life, to run into the incorruptible haven of Thy life.

*Troparion:*  
Those whom every nature of sea beasts and birds of heaven received as food, by Thy judgments, O Christ, raise up with glory on the last day, O God.[^2197]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadicon:*  
Noetically dividing the divine Unity into three Persons simply, I unite what is indivisible: for like the speed of lightning, the three-radiant light is seen in unity.[^2198]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Incomprehensible is thy wonder: for thou bearest a Child without a husband, and thou keepest thy virginity, O Pure One, having given birth: therefore the host of Angels and the human race hymn thee unto the ages.[^2199]

*Katavasia: O Word of God, Who with Thy hand didst establish the heavens...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*  
O Thou Who for our sake didst endure the Cross and death, and didst put hades to death, and didst raise the dead together with Thyself: give rest unto those departed from us, O Savior, as Thou art the Lover of mankind and God; and at Thy dread and trembling Coming, O Giver of life, as Thou hast a multitude of mercy, account them worthy of Thy Kingdom.[^2200]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, the same; both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Show forth thy speedy protection and help and mercy upon thy servants, and calm the waves of vain thoughts, O Pure One, and raise up my fallen soul, O Theotokos: for I know, yea, I know, O Virgin, that thou canst do whatsoever thou desirest.[^2201]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
From the overshadowing mountain, O Word, even the only Theotokos, the Prophet with God-enlightened vision beheld Thee about to become incarnate, and with fear glorified Thy power.[^2202]

*Troparion:*  
Fathers and forefathers, grandfathers and great-grandfathers, from the first even unto the last, who died in lawful order and right faith: remember them all, O our Savior.[^2203]

*Troparion:*  
Those who left this life in faith in the mountain, on the way, in desert places: monastics and laymen, youths and elders: establish them with the saints, O Christ.[^2204]

*Troparion:*  
Those who unexpectedly passed away from sorrow or from joy, who nevertheless exchanged this life in faith, who suffered in prosperity or in adversity: give rest unto all, O our Savior.[^2205]

*Troparion:*  
Those whom the sword slew, or whom a horse dragged away, whom hail, snow, or abundant rain destroyed; whom a falling tile crushed, or earth buried: give rest, O Christ our Savior.[^2206]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadicon:*  
Strange it is that the Godhead is one and three, wholly indivisible in each Person: for the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit are worshipped as one God.[^2207]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Direct us, O Virgin, by thy prayers, steering those tossed by the wave of sin unto the havens of salvation, O Mother of God, delivering us from all grievous evils.[^2208]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
My spirit seeks Thee early at dawn, O God, for the commandments of Thy coming are light: wherewith enlighten our mind, O Master, and guide us into the path of life.[^2209]

*Troparion:*  
Performing today the memory of every one of the dead who have fallen asleep in piety from the ages, O Lord, we earnestly cry unto Thee: Give rest unto all with Thy saints.[^2210]

*Troparion:*  
Whomsoever Thou hast received of every race, among emperors, princes, or monastics who lived in the Orthodox faith, O Compassionate One, deliver them from eternal torment.[^2211]

*Troparion:*  
Knowing what is profitable for all whom Thou hast fashioned, unto whomsoever Thou didst permit, O Lord, to die unexpectedly by sudden falls: deliver them from every punishment, O our God.[^2212]

*Troparion:*  
From the ever-burning fire, and from unillumined darkness, from the gnashing of teeth, and the never-ending tormenting worm, and from all torture, deliver all who have died in faith, O our Savior.[^2213]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadicon:*  
O co-enthroned, unoriginate, tripersonal Unity, possessing unity by division, and by contrast a single nature in Persons: unite us unto the single desire of Thy commandments.[^2214]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Thou hast been shown forth more honorable than the fiery Seraphim, O Pure One, having borne Him Who is inaccessible unto them, Jesus the Savior, Who by incarnation hath deified the composite nature of the earthborn.[^2215]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Held fast by many sins, receive me, O Lover of mankind, who fall down before Thy tender mercies, as the Prophet, O Lord, and save me.[^2216]

*Troparion:*  
Having loosed the pangs of death, having suffered, O Prince of life, our God: give rest unto Thy servants who have fallen asleep from the ages.[^2217]

*Troparion:*  
By Thine ineffable judgments, give rest with the saints, O Lord, unto those whom magical potions, poisons, or choking upon bones have killed.[^2218]

*Troparion:*  
When Thou shalt judge all things, as all stand naked and uncovered before Thy face, then spare those who have faithfully served Thee, O Compassionate One, O God.[^2219]

*Troparion:*  
When Thine Archangel shall sound the final trumpet unto the resurrection of life for all, then, O Christ, give rest unto Thy servants.[^2220]

*Troparion:*  
Account the faithful of all the human race whom Thou hast received from the ages worthy, O God, to glorify Thee unto the ages with those who serve Thee.[^2221]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Triadicon:*  
O thrice-holy, co-enthroned Godhead, Father, Son, with the Holy Spirit: Thou art my God, Who upholdest all things by Thine almighty power.[^2222]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
From thy root the Flower of life hath blossomed, leap for joy, O forefather Jesse: Christ God Who saves the world, born of the pure Maiden.[^2223]

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*  
With the saints give rest, O Christ, unto the souls of Thy servants, where there is neither sickness, nor sorrow, nor sighing, but life everlasting.[^2224]

*Ikos:*  
Thou alone art immortal, Who hast made and fashioned man: for we mortals were fashioned from the earth, and unto the same earth shall we go, as Thou didst command, Who didst fashion me and say unto me: Earth thou art, and unto the earth shalt thou return: whither all we mortals go, making our funeral lamentation the song: Alleluia.[^2225]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
O Thou Who in the beginning didst lay the foundations of the earth, and by Thy word didst establish the heavens: blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers.[^2226]

*Troparion:*  
Performing the memory of those who have fallen asleep in piety from the ages, we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers.[^2227]

*Troparion:*
Those who have died suddenly in piety, and those who have fallen from every precipice, from wood, from iron, or from any stone: give rest, O God, unto the departed faithful.[^2228]

*Troparion:*
At Thy dread Coming, O Compassionate One, place at the right hand of Thy sheep those who in life served Thee in an Orthodox manner, O Christ, and have departed unto Thee.[^2229]

*Troparion:*
In the choir of Thine elect, O Christ, appoint Thy servants to cry unto Thee: Blessed art Thou unto ages, O Lord, God of our fathers.[^2230]

*Troparion:*
O Compassionate Savior, Who didst fashion dust from the earth and quicken the flesh by the Spirit: give rest in the unfading life, O God, unto those whom Thou hast received.[^2231]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Triadicon:*
Even as by three suns, let the Godhead be hymned by a single blending of light: the Father, and the Son, and the Divine Spirit, one in essence, yet three in Hypostases.[^2232]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:*
In concord we sing unto thee a virginal hymn, calling thee the Mountain of God, O Virgin, wherein the Word, having dwelt in the flesh, spiritually deified us unto Himself.[^2233]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and by fire in the bush revealed unto Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin: praise the Lord unto all ages.[^2234]

*Troparion:*
O Lord of glory, Who didst formerly destroy the shadow of death, shining forth like the sun from the tomb: make all who have died in the faith sons of Thy Resurrection unto the ages.[^2235]

*Troparion:*
O Witness of secret and hidden things, when Thou shalt reveal the works of darkness and the counsels of our hearts, do not enter into strict judgment with all who have fallen asleep in faith.[^2236]

*Troparion:*
When Thou shalt sit upon the throne and command those gathered by the trumpet from the ends of the earth to stand for judgment: then spare all, O Christ, as Thou art merciful.[^2237]

*Troparion:*
Unto those who have died suddenly by accidents, by violent shouting, by swift rushing, by blows, by strangulation, or by hanging, O Lord of glory: grant relief unto the ages unto those who have fallen asleep in faith.[^2238]

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*  
*Triadicon:*
As Monad in essence I hymn Thee; as Trinity in Persons I honor Thee, O Father, and Son, and Most Holy Spirit: I glorify the unoriginate dominion of Thy Kingdom unto the ages.[^2239]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:*
Thou hast been shown forth as a sealed Fountain of living waters, O Virgin Theotokos: for having borne the Lord without a man, thou waterest the faithful with the water of immortality unto the ages.[^2240]

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, hymning and supreme exalting Him unto all ages.*  
*Heirmos:* Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain...

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
The Birthgiving of the Ever-Virgin, revealed on the mountain unto the Lawgiver in fire and the bush for the salvation of us, the faithful: with unceasing hymns we magnify.[^2241]

*Troparion:*
Where the dwelling of Thy Saints that rejoice is found, O Lord: vouchsafe all who from the ages have fallen asleep in faith and hope to rejoice.[^2242]

*Troparion:*
Those who have died by the divine threat, by death-bearing thunders of all kinds cast down from heaven, the earth having split asunder, or the sea having surged: give rest, O Christ, unto all the faithful.[^2243]

*Troparion:*
Every age, the aged and young infants, children and sucklings of milk, the male nature and the female: give rest, O God, unto the faithful whom Thou hast received.[^2244]

*Troparion:*
Those who have died from poisonous bites, from being swallowed by serpents, from trampling by horses, or from strangling and hanging by a neighbor: give rest unto those who in faith have served Thee.[^2245]

*Troparion:*
Vouchsafe each one by name who from the ages and from generation of generations hath died in the faith, to stand uncondemned before Thee, O Christ, at Thy Coming.[^2246]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:*  
*Triadicon:*
O God, One in Trinity, glory unto Thee unceasingly: for though each is God, yet One in essence are Father, Son, and Spirit, with thrice-radiant personal properties.[^2247]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:*
Beyond understanding is thy Childbearing: for thou barest Him Who was before the ages, and ineffably feedest with milk the Nourisher of the world, and holdest Christ Who upholds all things, our only Redeemer, O All-Immaculate One.[^2248]

*Then:* *It is truly meet to bless thee, the Theotokos...*

*Exapostilarion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Having adorned the heavens with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*  
Celebrating the memory of all who have fallen asleep in Christ, let us, the faithful, remember the final Day, ever beseeching Christ both for ourselves and for them.[^2249]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Theotokion:*
Sweetness of Angels, joy of the sorrowful, protectress of Christians, O Virgin Mother of the Lord: defend us and deliver us from eternal torments.[^2250]

*At the Praises, let every breath praise the Lord. We set 4 stichera, and sing the following stichera:*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Despairing on account of her life" (Ѿча́ѧннаѧ):*  
Dread is the end of death, and terrible is the judgment of the Master: for unquenchable fire is prepared there, a never-sleeping, gnawing worm, gnashing of teeth, outer darkness, and eternal condemnation; therefore let us cry unto the Savior: Give rest unto those whom Thou hast chosen from temporal life, according to Thy great mercy.[^2251]

*Sticheron:*  
All ye that remain in life, come with trembling unto the tombs; bend down and behold the deceit of the world: where now is the beauty of the body, and the glory of riches? Where is the pride of life? In truth, all things are vanity! Therefore let us cry unto the Savior: Give rest unto those whom Thou hast chosen from temporal life, according to Thy great mercy.[^2252]

*Sticheron:*  
Now in the tomb is he that was upon the throne; now he that was in purple is clothed in corruption: for no longer is he upon the throne, but he lies in the grave; behold, the imperial dominion hath vanished; behold, human life passes away like a dream; therefore let us cry unto the Savior: Give rest unto those whom Thou hast chosen from temporal life, according to Thy great mercy.[^2253]

*Sticheron:*  
Unto all who have passed over in hope of eternal life, and who in every manner, O Savior, have changed their life: every rank, race, and every age, men and women together, and newborn infants: as Thou art the Lover of mankind, appoint them in the bosom of Abraham and in places of refreshment, according to Thy great mercy.[^2254]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 2:*  
As a flower withers, and as a shadow passes away, so every man is dissolved; but when the trumpet sounds again, all the dead shall rise as in an earthquake to meet Thee, O Christ God: then, O Master, appoint in the tents of Thy saints those whom Thou hast translated from us, overlooking their transgressions, O Compassionate One.[^2255]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 6. Theotokion:*  
Thou art our God, Who in wisdom hast made all things, Who also didst send us the Prophets to prophesy Thy Coming, and the Apostles to proclaim Thy marvelous deeds: and some indeed prophesied Thy Coming, while others enlightened the nations through Baptism, and the Martyrs, having suffered, received glory from them; and they earnestly pray unto Thee, the Master, with the Mother who bare Thee: Give rest, O God, unto the souls whom Thou hast received, and account us worthy of Thy Kingdom, O Thou Who didst endure the Cross for the sake of me, the condemned, my Redeemer and God.[^2256]

*At the Aposticha, stichera.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*  
O Most Merciful One, Who hast incomprehensible tender compassion toward us, and an inexhaustible fountain of divine goodness: settle those who have passed over unto Thee, O Master, in the land of the living; grant them an inheritance abiding for ever in beloved and longed-for dwellings: for Thou, O Christ, hast shed Thy Blood for all, and with a life-bearing price hast redeemed the world.[^2257]

*Verse:* Their souls shall dwell among good things.[^2258]  
*Sticheron:*  
Thou didst willingly endure life-creating putting-to-death, and didst pour forth life, and by divine tender compassion, O Master, didst bestow everlasting sweetness upon the faithful, O only All-Compassionate, Sinless One: among them, we pray, rank the departed in the hope of resurrection, granting remission of sins, that Thy Name may be hymned by all, O Christ, and being saved, we may glorify Thy love for mankind.

*Verse:* Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and received, O Lord.[^2259]  
*Sticheron:*  
Knowing Thee, O Christ, to rule over the living with divine authority, and to exercise dominion over the dead, we pray: Give rest Thyself, O Lover of mankind, with Thine elect, in places of refreshment, in the splendors of the saints, unto Thy faithful servants who have departed unto Thee, the only Benefactor: for Thou art He that desires mercy, and as God Thou savest those whom Thou hast created according to Thine image, O only Most Merciful One.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Tone 6:*  
Grief came unto Adam through tasting of the tree of old in Eden, when the serpent spat forth poison: for because of this, all-racial death entered in, devouring man; but the Master, having come, cast down...

...the serpent and granted resurrection unto us: unto Him let us cry: Spare, O Savior, those whom Thou hast received, and with the saints give them rest, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.[^2260]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*  
Thou hast been shown forth as a divinely-fitting habitation, O Most Holy One, and hast contained God, and hast borne God, O Unwedded One, in two essences, yet in one Hypostasis, O Pure Maiden: entreat Him, the Only-Begotten and Firstborn, Who preserved thee spotless even after Childbearing, to give rest unto the souls of those who have fallen asleep in faith, in the light of unfading radiance and blessedness.[^2261]

*Troparion: O Thou Who with wisdom profound... Glory, both now: Thee we have as a wall and a haven... And the customary dismissal.*[^2262]

*AT THE LITURGY, the Typika.*[^2263]  
*Prokeimenon of the Apostle, Tone 6:*  
Their souls shall dwell among good things.  
*Epistle to the Acts, Pericope 51 (Acts 28:1-31).*[^2264]  
*And the Epistle for the Departed, Pericope 163 (1 Cor. 15:47-57):*  
Brethren: The first man was of the earth, earthy...  
*Alleluia, Tone 6:*  
*Verse:* Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and received, O Lord.[^2265]  
*Gospel from John, Pericope 67 (Jn. 21:15-25): And another for the Departed, Pericope 21 (Jn. 5:24-30).*  
*Communion Verse (Communion Hymn):*  
Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and received, O Lord. Alleluia, thrice.[^2266]

---

### Notes

[^1147]: Major Part division: Part VI of the 1907 Lviv Pentecostarion, encompassing the liturgical cycle of the Sixth Sunday after Pascha (Sunday of the Blind Man), the Apodosis of Pascha, the Great Feast of the Ascension of our Lord, and the Seventh Sunday after Pascha (Fathers of the First Ecumenical Council). Physical Leaf p434 (printed fol. 428v).
[^1148]: Description of the engraved woodcut on physical leaf p434 (printed fol. 428v): Christ applying clay to the eyes of the man blind from birth, with the disciples witnessing the miracle and the restored man rejoicing.
[^1149]: Small Vespers of the Sixth Sunday after Pascha (Sunday of the Blind Man), rubrics for "Lord, I have cried": setting 4 verses, with 4 Resurrection stichera of Tone 5 indicated by their incipits. Physical Leaf p434 (printed fol. 428v).
[^1150]: Small Vespers rubric for the Doxastikon (*Сла́ва*), Tone 5: "O Lord, passing by on the way..." (*Гдⷭ҇и мимоходѧ̀ пꙋте́мъ*), directing the reader to Great Vespers where the full text is provided. Physical Leaf p434 (printed fol. 428v).
[^1151]: Small Vespers Dogmatikon Theotokion, Tone 5 (*Бг҃олѣ́пнꙋю и҆ пречт҃ꙋю Ѻ҆трокови́цꙋ почти́мъ*), dogmatic hymn extolling the incarnation without change and citing the prophecy of Ezekiel 44:1–3 concerning the shut gate of virginity. Physical Leaf p434 (printed fol. 428v).
[^1152]: Small Vespers, Resurrection Aposticha sticheron, Tone 5: magnifying Christ the incarnate Savior Who did not depart from heaven, accepting the Cross and death to destroy the gates of hades. Physical Leaf p435 (printed fol. 429r).
[^1153]: Psalm verse accompanying the first Theotokion Prosomoion: Psalm 44:18 [LXX 44:18 / MT 45:17]: "I will remember thy name in every generation and generation." Physical Leaf p435 (printed fol. 429r).
[^1154]: Small Vespers Aposticha, first Theotokion Prosomoion, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡмъ): celebrating the Virgin Mother as the Seal of the Prophets and proclamation of the Apostles. Physical Leaf p435 (printed fol. 429r).
[^1155]: Psalm verse accompanying the second Theotokion Prosomoion: Psalm 44:11 [LXX 44:11 / MT 45:10]: "Hear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear." Physical Leaf p435 (printed fol. 429r).
[^1156]: Small Vespers Aposticha, second Theotokion Prosomoion, Tone 5: an accumulation of Old Testament typological titles for the Virgin: spiritual Heaven, Jar of Manna, Candlestick, overshadowing Mountain, and Rod of Jesse. Physical Leaf p435 (printed fol. 429r).
[^1157]: Psalm verse accompanying the third Theotokion Prosomoion: Psalm 44:13 [LXX 44:13 / MT 45:12]: "The rich among the people shall entreat thy face." Physical Leaf p435 (printed fol. 429r).
[^1158]: Small Vespers Aposticha, third Theotokion Prosomoion, Tone 5: dogmatic hymn confessing Christ's two natures and His human rational and volitional soul assumed from the Virgin's blood. Physical Leaf p435 (printed fol. 429r).
[^1159]: Small Vespers Aposticha, Doxastikon, Tone 8 (*Сла́ва*): addressing Christ as the spiritual Sun of Righteousness Who illuminated the eyes of the man born blind and asking illumination for the chanters' souls. Physical Leaf p435 (printed fol. 429r).
[^1160]: Small Vespers Aposticha, Theotokion, Tone 5 (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): addressing the Virgin as Temple, Door, Palace, and Throne of the King, through whom Christ shone upon those sleeping in darkness. Physical Leaf p435 (printed fol. 429r).
[^1161]: Great Vespers of the Sunday of the Blind Man, rubrics at "Lord, I have cried": setting 10 verses, chanting 3 Resurrection stichera of Tone 5, 4 Eastern stichera of Tone 5, and 2 idiomela of the Blind Man in Tone 2 (repeating the first). Physical Leaf p436 (printed fol. 430v).
[^1162]: Great Vespers, first Resurrection sticheron at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 5: Christ shaming the devil by His Cross, blunting the sting of sin by His Resurrection, and delivering humanity from death. Physical Leaf p436 (printed fol. 430v).
[^1163]: Great Vespers, second Resurrection sticheron, Tone 5: Christ led as a lamb to the slaughter, entering hades and bidding the captives to come forth. Physical Leaf p436 (printed fol. 430v).
[^1164]: Great Vespers, third Resurrection sticheron, Tone 5: the invisible Creator suffering in the flesh and rising immortal, instructing the nations to worship the one God in three Hypostases. Physical Leaf p436 (printed fol. 430v).
[^1165]: Great Vespers, first Eastern (Anatolian) sticheron, Tone 5: offering evening worship unto the Unwaning Light Who shone forth in the flesh at the end of the ages. Physical Leaf p436 (printed fol. 430v).
[^1166]: Great Vespers, second Eastern (Anatolian) sticheron, Tone 5: praising Christ the Author of salvation, at Whose Rising the angelic choir rejoices and demons flee. Physical Leaf p436 (printed fol. 430v).
[^1167]: Great Vespers, third Eastern (Anatolian) sticheron, Tone 5: the bribed Roman guard (*кꙋстѡді́ѧ*) spreading the false rumor that the disciples stole Christ's body while they slept (Mt 28:11–15). Physical Leaf p436 (printed fol. 430v).
[^1168]: Great Vespers, fourth Eastern (Anatolian) sticheron, Tone 5: Christ leading hades captive, trampling down death, and enlightening the world by His precious Cross. Physical Leaf p436 (printed fol. 430v).
[^1169]: Great Vespers, first idiomelon of the Blind Man, Tone 2 (chanted twice): the internal dialogue and supplication of the man born blind, reflecting upon his blindness and imploring Christ for mercy (Jn 9:1–3). Physical Leaf p436 (printed fol. 430v).
[^1170]: Great Vespers, second idiomelon of the Blind Man, Tone 2: Christ encountering the blind man upon departing from the temple, making clay, sending him to Siloam, and the blind man confessing Him as the Messiah foretold by Moses. Physical Leaf p437 (printed fol. 431r).

[^1428]: Three-Ode Canon, Ode 8, Theotokion [Acrostic letter Ι / I] (preceded by *Both now*): petitioning the Virgin Mother to intercede before the incarnate Word for mercy upon the humble. Physical Leaf p458 (printed p. 452 / fol. 452r).
