[^1430]: Three-Ode Canon, Ode 9, first troparion [Acrostic letter Ω / O]: Christ led as a lamb to slaughter and binding demonic malice through His voluntary wounds. Physical Leaf p459 (printed p. 453 / fol. 453r).
[^1431]: Three-Ode Canon, Ode 9, second troparion [Acrostic letter Σ / S]: the shattering of the bars of hades and the resurrection of those held in ancient decay. Physical Leaf p459 (printed p. 453 / fol. 453r).
[^1432]: Three-Ode Canon, Ode 9, third troparion [Acrostic letter Η / E] (preceded by *Glory*): Christ's holy Rising as life and His Ascension as eternal gladness forefeasted in faith. Physical Leaf p459 (printed p. 453 / fol. 453r).
[^1433]: Three-Ode Canon, Ode 9, Theotokion [Acrostic letter Φ / Ph] (preceded by *Both now*): concluding the acrostic *ᾨδὴ αὕτη Ἰωσήφ*, praising the Virgin for giving birth to Light from Light and dispelling godlessness. Physical Leaf p459 (printed p. 453 / fol. 453r).
[^1434]: Major liturgical headpiece and title for the Apodosis of the Feast of Pascha, celebrated at Vespers on Tuesday evening of the Sixth Week after Pascha. Physical Leaf p459 (printed p. 453 / fol. 453r).
[^1435]: Title rubric indicating the Apodosis (*Ѿда́нїе*) of the Feast of Pascha. Physical Leaf p459 (printed p. 453 / fol. 453r).
[^1436]: Rubric for the opening of Apodosis Vespers: the priest beginning with censer and lighted candle before the open Royal Doors, chanting the Paschal troparion with verses, followed by the Prooemiac Psalm (Ps. 103), Litany, and customary Kathisma. Physical Leaf p459 (printed p. 453 / fol. 453r).
[^1437]: Rubric for the 6 automelic stichera of the Blind Man at *Lord, I have cried*. Physical Leaf p459 (printed p. 453 / fol. 453r).
[^1438]: Automelic Sticheron of the Blind Man at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2: the monologue of the blind man reflecting on whether his affliction was due to parental sin or for the censure of the Gentiles, lamenting perpetual darkness and praying to Christ for mercy. Physical Leaf p459 (printed p. 453 / fol. 453r).
[^1439]: Second Automelic Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2: Jesus departing the temple, making clay, sending the blind man to wash in Siloam, and the healed man boldly proclaiming Jesus as the Messiah foretold by Moses. Physical Leaf p459 (printed p. 453 / fol. 453r).
[^1440]: Third Automelic Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 4: the blind man considering his life as night and praying to the Son of David for sight to hymn His power. Physical Leaf p460 (printed p. 454 / fol. 454r).
[^1441]: Fourth Automelic Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 5: Christ answering the disciples' question regarding sin, declaring the manifestation of God's works, and healing the blind man at Siloam. Physical Leaf p460 (printed p. 454 / fol. 454r).
[^1442]: Fifth Automelic Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 8: Christ the Noetic Sun illuminating the blind man with His touch and making the faithful sons of the day. Physical Leaf p460 (printed p. 454 / fol. 454r).
[^1443]: Sixth Automelic Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 8: petition to the incarnate Word, Who touched the blind man's eyes with creative fingers, to enlighten our spiritual senses as the grudgeless Giver. Physical Leaf p460 (printed p. 454 / fol. 454r).
[^1444]: Doxastikon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 8 (preceded by *Glory... Both now...*): extolling Christ's two-fold works of mercy in granting both physical and spiritual sight to the man blind from birth. Physical Leaf p460 (printed p. 454 / fol. 454r).
[^1445]: Rubric for the Prokeimenon of the day (*Прокі́менъ днѐ*). Physical Leaf p460 (printed p. 454 / fol. 454r).
[^1446]: Aposticha Sticheron of the Resurrection, Tone 5: Christ incarnate and undivided from heaven accepting the Cross and death to despoil hades and raise man on the third day. Physical Leaf p460 (printed p. 454 / fol. 454r).
[^1447]: Concluding Paschal rubrics for Apodosis Vespers: chanting the Paschal verses (*Let God arise...*), the Paschal Stichera (*A sacred Pascha hath been shown forth...*), the Doxastikon of Pascha (*It is the day of Resurrection...*), and *Christ is risen from the dead...* once with melody. Physical Leaf p460 (printed p. 454 / fol. 454r).
[^1448]: Custos / catchword (*Та́же,*) linking the conclusion of Paschal Apodosis Vespers to the service continuing on physical leaf p461. Physical Leaf p460 (printed p. 454 / fol. 454r).
[^1449]: Concluding rubrics for Tuesday Vespers of the Blind Man: reciting the Prayer of St. Simeon (*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart*), the Trisagion prayers through the Lord's Prayer, the Resurrection Troparion in Tone 5 (*The Word co-unoriginate*), its Theotokion, the Augmented Litany, and the customary dismissal without the Cross (*He Who rose from the dead...*) commemorating the saint of the day. Physical Leaf p461 (printed p. 455 / fol. 455r).
[^1450]: Rubric for Small Compline: following *It is truly right* and the Trisagion prayers, chanting the Kontakion of the Blind Man (*With spiritual eyes...*), and for *Glory... Both now...*, the Kontakion of Pascha (*Though Thou didst descend into the grave...*). Physical Leaf p461 (printed p. 455 / fol. 455r).
[^1451]: Rubric for the Midnight Office on the Apodosis of Pascha: chanted according to custom with the censer; substituting the Resurrection Troparion in Tone 5 (*The Word co-unoriginate*) after the first Trisagion in place of *Behold, the Bridegroom comes*, and the Paschal Kontakion after the second Trisagion in place of *Remember, O Lord, for Thou art good*; concluding with 40 repetitions of *Lord, have mercy* and the dismissal, while omitting the prayer for the departed (*Remember, O Lord, in hope...*). Physical Leaf p461 (printed p. 455 / fol. 455r).
[^1452]: Title and Matins opening rubric for Wednesday of the Sixth Week after Pascha (the Apodosis or Apodosis of Pascha): the priest begins with the censer before the open Royal Doors, proclaiming *Glory to the holy, consubstantial...*, chanting *Christ is risen from the dead...* with the Paschal verses as on the eve; followed by the Superior or Reader reciting the Lesser Doxology (*Glory to God in the highest...*) and the customary Six Psalms. Physical Leaf p461 (printed p. 455 / fol. 455r).
[^1453]: Rubric for *God is the Lord* (Ps. 117:27 LXX) and the Resurrection Troparion in Tone 5 (*The Word co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit...*), chanted twice, followed by *Glory... Both now...* and its Theotokion. Physical Leaf p461 (printed p. 455 / fol. 455r).
[^1454]: Rubric for the Sessional Hymns after the first Stichology (Kathisma psalmody), directing the reader to the hymns written for the Sunday of the Blind Man (see fol. 442) and prescribing a Patristic reading from St. John Chrysostom. Physical Leaf p461 (printed p. 455 / fol. 455r).
[^1455]: Sessional Hymn after the second Stichology, Tone 5 (repeated for *Glory... Both now...*): Christ, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Holy Spirit and robed in uncreated light, lovingly clothing Himself in human nature, banishing infirmities, and granting sight to the man blind from his mother's womb. Physical Leaf p461 (printed p. 455 / fol. 455r).
[^1456]: Matins rubrics following the Sessional Hymns: reciting *Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ* and Psalm 50 (Ps. 51), followed by the appointment of three Canons: the Paschal Canon without Theotokia with its heirmos to 6, the Canon of the Blind Man to 4, and the Canon of the Forefeast of the Ascension to 4. Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1457]: Heading and acrostic rubric for the Canon of the Forefeast of the Ascension, composed by Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing an alphabetical acrostic in Greek: *Канѡ́нъ предпра́зднствꙋ Вознесе́нїѧ, є҆гѡ́же краестро́чїе по а҆лфави́тꙋ*. Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1458]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 1: Heirmos (*Конѧ̀ и҆ вса́дника* / *The steed and the rider*), celebrating the overthrow of Pharaoh in the Red Sea (Ex. 15:1). Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1459]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 1, first troparion [Acrostic letter A] (chanted twice): Christ ascending unto the Father and offering the human flesh He assumed from us, evoking a hymn of victory from the faithful. Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1460]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 1, second troparion [Acrostic letter B] (preceded by *Glory*): the fulfillment of the divine Scriptures and prophetic announcements in the Master ascending into the heavens following His Resurrection. Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1461]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 1, third troparion [Acrostic letter G] (preceded by *Both now*): heaven and earth rejoicing mystically at Christ's Ascension, which reunited what was separated of old and broke down the barrier of enmity (Eph. 2:14). Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1462]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 1: directing the choir to sing the Heirmos of the Ascension (*Спаси́телю Бг҃ꙋ* / *Unto God the Savior*). Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1463]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 3: Heirmos (*Водрꙋзи́вый на ничесо́мже зе́млю* / *O Thou Who didst establish the earth upon nothing*), praising the Creator Who suspended the earth upon nothing (Job 26:7) and established the Church upon the rock of His commandments. Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1464]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 3, first troparion [Acrostic letter D] (chanted twice): the Word binding death by His own death, rising on the third day, and ascending unto the heavenly heights while the incorporeal hosts praise His unutterable dispensation. Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1465]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 3, second troparion [Acrostic letter E] (preceded by *Glory*): Christ assuming flesh to suffer the Cross, rising from the dead, and ascending unto the source of uncreated light, the Father, bringing peace to all creation. Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1466]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 3, third troparion [Acrostic letter Z] (preceded by *Both now*): Christ the divine Word seeking the lost sheep upon earth (Lk. 15:4–6), uniting it unto the unwandering flock, and seating it in glory at the Father's right hand. Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1467]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 3: directing the choir to sing the Heirmos of the Ascension (*Си́лою Крⷭ҇та̀ твоегѡ̀ Хрⷭ҇тѐ* / *By the power of Thy Cross, O Christ*). Physical Leaf p462 (printed p. 456 / fol. 456r).
[^1468]: Rubric for the Kontakion and Ikos of the Blind Man after Ode 3 (*With spiritual eyes...*), referencing printed fol. 447 (Physical Leaf p453). Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1469]: Sessional Hymn of the Blind Man, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (*Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се*): Christ granting sight to the man born without eyes to manifest His divine glory as the Light of the world, while envious opponents plotted to slay the Giver of life. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1470]: Sessional Hymn of the Forefeast of the Ascension, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (preceded by *Glory... Both now...*): Christ's voluntary nativity, manifestation, Passion, Resurrection, and glorious Ascension elevating and glorifying human nature. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1471]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 4: Heirmos (*Бж҃твенное твоѐ разꙋмѣ́въ* / *Perceiving Thy divine condescension*), Habakkuk contemplating the divine exhaustion and crying aloud: *Glory to Thy power, O Lord!* (Hab. 3:2). Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1472]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 4, first troparion [Acrostic letter H]: human nature, formerly condemned by sin, receiving divine compassion through Christ's assumption of flesh and hymning His Resurrection and Ascension with fear. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1473]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 4, second troparion [Acrostic letter Th]: the incorporeal ranks marveling as they behold Christ bearing earthly flesh, ascending upon a cloud, and mounting unto the heavenly heights. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1474]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 4, third troparion [Acrostic letter I] (preceded by *Glory*): the angelic powers awestruck at the sight of Christ's crimson garments as He ascends, bowing down in mingled fear and joy. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1475]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 4, fourth troparion [Acrostic letter K] (preceded by *Both now*): summoning creation to clap hands as the risen Lord ascends into heaven and the Angels submit to Him as Creator and God. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1476]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 4: Heirmos of the Ascension (*Оу҆слы́шахъ слꙋ́хъ си́лы Крⷭ҇та̀* / *I heard the report of the power of the Cross*). Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1477]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 5: Heirmos (*Ѡ҆дѣѧ́йсѧ свѣ́томъ* / *O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light*), the prophet rising early to seek the Lord Who is clothed with light as with a garment (Ps. 103:2 LXX). Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1478]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 5, first troparion [Acrostic letter L] (chanted twice): Christ abolishing the ancient condemnation of humanity, rising from the dead, and ascending to bestow the honor of session beside the Father upon those He loved. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1479]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 5, second troparion [Acrostic letter M] (preceded by *Glory*): Christ gladdening His Disciples after the Resurrection and ascending unto the Father from Whom He was never separated, even while conversing with mortals on earth. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1480]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 5, third troparion [Acrostic letter N] (preceded by *Both now*): Christ fulfilling the predictions of the Law and the prophetic proclamations, ascending upon a receiving cloud unto the heavens. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).
[^1481]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 5: Heirmos of the Ascension (*Оу҆́тренююще вопіе́мъ тѝ* / *Rising early we cry unto Thee*). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1482]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 6: Heirmos (*Неи́стовствꙋющеесѧ бу́рею* / *Raging with a tempest*), the soul tossed by the tempest of passions praying to be brought up from corruption like Jonah from the sea monster (Jonah 2:3–7). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1483]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 6, first troparion [Acrostic letter X] (chanted twice): Christ appearing as a strange sight unto the Angels, bearing the form of a man in His awesome ascent. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1484]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 6, second troparion [Acrostic letter O] (preceded by *Glory*): the Lord of all ascending in radiant glory unto His unoriginate Father while all creation keeps festival. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1485]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 6, third troparion [Acrostic letter P] (preceded by *Both now*): all nations called to cry aloud as Christ ascends with a shout and the sound of a divine trumpet unto the heavens, which He never abandoned. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1486]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 6: Heirmos of the Ascension (*Ѡ҆бы́де мѧ̀ бе́здна* / *The abyss hath compassed me*). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1487]: Rubric for the Kontakion and Ikos of Pascha after Ode 6 (*Though Thou didst descend into the grave...*), referencing printed fol. 16 (Physical Leaf p22), followed by the reading of the Synaxarion from the Menaion. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1488]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 7: Heirmos (*Превозноси́мый Ѻ҆тце́въ* / *The supremely exalted Lord of the fathers*), praising the God of the fathers Who quenched the fiery flame for the Three Holy Youths in Babylon (Dan. 3:26). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1489]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 7, first troparion [Acrostic letter R]: Christ tearing asunder the middle wall of enmity by His Cross and Passion (Eph. 2:14), borne upon a cloud into the presence of His unoriginate Father. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1490]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 7, second troparion [Acrostic letter S]: the heavenly powers crying in trembling for the eternal gates to be lifted up (Ps. 23:7 LXX) as Christ arrives in earthly flesh having vanquished Beliar. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1491]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 7, third troparion [Acrostic letter T] (preceded by *Glory*): the divine hosts questioning why the Savior's robes are dyed crimson, and Christ answering that He has trodden the winepress alone (Isa. 63:1–3). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1492]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 7, fourth troparion [Acrostic letter Y] (preceded by *Both now*): the faithful harmoniously praising Christ's Crucifixion and Rising, and radiantly celebrating His venerable Ascension unto all ages. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1493]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 7: Heirmos of the Ascension (*Въ пещѝ ѻ҆́гненнѣй* / *In the fiery furnace*). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1494]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 8: Heirmos (*Тебѣ̀ всесодѣ́телю* / *Unto Thee, the Fashioner of all*), calling the children in the furnace to sing and exalt the Creator of all unto the ages. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1495]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 8, first troparion [Acrostic letter Ph]: a radiant cloud receiving Christ, the Light of Light, from the Mount of Olives before the gaze of His divine Disciples. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).
[^1496]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 8, second troparion [Acrostic letter Ch]: clapping hands in psalmody as Christ our God rises and ascends to the place whence He descended in divine love for mankind, establishing peace. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1497]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 8, third troparion [Acrostic letter Ps] (preceded by *Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord*): King David proclaiming Christ ascending upon the Cherubim as upon wings of spiritual hosts (Ps. 17:11 LXX). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1498]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 8, fourth troparion [Acrostic letter O] (preceded by *Both now*): Christ appearing to the Disciples after His Resurrection, leading them to the Mount of Olives, and being lifted up on a radiant cloud into the heights. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1499]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 8: Heirmos of the Ascension (*И҆з̾ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ пре́жде вѣ́къ* / *Begotten of the Father before the ages*). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1500]: Rubric omitting the Magnificat (*More honorable than the Cherubim...*) and prescribing the chanting of the Paschal refrains for Ode 9. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1501]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 9: Heirmos (*И҆са́їе ликꙋ́й* / *Rejoice, O Isaiah*), the prophetic vision of the Virgin conceiving and bearing Emmanuel, God and Man (Isa. 7:14). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1502]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 9, first troparion: all creation celebrating Christ's Ascension, wherein He presented the human nature He assumed unto the Father in an unchangeable mystery. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1503]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 9, second troparion: Christ breaking the bars of hades, rising from the dead, and ascending to the heavenly ranks who command the eternal gates to open. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1504]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 9, third troparion: the Angels appearing to the disciples on the Mount of Olives, testifying that Christ Who ascended shall return in glory to judge the living and the dead (Acts 1:11). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1505]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 9, fourth troparion (preceded by *Glory*): prayer for Christ to bestow His divine peace upon the faithful, preserving all in mutual love even as He blessed His disciples of old. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1506]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 9, Theotokion (preceded by *Both now*): praising the immaculate Virgin as the light-bearing chariot of the noetic Sun, bringing the light of divine knowledge to those sitting in darkness. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1507]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 9: Heirmos of the Ascension (*Тѧ̀ па́че ᲂу҆ма̀* / *Thee who art beyond understanding*). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1508]: Rubric for the Exapostilaria: the Paschal Exapostilarion (*Having fallen asleep in the flesh...*) chanted twice, followed by the Exapostilarion of the Blind Man (*My spiritual eyes...*), referencing printed fol. 440 (Physical Leaf p446). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1509]: Rubric for the Praises (*Ainoi*, Pss. 148–150 LXX), appointing 8 verses and 4 automelic stichera of the Blind Man. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1510]: First Automelic Sticheron of the Blind Man at the Praises, Tone 2: the monologue of the man born blind, questioning whether parental sin caused his blindness and imploring Christ for mercy. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1511]: Second Automelic Sticheron of the Blind Man at the Praises, Tone 2: Jesus departing the temple, making clay with spittle to anoint the blind man's eyes, and sending him to wash in Siloam, leading to his bold confession of Christ the Messiah before his neighbors (Jn. 9:1–11). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).
[^1512]: Third and Fourth Automelic Stichera of the Blind Man at the Praises (Tone 4: *Considering his whole life as night...*; Tone 5: *O Lord, as Thou wast passing by along the way...*), referenced from Tuesday Vespers. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).
[^1513]: Rubric for the Stichera of Pascha with their psalm refrains (*Let God arise...*), referencing printed fol. 305 (Physical Leaf p311), followed by the Doxastikon in Tone 5 (*It is the day of Resurrection...*) and *Christ is risen from the dead...* sung once with melody. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).
[^1514]: Rubric for the Great Doxology, the Resurrection Troparion in Tone 5 (*The Word co-unoriginate*), its Theotokion, the litany, and the dismissal without the Cross commemorating the saint of the day. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).
[^1515]: Rubric for the First Hour celebrated in the narthex, reciting the customary psalms, Troparion, and Theotokion (*What shall we call thee, O full of grace...*). Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).
[^1516]: Rubric concluding the First Hour with the Kontakion of the Blind Man (*With spiritual eyes...*) and the final dismissal, and prescribing the Third, Sixth, and Ninth Hours before Liturgy to be chanted with their customary three psalms, alternating the Resurrection and Paschal Kontakia. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).
[^1517]: Rubric for the solemn opening of the Divine Liturgy celebrated exactly as on Easter Sunday itself, with the priest chanting the Paschal verses before the altar with the censer and paschal candle. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).
[^1518]: Rubric for the Divine Liturgy on the Apodosis of Pascha: appointing the Typika (Pss. 102 and 145 LXX with the Beatitudes), Odes 3 and 6 of the Canon, the Resurrection Troparion, Kontakia of the Blind Man and Pascha, the Paschal Prokeimenon (*This is the day which the Lord hath made*, Ps. 117:24 LXX), Alleluia, Epistle and Gospel of the day, and Communion Hymn (*Receive the Body of Christ*) with Psalm 33 (Ps. 34 LXX). Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).
[^1519]: Concluding Liturgy rubric for the solemn dismissal of Pascha with the blessing of the Holy Cross, sealing the forty-day liturgical celebration of the Feast of Pascha. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).
[^1520]: Trapeza rubric permitting a festive meal with oil, fish, and wine for the monastic brethren on the Apodosis of Pascha before the Great Feast of the Ascension. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).
[^1521]: Major feast day headpiece: Feast of the Ascension of our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ (*ВОЗНЕСЕ́НЇЕ Го́спода Бо́га и҆ Спа́са на́шегѡ І҆исоу́са Хрїста́*), celebrated on Thursday of the Sixth Week after Pascha (forty days after the Resurrection). Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).
[^1522]: Rubric for the Ninth Hour on Wednesday evening, prescribing the Trisagion to be chanted at the beginning of all liturgical offices until Pentecost Sunday, ending the Paschal season's replacement of introductory prayers. Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).
[^1523]: Rubric for Small Vespers on the Eve of the Ascension, appointing 4 stichera at *Lord, I have cried*. Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).
[^1524]: First Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (*Нб҃нымъ чинѡ́мъ*): Christ renewing creation through His incarnation and ascending in flesh to sit at the right hand of the Father, awaiting to return as Judge. Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).
[^1525]: Second Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1: angels addressing the Apostles on the Mount of Olives, declaring that Christ Who ascended shall return as Judge to render unto each according to their works (Acts 1:11). Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).
[^1526]: Third Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1: petition for the ascended Savior to illumine the hearts of the faithful with divine grace even as He bestowed heavenly glory upon the witnesses of His Ascension. Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).
[^1527]: Doxastikon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 6 (preceded by *Glory... Both now...*): exhortation for the assembly of the faithful to join the Apostles on the Mount of Olives and chant with David: *God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet* (Ps. 46:5 LXX). [^1528]: Rubric for the Stichera at the Aposticha of Small Vespers on the Eve of the Ascension. Physical Leaf p468 (printed p. 462 / fol. 462r).

[^1545]: Fifth Automelic Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 6 (chanted twice): Christ accomplishing the mystery of the dispensation on the Mount of Olives, traversing the heavenly firmament, and ascending unto the Father while promising to send the All-Holy Spirit. [^1549]: Second Old Testament Reading: Prophecy of Isaiah (Isa. 62:10–12; 63:1–3, 7–9 LXX), preparing the way for the Lord, the proclamation of the Savior coming to Sion, the warrior returning from Edom with crimson garments from Bosor having trodden the winepress alone, and the Lord saving His people through His own love and mercy. [^1550]: Third Old Testament Reading: Prophecy of Zechariah (Zech. 14:4, 8–11 LXX), the Lord standing upon the Mount of Olives east of Jerusalem, living waters flowing forth to the eastern and western seas in summer and spring, the Lord reigning as King over all the earth, and Jerusalem dwelling safely without anathema. [^1553]: Second Automelic Sticheron at the Litiya, Tone 1: Christ ascending unto His unoriginate Father without departing from His uncircumscribed bosom, receiving the ceaseless thrice-holy praise of the heavenly hosts, and remaining the only-begotten Son even in His incarnate state. [^1554]: Third Automelic Sticheron at the Litiya, Tone 1: the Angels declaring unto the Apostles on the Mount of Olives that Christ Who ascended into heaven shall return in like manner, summoning all to serve Him in holiness and righteousness (Acts 1:11; Lk. 1:75). [^1555]: Fourth Automelic Sticheron at the Litiya, Tone 4: Christ ascending from the Mount of Olives to accomplish the Father's good pleasure, causing awe among the Angels and terror in the abyss, while the disciples tremble with joy before the cloud prepared as an awaiting throne, heaven revealing its beauty, and the earth revealing its hidden treasures. [^1556]: Fifth Automelic Sticheron at the Litiya, Tone 4: human nature renewed in Christ and exalted above all principalities and powers, seated with the Father through His co-suffering and impassible Passion, while the bodiless hosts marvel at the comely God-Man and Angels in white robes announce His second coming as Judge of living and dead (Acts 1:10–11). [^1557]: Sixth Automelic Sticheron at the Litiya, Tone 4: the clouds bearing Christ upward in His flesh before the eyes of the Disciples, the heavenly gates lifting up (Ps. 23:7 LXX), the angelic choir rejoicing, and the Disciples imploring the Good Shepherd to send the Most Holy Spirit to guide, confirm, enlighten, and sanctify their souls. [^1560]: First Automelic Sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 2: Christ's voluntary Nativity, manifestation, Passion in the flesh, Resurrection, and Ascension into glory, filling all creation with His Godhead and sending the divine Holy Spirit. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1870]: Doxastikon of the Holy Fathers at the Aposticha, Tone 4 (preceded by *Glory*): the assembly of Orthodox celebrating the memory of the 318 Fathers at Nicaea who formulated the Symbol of Faith. Physical Leaf p499 (printed p. 493 / fol. 493r).

[^1877]: Troparion of the Feast of the Ascension at *God is the Lord*, Tone 4 (*Both now*): *Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God...* Physical Leaf p500 (printed p. 494 / fol. 494r).

[^1883]: Theotokion after the second stichology, Tone 6 (*Glory... Both now...*): Supplication to the Virgin Mother to intercede with her crucified and risen Son for the salvation of our souls. Physical Leaf p501 (printed p. 495).

[^1888]: Second Antiphon of the Hymns of Ascent, Tone 6, first stanza (cf. Ps. 123:1 LXX [Ps. 124:1]): Exaltation of those who conquer through the assistance of the Lord. Physical Leaf p501 (printed p. 495).

[^1890]: Doxological stanza (*Glory... Both now...*) of the Second Antiphon of the Hymns of Ascent, Tone 6: Deification, good pleasure, understanding, peace, and blessing granted unto all through the Holy Spirit, Who is equal in operation with the Father and the Word. [^1960]: Second troparion of Ode 6 of the Canon of the Holy Fathers, Tone 6 (chanted twice): The synodical proclamation of the Son as the only-begotten Radiance (*apaugasma*) shining from the Father's essence before all ages. Physical Leaf p507 (printed p. 501).
[^1961]: Troparion of the Fathers (*Glory*), Ode 6, Tone 6: The heretical belly that belched forth poison torn open by the spiritual plow of the orthodox hierarchs' prayers. Physical Leaf p507 (printed p. 501).
[^1962]: Theotokion of Ode 6 of the Canon of the Fathers, Tone 6 (*Both now*): Moses prefiguring the Virgin as the Ark of the Covenant, the Table of showbread, the Lampstand, and the golden Jar of manna (Heb. 9:4). Physical Leaf p507 (printed p. 501).
[^1963]: Katavasia of Ode 6: Incipit of the fifth heirmos of the Iambic Canon of Pentecost (*A cleansing and salvation unto us, O Christ Master...*). Physical Leaf p507 (printed p. 501).
[^1964]: Kontakion of the Holy Fathers, Tone 8, Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки): The apostolic preaching and conciliar dogmas sealing the one robe of truth woven from heavenly theology. Physical Leaf p507 (printed p. 501).
[^1965]: Ikos of the Holy Fathers: The Church crying aloud with the voice of wisdom (Jn. 7:37; Prov. 9:1–5), offering the chalice of orthodox confession and citing divine immutability (Mal. 3:6; Is. 44:6). Physical Leaf p507 (printed p. 501).
[^1966]: Title of the liturgical Synaxarion commemorating the 318 God-bearing Fathers of the First Ecumenical Council held at Nicaea in AD 325. Physical Leaf p508 (printed p. 502).
[^1967]: First poetic verse prefixed to the Synaxarion of the Fathers: Pleading with the conciliar assembly to illumine the mind with rays of divine wisdom. Physical Leaf p508 (printed p. 502).
[^1968]: Second poetic verse of the Synaxarion: Declaring that Arius, having alienated the Son from the Father's essence, became an alien to the glory of God. Physical Leaf p508 (printed p. 502).
[^1969]: Prose narrative of the Synaxarion for the Seventh Sunday after Pascha: Chronicling the historical background of the Council of Nicaea (AD 325), the rise of Arianism in Alexandria, the excommunication by St. Peter of Alexandria following his vision of Christ in a torn robe, the restoration by Achillas, the expulsion by St. Alexander, the theological arguments recorded by Theodoret, the convocation by St. Constantine the Great, the arrival of 318 delegates (including Sts. Sylvester, Metrophanes through locum tenentes, Alexander with Archdeacon Athanasius, Eustathius, Macarius, Paphnutius, Nicholas of Myra, and Spyridon of Trimythous), the drafting and signing of the Nicene Creed up to the Holy Spirit, the fixing of the Paschal computus, the promulgation of twenty canons, the miracle of the deceased bishops signing the decree, the dedication of Constantinople to the Mother of God, and the horrific death of Arius in a public latrine mirroring that of Judas. Physical Leaf p508 (printed p. 502).
[^1970]: Concluding prayer of the Synaxarion invoking the intercessions of the 318 Holy Fathers of Nicaea. Physical Leaf p508 (printed p. 502).

[^2242]: Troparion 1 of Ode 9 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Petition for all who from the beginning of time have fallen asleep in faith and hope to dwell in the joy of the saints. Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).
[^2243]: Troparion 2 of Ode 9 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Prayer for those who perished under sudden manifestations of natural wrath—lightning bolts, thunder from heaven, earthquakes, or shipwrecks at sea. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).
[^2244]: Troparion 3 of Ode 9 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Universal intercession spanning every human stage and condition: the aged, youth, infants, sucklings, men and women together, commending them to God's mercy. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).
[^2245]: Troparion 4 of Ode 9 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Commemoration of those who died from poisonous bites, ferocious beasts, horse trampling, or homicide through strangulation and hanging. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).
[^2246]: Troparion 5 of Ode 9 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Supplication for each departed believer by name from every generation to stand without condemnation before Christ at His glorious Second Coming. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).
[^2247]: Triadicon of Ode 9 (*Glory*), Tone 8: Dogmatic confession of the Holy Trinity as one Godhead possessing three distinct personal hypostatic properties (*tres personae, una essentia*). The marginal letter *(Th)* opens the concluding acrostic block. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).
[^2248]: Theotokion of Ode 9 (*Both now*), Tone 8: The transcendent paradox of the Incarnation, wherein the unwedded Virgin suckles the Nourisher of the universe and holds in her arms the Redeemer Who sustains all things. The marginal letter *(U/V)* concludes the acrostic signature of St. Theodore the Studite. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).
[^2249]: Exapostilarion for the Saturday of Souls, Tone 3 (Melodic model: *Having adorned the heavens with stars*): Exhortation to the faithful to commemorate all who have fallen asleep in Christ and keep mindful of the Great and Final Day of Judgment. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).
[^2250]: Theotokion of the Exapostilarion, Tone 3: Entreaty to the Mother of God as the sweetness of angels, joy of the afflicted, and protectress of all Christians, delivering souls from eternal torments. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).
[^2251]: First sticheron at the Praises (Lauds), Tone 6 (Melodic model: *Despairing on account of her life*): Solemn meditation on the dread hour of death and the terrifying tribunal of the Master, where unquenchable fire, outer darkness, and gnashing of teeth await the condemned. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).
[^2252]: Second sticheron at the Praises, Tone 6 (Melodic model: *Despairing on account of her life*): Moving call to all the living to visit the graves and observe the fleeting vanity of bodily beauty, worldly wealth, and haughty ambition. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).
[^2253]: Third sticheron at the Praises, Tone 6 (Melodic model: *Despairing on account of her life*): Contemplation of the transitory nature of earthly power, depicting the monarch once seated upon an imperial throne and clad in royal purple now lying corrupting in a lowly grave. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).
[^2254]: Fourth sticheron at the Praises, Tone 6 (Melodic model: *Despairing on account of her life*): Supplication to the Savior to gather all departed of every age, rank, and condition into the bosom of Abraham and places of heavenly refreshment. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).
[^2255]: Doxasticon at the Praises (*Glory*), Tone 2: Poetic reflection comparing mortal existence to a fading flower and passing shadow (cf. Ps. 102:15-16 LXX; Isa. 40:6-8), contrasting human frailty with the triumphant resurrection of all the dead at the archangelic trumpet. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).
[^2256]: Theotokion at the Praises (*Both now*), Tone 6: Comprehensive theological synthesis honoring the Prophets who foresaw Christ, the Apostles who enlightened the nations, the Martyrs who glorified God by their blood, and the Virgin Mother who intercedes for the departed. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).
[^2257]: First sticheron at the Aposticha of Matins, Tone 6 (Melodic model: *Having laid aside all earthly hope*): Supplication to the inexhaustible fountain of divine mercy to grant the departed an enduring inheritance in the land of the living, bought by the precious Blood of Christ. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).
[^2258]: Second sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 6, with psalm verse Psalm 24:13 LXX: Commemoration of Christ's voluntary, life-giving death and descent into Hades, beseeching remission of sins and resurrectional hope for all deceased faithful. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).
[^2259]: Third sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 6, with psalm verse Psalm 64:5 LXX: Confession of Christ's sovereign lordship over both the living and the dead (Rom. 14:9), beseeching Him as Creator to rest His servants in the splendors of the saints. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).
[^2260]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha (*Glory*), Tone 6 (spanning leaves p543-p544): Scriptural reflection on the ancestral fall of Adam in Eden through the poison of the serpent and the entry of universal mortality, overthrown by the incarnation and resurrection of the Savior. Physical Leaves p543-p544 (printed pp. 537-538).
[^2261]: Theotokion at the Aposticha (*Both now*), Tone 6: Pure dogmatic confession of the Theotokos containing and bearing God in two unconfused natures and one Hypostasis, entreating her to pray for the rest of the departed in unfading heavenly radiance. Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).
[^2262]: Dismissal troparion for the departed (*O Thou Who with wisdom profound*) and its Theotokion (*Thee we have as a wall and a haven*), Tone 8, followed by the customary liturgical dismissal concluding the Saturday of Souls Matins. Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).
[^2263]: Rubric for the Divine Liturgy of the Saturday of Souls prescribing the Typika (*Izobrazitelna*), marking the final liturgical commemoration of the dead before the great Pentecost festival. Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).
[^2264]: Epistle pericopes for the Saturday of Souls: Acts pericope 51 (Acts 28:1-31, the concluding chapter of Acts describing Paul's preaching in Rome) followed by the funeral pericope 163 (1 Cor. 15:47-57, the second Adam and the victory over death). Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).
[^2265]: Alleluiarion in Tone 6 with psalm verse Psalm 64:5 LXX (*Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen*), followed by Gospel pericopes: John 67 (Jn. 21:15-25, Christ's dialogue with Peter by the Sea of Tiberias) and funeral pericope 21 (Jn. 5:24-30, resurrection of the dead). Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).
[^2266]: Communion verse (Communion Hymn) for the departed from Psalm 64:5 LXX with threefold Alleluia, sealing the funeral rites of the Saturday of Souls before Pentecost. Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).
